Math Finder

Build it yourself, one step at a time
The map 0. Prerequisites M0. Project skeleton M1. Lean checker M2. Hammer M3. LLM backend M4a. Planner + critic M4b. Prover M4c. Search + CLI M4d. Sub-searches M5. LaTeX in M6. Explanations M7. Working together M8. Traces + data M9. Web server M10. The website M11. Better proofs M12. Benchmark M13. Training What's next

The map

Math Finder takes a theorem you write in LaTeX, turns it into a precise Lean statement, searches for a proof, checks every step with Lean (a program that cannot be fooled), and explains the result back to you. You can give hints at any point, so it works like a partner rather than a black box.

How the pieces connect

The proof loop in one paragraph

The planner (an LLM) suggests a proof plan: a few small lemmas plus how they combine. The critic asks Lean whether the plan even fits together, then checks each lemma: proved, false (Lean found a counterexample), or stuck. The prover attacks each lemma, first with cheap automatic tactics, then with LLM-written proofs, repairing them from Lean's error messages. Anything stuck goes back to the planner as specific feedback, or to you as a question. Once everything is proved, the optimizer looks for a shorter proof and the explainer writes it up in plain math.

Folder tree (finished project)

math_proofs/ ├── pyproject.toml Python project + dependencies M0 ├── PROGRESS.md build log: what's done, how it was verified ├── runs/ one folder per run: trace.jsonl, proof.lean, explanation.md M8 ├── cache/lemmas.json lemma → verified proof, reused across runs M4 ├── data/ training datasets built by `mathfinder export` M8 ├── guide.html this guide (built by tools/build_guide.py) ├── guide/ guide source: template, chapters, snapshots of older file versions ├── tools/build_guide.py pastes the real code into the guide ├── tools/watch.py print a running search's events in the terminal M9 ├── tools/make_demo.py turn a trace into web/demo_events.json (open /#demo) M10 ├── lean_env/ Lean 4 + Mathlib project Ch 0 ├── mathfinder/ the engine (Python package) │ ├── __init__.py paths + version M0 │ ├── lean.py run Lean, read its errors M1 │ ├── hammer.py cheap automatic tactics + counterexamples M2 │ ├── llm.py talk to Claude M3 │ ├── planner.py critic.py prover.py search.py M4 │ ├── cli.py __main__.py command line M4 │ ├── formalize.py LaTeX → Lean M5 │ ├── explain.py Lean → plain math M6 │ ├── collab.py your hints, questions back to you M7 │ ├── traces.py logs + training data export M8 │ ├── optimize.py find a better proof (3 metrics) M11 │ └── bench.py run the benchmark, make the results table M12 ├── server/ FastAPI + WebSocket M9 ├── web/ the website: index.html, style.css, app.js M10 ├── bench/ problems.jsonl (20 problems) + results/ M12 ├── training/ prepare.py (datasets) + finetune.py (LoRA) M13 └── tests/ pytest tests, one file per module M0

Build order

ChapterWhat you get at the end

M2. HammerEasy lemmas get proved with no AI at all; false ones get counterexamples

Chapter 0: Prerequisites

What and why. You need three things: Lean (the proof checker), Mathlib (Lean's huge library of already-proved math), and Python tooling to run the search engine. You also need a way to talk to Claude: either the claude command line tool or an API key.

  1. Install Homebrew (the Mac package manager) if brew --version doesn't print a version. Follow the one-line install on brew.sh, then open a new terminal.

    brew --version

  2. Install elan, the Lean version manager. It downloads the right Lean version for each project automatically.

    brew install elan-init

    This gives you three commands: elan (manages Lean versions), lean (the checker) and lake (Lean's build tool, like npm or cargo).

  3. Install uv, a fast Python project manager.

    brew install uv

    uv creates an isolated Python environment (.venv) per project and installs packages into it, so this project can't break your other Python code.

  4. Make sure you can talk to Claude. Option A: the Claude Code CLI (uses your Claude subscription, no key needed):

    claude --version

    Option B: an API key from platform.claude.com, set in your terminal only (never in a file you share):

    export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="sk-ant-..."

  5. Create the project folder and go into it.

    mkdir -p ~/Desktop/math_proofs
cd ~/Desktop/math_proofs

  6. Create a Lean project that uses Mathlib. The +leanprover-community/mathlib4:lean-toolchain part means "use the Lean version Mathlib currently needs".

    lake +leanprover-community/mathlib4:lean-toolchain new lean_env math

    This creates lean_env/ with lakefile.toml (declares the Mathlib dependency) and lean-toolchain (pins the Lean version, here v4.36.0-rc2).

  7. Download Mathlib's prebuilt files. Compiling Mathlib yourself takes hours; this downloads it (several GB) instead.

    cd lean_env
lake exe cache get
cd ..

  8. Create the empty folders the project will fill in.

    mkdir -p mathfinder tests server web guide/chapters tools

Checkpoint

Prove something real. Create a scratch file and check it:

cd lean_env
mkdir -p .mf_tmp
printf 'import Mathlib\n\nexample (a b : ℝ) : a ^ 2 + b ^ 2 ≥ 2 * a * b := by\n  nlinarith [sq_nonneg (a - b)]\n\n#eval 2 + 2\n' > .mf_tmp/hello.lean
lake env lean --json .mf_tmp/hello.lean
cd ..

You should see exactly one message, the output of #eval, and no errors:

{"caption":"","data":"4", ... "severity":"information"}

What happened: example ... := by nlinarith [sq_nonneg (a - b)] states a² + b² ≥ 2ab for real numbers and proves it. The hint sq_nonneg (a - b) says "(a−b)² ≥ 0", and nlinarith (nonlinear arithmetic) finishes it. No output about the theorem means Lean accepted the proof. --json makes Lean print messages as JSON, which our Python code will read in M1.

The first run can take a minute or more while your Mac loads Mathlib from disk. Later runs take about 20 seconds.

Common errors

  • unknown module prefix 'Mathlib': you're not inside lean_env, or you ran lean instead of lake env lean.
  • It takes many minutes and your CPU is at 100%: the cache download didn't finish and Lean is compiling Mathlib. Stop it (Ctrl-C) and rerun lake exe cache get.
  • command not found: lake: open a new terminal after brew install elan-init.

M0: Project skeleton

What and why. Before any math, we set up the Python package, a test runner, and the tool that builds this guide. Every later chapter adds one module and one test file, so having tests run from day one means you always know whether things still work.

  1. Create the Python project file at the project root.

    pyproject.toml
    [project]
name = "mathfinder"
version = "0.1.0"
description = "Collaborative AI theorem prover: LaTeX in, verified Lean proof out."
requires-python = ">=3.13"
dependencies = [
    "anthropic>=0.121",
]

[dependency-groups]
dev = [
    "pytest>=8",
]

[build-system]
requires = ["hatchling"]
build-backend = "hatchling.build"

[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]
packages = ["mathfinder"]

[tool.pytest.ini_options]
testpaths = ["tests"]
markers = [
    "llm: calls a real LLM (slow, uses quota). Run with: pytest -m llm",
]
addopts = "-m 'not llm'"

    [project] names the package and lists runtime dependencies (just the anthropic SDK for now). [dependency-groups] dev holds tools only needed while developing (pytest). [build-system] tells uv how to install our own package so that import mathfinder works everywhere. The pytest section registers an llm marker and skips those tests by default (-m 'not llm'), because they are slow and use your Claude quota.

  2. Create the package's first file.

    mathfinder/__init__.py
    """Math Finder: a collaborative AI theorem prover.

You give it a theorem (in LaTeX). It formalizes the theorem in Lean 4,
searches for a proof with an LLM planner + a Lean-checked critic,
and explains the verified proof back to you in plain math.
"""

from pathlib import Path

__version__ = "0.1.0"

# The project root is the folder that contains this package.
PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent

# The Lean project (Lean 4 + Mathlib) that every proof is checked in.
LEAN_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / "lean_env"

    A folder with an __init__.py is a Python package. Path(__file__) is this file's location; .resolve().parent.parent walks up to the project root. Every module will use LEAN_DIR to find the Lean project, so no path is hard-coded twice.

  3. Create a .gitignore so generated and heavy folders are never committed.

    .gitignore
    .venv/
__pycache__/
*.pyc
.pytest_cache/
runs/
lean_env/.lake/
lean_env/.mf_tmp/
cache_get.log

    lean_env/.lake/ holds the multi-GB Mathlib download; runs/ will hold search logs.

  4. Create the first tests.

    tests/test_smoke.py
    import shutil

import mathfinder


def test_package_imports():
    assert mathfinder.__version__ == "0.1.0"


def test_lean_project_exists():
    assert (mathfinder.LEAN_DIR / "lean-toolchain").exists()
    assert (mathfinder.LEAN_DIR / "lakefile.toml").exists()


def test_lake_is_installed():
    assert shutil.which("lake") is not None

    pytest runs every function whose name starts with test_. assert fails the test if the condition is false. shutil.which("lake") returns the path of the lake command, or None if it isn't installed.

  5. Create the virtual environment and install everything.

    uv sync

    This reads pyproject.toml, creates .venv/, installs the dependencies, and writes uv.lock (exact versions, so the setup can be reproduced later).

  6. Create the guide builder. (This is tooling for the guide itself; you don't need to understand it to build Math Finder.)

    tools/build_guide.py
    """Build guide.html from guide/template.html + guide/chapters/*.html.

Inside a chapter, write   {{file:path/to/file.py}}   and the build pastes
the REAL current contents of that project file into a code block. That is
how the guide's code always matches the project exactly.

If a file changes in a later chapter, the earlier chapter shows the version
from its own time instead:  {{file:path/to/file.py@m0}}  reads the frozen copy
guide/snapshots/m0/path/to/file.py. The newest chapter uses the live file.

Run:  uv run python tools/build_guide.py
"""

import html
import re
import sys
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
GUIDE_DIR = ROOT / "guide"
OUT = ROOT / "guide.html"

FILE_TAG = re.compile(r"\{\{file:([^}@]+)(?:@([\w-]+))?\}\}")
SECTION_TAG = re.compile(r'<section[^>]*\bid="([^"]+)"[^>]*\bdata-title="([^"]+)"')


def code_block(rel_path: str, snapshot: str | None = None) -> str:
    rel_path = rel_path.strip()
    if snapshot:
        path = GUIDE_DIR / "snapshots" / snapshot / rel_path
    else:
        path = ROOT / rel_path
    if not path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(f"guide references missing file: {path}")
    text = html.escape(path.read_text())
    return (
        '<div class="code"><div class="code-head">'
        f'<span class="path">{html.escape(rel_path)}</span></div>'
        f"<pre><code>{text}</code></pre></div>"
    )


def build() -> str:
    template = (GUIDE_DIR / "template.html").read_text()
    chapters = []
    toc = []
    for chapter_file in sorted((GUIDE_DIR / "chapters").glob("*.html")):
        body = chapter_file.read_text()
        body = FILE_TAG.sub(lambda m: code_block(m.group(1), m.group(2)), body)
        for sec_id, title in SECTION_TAG.findall(body):
            toc.append(f'<a href="#{sec_id}" data-sec="{sec_id}">{title}</a>')
        chapters.append(body)
    page = template.replace("{{TOC}}", "\n".join(toc))
    return page.replace("{{CHAPTERS}}", "\n".join(chapters))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    OUT.write_text(build())
    print(f"wrote {OUT.relative_to(ROOT)} ({OUT.stat().st_size // 1024} KB)")
    sys.exit(0)

    It reads guide/template.html and every guide/chapters/*.html file, and replaces each {{file:...}} tag with the real contents of that file. A regular expression (re.compile(...)) finds those tags. html.escape makes characters like < show up as text instead of being read as HTML.

  7. Add a test that fails whenever the guide is out of date with the code.

    tests/test_guide.py
    """The guide must match the real code. If this fails, run:
    uv run python tools/build_guide.py
"""

import importlib.util

from mathfinder import PROJECT_ROOT


def load_builder():
    path = PROJECT_ROOT / "tools" / "build_guide.py"
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("build_guide", path)
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module


def test_guide_is_up_to_date():
    expected = load_builder().build()
    actual = (PROJECT_ROOT / "guide.html").read_text()
    assert actual == expected, "guide.html is stale: run tools/build_guide.py"

    importlib loads tools/build_guide.py as a module even though tools/ isn't a package. The test rebuilds the guide in memory and compares it to guide.html on disk.

    You only need tools/build_guide.py and this test if you also want to rebuild the guide. They're always shown in their latest version.

  8. Build the guide.

    uv run python tools/build_guide.py

Checkpoint

uv run pytest -q

Expected:

....                                                                     [100%]
4 passed in 0.05s

Common errors

  • ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'mathfinder': run tests with uv run pytest, not plain pytest, and rerun uv sync.
  • test_guide_is_up_to_date fails: you changed a file; rebuild with uv run python tools/build_guide.py.
  • test_lake_is_installed fails: go back to Chapter 0, step 2.

M1: The Lean checker

What and why. Everything in Math Finder rests on one question: does Lean accept this proof? This chapter writes lean.py, which asks that question from Python. Starting Lean and loading Mathlib takes about 20 seconds, so we never check one proof at a time. We write many candidate theorems into one file, run Lean once, and use each message's line number to work out which theorem it belongs to. That batching is what makes search affordable.

Three Lean ideas you need

  • Theorem = statement + proof. theorem name (n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n := by simp: the part before := is the claim, and by simp is a tactic proof (a small program that builds the proof).
  • sorry means "trust me". Lean accepts it with a warning, so we must treat it as not proved.
  • Heartbeats are Lean's fuel counter. set_option maxHeartbeats 200000 in gives one theorem a budget, so a runaway tactic fails that theorem instead of hanging the whole file.

  1. Register a lean test marker so the slow Lean tests can be skipped. Replace the whole file with this version:

    pyproject.toml
    [project]
name = "mathfinder"
version = "0.1.0"
description = "Collaborative AI theorem prover: LaTeX in, verified Lean proof out."
requires-python = ">=3.13"
dependencies = [
    "anthropic>=0.121",
]

[dependency-groups]
dev = [
    "pytest>=8",
]

[build-system]
requires = ["hatchling"]
build-backend = "hatchling.build"

[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel]
packages = ["mathfinder"]

[tool.pytest.ini_options]
testpaths = ["tests"]
markers = [
    "llm: calls a real LLM (slow, uses quota). Run with: pytest -m llm",
    "lean: runs Lean (about 25 s each). Skip with: pytest -m 'not lean'",
]
addopts = "-m 'not llm'"

    What changed: one new line in markers, the one starting with "lean: runs Lean. Marking a test @pytest.mark.lean lets you run just the fast tests with uv run pytest -m "not lean".

  2. Create the checker.

    mathfinder/lean.py
    """Run Lean on generated code and read back what it says.

Starting Lean + loading Mathlib takes ~20 seconds, so we never check one proof
at a time. Instead we put many candidate theorems in ONE file, run Lean once,
and use line numbers to work out which messages belong to which theorem.
"""

import json
import os
import signal
import subprocess
import threading
import time
import uuid
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from mathfinder import LEAN_DIR

# autoImplicit false: an unknown name in a statement is an ERROR, not a silent new variable.
HEADER = "import Mathlib\n\nset_option autoImplicit false\n\nopen BigOperators Real Nat Topology\n\n"
DEFAULT_HEARTBEATS = 200_000  # Lean's "fuel" per theorem; stops runaway tactics
DEFAULT_TIMEOUT = 300  # seconds for one whole Lean run
ALLOWED_AXIOMS = {"propext", "Classical.choice", "Quot.sound"}

# At most this many Lean processes at once (each one uses a lot of memory).
MAX_PARALLEL = 2
_slots = threading.Semaphore(MAX_PARALLEL)


@dataclass
class Message:
    line: int  # 1-based line in the generated file
    severity: str  # "error", "warning" or "information"
    text: str
    kind: str = ""


@dataclass
class RunOutput:
    messages: list[Message]
    timed_out: bool
    seconds: float


@dataclass
class Decl:
    """One candidate theorem: `theorem <name> <statement> := by <proof>`."""

    name: str
    statement: str  # everything after the name, e.g. "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"
    proof: str  # tactic script, e.g. "simp"

    def render(self, heartbeats: int = DEFAULT_HEARTBEATS) -> str:
        body = indent(self.proof)
        return (
            f"set_option maxHeartbeats {heartbeats} in\n"
            f"theorem {self.name} {self.statement} := by\n{body}\n"
        )


@dataclass
class DeclResult:
    name: str
    ok: bool  # True only if Lean accepted it with no errors and no sorry
    errors: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    used_sorry: bool = False


@dataclass
class FinalResult:
    ok: bool
    errors: list[str]
    axioms: list[str]


def indent(text: str, spaces: int = 2) -> str:
    pad = " " * spaces
    return "\n".join(pad + line if line.strip() else line for line in text.strip("\n").split("\n"))


def run_lean(code: str, timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT) -> RunOutput:
    """Write `code` to a temporary .lean file, run Lean on it, parse its JSON messages."""
    tmp_dir = LEAN_DIR / ".mf_tmp"
    tmp_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    path = tmp_dir / f"check_{uuid.uuid4().hex}.lean"
    path.write_text(code)
    start = time.time()
    timed_out = False
    try:
        with _slots:
            proc = subprocess.Popen(
                ["lake", "env", "lean", "--json", str(path.relative_to(LEAN_DIR))],
                cwd=LEAN_DIR,
                stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
                text=True,
                start_new_session=True,  # so we can kill Lean and all its children
            )
            try:
                stdout, _ = proc.communicate(timeout=timeout)
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
                os.killpg(proc.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
                stdout, _ = proc.communicate()
                timed_out = True
    finally:
        path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    return RunOutput(parse_messages(stdout), timed_out, time.time() - start)


def parse_messages(stdout: str) -> list[Message]:
    messages = []
    for line in stdout.splitlines():
        if not line.startswith("{"):
            continue
        data = json.loads(line)
        messages.append(
            Message(
                line=data["pos"]["line"],
                severity=data["severity"],
                text=data["data"],
                kind=data.get("kind", ""),
            )
        )
    return messages


def check_decls(
    decls: list[Decl],
    preamble: str = "",
    heartbeats: int = DEFAULT_HEARTBEATS,
    timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT,
) -> dict[str, DeclResult]:
    """Check many theorems in one Lean run. Returns one result per theorem name.

    `preamble` is extra Lean code placed before the theorems (for example,
    lemmas they are allowed to use). Problems inside the preamble are ignored
    unless they are errors, which make every theorem fail.
    """
    code = HEADER + preamble.strip() + "\n\n"
    ranges = {}  # name -> (first line, last line)
    for decl in decls:
        first = code.count("\n") + 1
        code += decl.render(heartbeats) + "\n"
        ranges[decl.name] = (first, code.count("\n"))

    out = run_lean(code, timeout)
    results = {d.name: DeclResult(d.name, ok=True) for d in decls}

    for msg in out.messages:
        owner = next((n for n, (a, b) in ranges.items() if a <= msg.line <= b), None)
        if owner is None:
            if msg.severity == "error":  # broken header/preamble: nothing can pass
                for r in results.values():
                    r.ok = False
                    r.errors.append(f"preamble: {msg.text}")
            continue
        result = results[owner]
        if msg.severity == "error":
            result.ok = False
            result.errors.append(msg.text)
        elif msg.kind == "hasSorry":
            result.ok = False
            result.used_sorry = True

    if out.timed_out:
        for r in results.values():
            if r.ok:  # we can't trust a run that never finished
                r.ok = False
                r.errors.append(f"Lean timed out after {timeout}s")
    return results


def statement_errors(statement: str, timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT) -> list[str]:
    """Does this statement even make sense to Lean? Checks it with `sorry` as the proof."""
    result = check_decls([Decl("mf_statement", statement, "sorry")], timeout=timeout)
    return result["mf_statement"].errors


def verify_final(code: str, theorem: str, timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT) -> FinalResult:
    """The strict last check of a complete proof file.

    Passes only if Lean reports no errors, nothing uses sorry, and `theorem`
    depends on no axioms beyond Lean's three standard ones.
    """
    out = run_lean(HEADER + code.strip() + f"\n\n#print axioms {theorem}\n", timeout)
    errors = [m.text for m in out.messages if m.severity == "error"]
    if any(m.kind == "hasSorry" for m in out.messages):
        errors.append("proof uses sorry")
    if out.timed_out:
        errors.append(f"Lean timed out after {timeout}s")

    axioms = []
    report = [m.text for m in out.messages if m.text.startswith(f"'{theorem}' ")]
    if not report:
        errors.append(f"no axiom report for {theorem}")
    elif "depends on axioms:" in report[0]:
        inside = report[0].split("[", 1)[1].rsplit("]", 1)[0]
        axioms = [a.strip() for a in inside.split(",") if a.strip()]
    extra = [a for a in axioms if a not in ALLOWED_AXIOMS]
    if extra:
        errors.append(f"uses non-standard axioms: {extra}")
    return FinalResult(ok=not errors, errors=errors, axioms=axioms)

    HEADER starts every generated file. set_option autoImplicit false matters a lot: without it, a typo like undefined_thing in a statement silently becomes a new variable, and a wrong statement could "prove" fine. open BigOperators Real Nat Topology lets us write π, ∑ and n ! without prefixes.

    @dataclass turns a class with type-annotated fields into a simple record (it writes __init__ for you). field(default_factory=list) gives each record its own empty list. A plain = [] would be shared between all records, a classic Python bug.

    run_lean writes the code to a uniquely named file (uuid4), runs lake env lean --json on it, and always deletes the file (try/finally). start_new_session=True puts Lean in its own process group, so on timeout os.killpg kills Lean and any child processes it started. The Semaphore is a counter that lets at most MAX_PARALLEL threads inside the with _slots: block at once, so we never run more than two Lean processes in parallel.

    check_decls is the batching trick. While building the file it records each theorem's first and last line (code.count("\n") + 1 is the line number the next text will start on). Each Lean message carries a line number, so we hand it to whichever theorem's range contains it. A theorem passes only if it got no error and no hasSorry warning.

    verify_final is the strict last check: the whole proof file plus #print axioms. A proof that sneaks in axiom cheat : False can prove anything, and this catches it, because only Lean's three standard axioms (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound) are allowed.

  3. Create the tests.

    tests/test_lean.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/lean.py.

The ones marked `lean` really run Lean (about 25 seconds each).
Skip them with:  uv run pytest -m "not lean"
"""

import pytest

from mathfinder.lean import (
    Decl,
    check_decls,
    indent,
    parse_messages,
    run_lean,
    statement_errors,
    verify_final,
)

# ---------- fast tests: no Lean needed ----------


def test_indent_adds_two_spaces_per_line():
    assert indent("simp\nring") == "  simp\n  ring"


def test_render_wraps_heartbeats_and_indents_proof():
    text = Decl("t", "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n", "simp").render(heartbeats=1000)
    assert text == (
        "set_option maxHeartbeats 1000 in\n"
        "theorem t (n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n := by\n"
        "  simp\n"
    )


def test_parse_messages_reads_lean_json():
    line = (
        '{"caption":"","data":"declaration uses `sorry`","fileName":"x.lean",'
        '"kind":"hasSorry","pos":{"column":8,"line":14},"severity":"warning"}'
    )
    [msg] = parse_messages("some non-json noise\n" + line)
    assert (msg.line, msg.severity, msg.kind) == (14, "warning", "hasSorry")


# ---------- slow tests: these run Lean ----------


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def batch():
    """One Lean run that checks five different kinds of candidate at once."""
    preamble = "theorem helper (n : ℕ) : n * 1 = n := by simp"
    decls = [
        Decl("good", "(a b : ℝ) : a ^ 2 + b ^ 2 ≥ 2 * a * b", "nlinarith [sq_nonneg (a - b)]"),
        Decl("wrong", "(n : ℕ) : n + 1 > n + 2", "omega"),
        Decl("holey", "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n", "sorry"),
        Decl("nonsense", "(n : ℕ) : n = undefined_thing", "simp"),
        Decl("uses_helper", "(m : ℕ) : m * 1 = m", "exact helper m"),
    ]
    return check_decls(decls, preamble=preamble)


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_batch_correct_proof_passes(batch):
    assert batch["good"].ok
    assert batch["good"].errors == []

    assert not batch["wrong"].ok
    assert "omega could not prove the goal" in batch["wrong"].errors[0]

    assert batch["holey"].used_sorry

    assert batch["uses_helper"].ok

    assert statement_errors("(n : ℕ) : n ^ 3 - n ≥ 0") == []

    result = verify_final(code, "amgm2")
    assert result.ok, result.errors
    assert set(result.axioms) <= {"propext", "Classical.choice", "Quot.sound"}

    A fixture (@pytest.fixture) is setup code that tests receive as an argument. scope="module" means it runs once for the whole file, so five tests share a single 25-second Lean run. The test test_batch_bad_statement_fails exists because of the autoImplicit bug described above.

    uv run pytest -q -m "not lean" tests/test_lean.py

    uv run pytest -q

Checkpoint

uv run pytest -q

Expected (time varies, roughly 30–120 s depending on how warm your disk cache is):

.................                                                        [100%]
17 passed in 30.12s

Common errors

  • test_batch_bad_statement_fails fails with simp made no progress: your HEADER is missing set_option autoImplicit false.
  • Everything fails with preamble: unknown module prefix: LEAN_DIR doesn't point at lean_env/, or the Mathlib cache is missing (Chapter 0, step 7).
  • Tests hang for minutes: Lean is compiling Mathlib instead of using the cache. Rerun cd lean_env && lake exe cache get.
  • test_run_lean_timeout_is_reported fails: on a very fast machine import Mathlib might finish in under 1 s. That's unlikely, but if it happens, it's harmless.

M2: The hammer (no AI needed)

What and why. Many small lemmas fall to a single automatic tactic. Asking an LLM costs seconds and money; one Lean run that tries a dozen tactics at once costs nothing. So the hammer always goes first. In the same Lean run it also asks plausible to test the statement on random values. If it finds values that make the statement false, the search drops that lemma immediately and tells the planner why.

The tactics we try

TacticGood at
norm_numarithmetic with concrete numbers: 2 ^ 10 = 1024
simprewriting with hundreds of known simplification facts: n + 0 = n
omegalinear facts about integers / naturals: n < m → n + 1 ≤ m
decidesmall statements Lean can just compute: Nat.Prime 97
linarith / nlinarithlinear / nonlinear inequalities over ordered fields such as ℝ
positivitygoals like 0 < x ^ 2 + 1
ring_nf, field_simpalgebraic identities, clearing denominators
aesop, tauto, simp_alllogic puzzles and "use the hypotheses" goals
norm_num <;> nlinarith<;> means "then run the next tactic on every goal that's left"
exact?searches all of Mathlib for one lemma that closes the goal

  1. Fix a soundness bug in the checker and keep Lean's info messages. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/lean.py
    """Run Lean on generated code and read back what it says.

Starting Lean + loading Mathlib takes ~20 seconds, so we never check one proof
at a time. Instead we put many candidate theorems in ONE file, run Lean once,
and use line numbers to work out which messages belong to which theorem.
"""

import json
import os
import signal
import subprocess
import threading
import time
import uuid
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from mathfinder import LEAN_DIR

# autoImplicit false: an unknown name in a statement is an ERROR, not a silent new variable.
HEADER = "import Mathlib\n\nset_option autoImplicit false\n\nopen BigOperators Real Nat Topology\n\n"
DEFAULT_HEARTBEATS = 200_000  # Lean's "fuel" per theorem; stops runaway tactics
DEFAULT_TIMEOUT = 300  # seconds for one whole Lean run
ALLOWED_AXIOMS = {"propext", "Classical.choice", "Quot.sound"}

# At most this many Lean processes at once (each one uses a lot of memory).
MAX_PARALLEL = 2
_slots = threading.Semaphore(MAX_PARALLEL)


@dataclass
class Message:
    line: int  # 1-based line in the generated file
    severity: str  # "error", "warning" or "information"
    text: str
    kind: str = ""


@dataclass
class RunOutput:
    messages: list[Message]
    timed_out: bool
    seconds: float


@dataclass
class Decl:
    """One candidate proof of a statement.

    It is written as an `example`, not a `theorem`: Lean checks an example but
    never adds it to the environment, so one candidate can't cite another
    (failed!) candidate from the same file as if it were proved. `name` is only
    our label for finding its messages.
    """

    name: str
    statement: str  # e.g. "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"
    proof: str  # tactic script, e.g. "simp"

    def render(self, heartbeats: int = DEFAULT_HEARTBEATS) -> str:
        body = indent(self.proof)
        return (
            f"-- {self.name}\n"
            f"set_option maxHeartbeats {heartbeats} in\n"
            f"example {self.statement} := by\n{body}\n"
        )


@dataclass
class DeclResult:
    name: str
    ok: bool  # True only if Lean accepted it with no errors and no sorry
    errors: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    used_sorry: bool = False
    infos: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)  # e.g. "Try this: exact foo" from exact?


@dataclass
class FinalResult:
    ok: bool
    errors: list[str]
    axioms: list[str]


def indent(text: str, spaces: int = 2) -> str:
    pad = " " * spaces
    return "\n".join(pad + line if line.strip() else line for line in text.strip("\n").split("\n"))


def run_lean(code: str, timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT) -> RunOutput:
    """Write `code` to a temporary .lean file, run Lean on it, parse its JSON messages."""
    tmp_dir = LEAN_DIR / ".mf_tmp"
    tmp_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    path = tmp_dir / f"check_{uuid.uuid4().hex}.lean"
    path.write_text(code)
    start = time.time()
    timed_out = False
    try:
        with _slots:
            proc = subprocess.Popen(
                ["lake", "env", "lean", "--json", str(path.relative_to(LEAN_DIR))],
                cwd=LEAN_DIR,
                stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
                text=True,
                start_new_session=True,  # so we can kill Lean and all its children
            )
            try:
                stdout, _ = proc.communicate(timeout=timeout)
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
                os.killpg(proc.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
                stdout, _ = proc.communicate()
                timed_out = True
    finally:
        path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    return RunOutput(parse_messages(stdout), timed_out, time.time() - start)


def parse_messages(stdout: str) -> list[Message]:
    messages = []
    for line in stdout.splitlines():
        if not line.startswith("{"):
            continue
        data = json.loads(line)
        messages.append(
            Message(
                line=data["pos"]["line"],
                severity=data["severity"],
                text=data["data"],
                kind=data.get("kind", ""),
            )
        )
    return messages


def check_decls(
    decls: list[Decl],
    preamble: str = "",
    heartbeats: int = DEFAULT_HEARTBEATS,
    timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT,
) -> dict[str, DeclResult]:
    """Check many theorems in one Lean run. Returns one result per theorem name.

    `preamble` is extra Lean code placed before the theorems (for example,
    lemmas they are allowed to use). Problems inside the preamble are ignored
    unless they are errors, which make every theorem fail.
    """
    code = HEADER + preamble.strip() + "\n\n"
    ranges = {}  # name -> (first line, last line)
    for decl in decls:
        first = code.count("\n") + 1
        code += decl.render(heartbeats) + "\n"
        ranges[decl.name] = (first, code.count("\n"))

    out = run_lean(code, timeout)
    results = {d.name: DeclResult(d.name, ok=True) for d in decls}

    for msg in out.messages:
        owner = next((n for n, (a, b) in ranges.items() if a <= msg.line <= b), None)
        if owner is None:
            if msg.severity == "error":  # broken header/preamble: nothing can pass
                for r in results.values():
                    r.ok = False
                    r.errors.append(f"preamble: {msg.text}")
            continue
        result = results[owner]
        if msg.severity == "error":
            result.ok = False
            result.errors.append(msg.text)
        elif msg.kind == "hasSorry":
            result.ok = False
            result.used_sorry = True
        elif msg.severity == "information":
            result.infos.append(msg.text)

    if out.timed_out:
        for r in results.values():
            if r.ok:  # we can't trust a run that never finished
                r.ok = False
                r.errors.append(f"Lean timed out after {timeout}s")
    return results


def statement_errors(statement: str, timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT) -> list[str]:
    """Does this statement even make sense to Lean? Checks it with `sorry` as the proof."""
    result = check_decls([Decl("mf_statement", statement, "sorry")], timeout=timeout)
    return result["mf_statement"].errors


def verify_final(code: str, theorem: str, timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT) -> FinalResult:
    """The strict last check of a complete proof file.

    Passes only if Lean reports no errors, nothing uses sorry, and `theorem`
    depends on no axioms beyond Lean's three standard ones.
    """
    out = run_lean(HEADER + code.strip() + f"\n\n#print axioms {theorem}\n", timeout)
    errors = [m.text for m in out.messages if m.severity == "error"]
    if any(m.kind == "hasSorry" for m in out.messages):
        errors.append("proof uses sorry")
    if out.timed_out:
        errors.append(f"Lean timed out after {timeout}s")

    axioms = []
    report = [m.text for m in out.messages if m.text.startswith(f"'{theorem}' ")]
    if not report:
        errors.append(f"no axiom report for {theorem}")
    elif "depends on axioms:" in report[0]:
        inside = report[0].split("[", 1)[1].rsplit("]", 1)[0]
        axioms = [a.strip() for a in inside.split(",") if a.strip()]
    extra = [a for a in axioms if a not in ALLOWED_AXIOMS]
    if extra:
        errors.append(f"uses non-standard axioms: {extra}")
    return FinalResult(ok=not errors, errors=errors, axioms=axioms)

    What changed and why: candidates are now written as example instead of theorem name. The hammer test caught this: exact? "proved" a false-looking goal by citing mf_hammer_0, an earlier candidate in the same file that had failed. Lean still adds a failed theorem to the file's environment (as if it were proved by sorry), so later candidates could cite it. An example is checked but never added to the environment, so nothing can cite it. The -- name comment line keeps the file readable when you debug it.

    Second change: DeclResult gets an infos list holding Lean's "information" messages. We need them because exact? reports the lemma it found that way.

  2. Update the checker tests to match. Replace the whole file:

    tests/test_lean.py

    assert (msg.line, msg.severity, msg.kind) == (14, "warning", "hasSorry")

  3. Create the hammer.

    mathfinder/hammer.py
    """The hammer: try many cheap automatic tactics at once, and look for counterexamples.

No AI here. Lots of small lemmas fall to a single tactic like `omega` or
`nlinarith`, so we always try these first. It costs one Lean run, and an
LLM call costs far more.

In the same Lean run we also ask `plausible` to test the statement on random
values. If it finds values that make the statement false, nobody needs to
waste time trying to prove it.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass

from mathfinder.lean import DEFAULT_TIMEOUT, Decl, check_decls

# Roughly from fastest/most specific to slowest/most general.
TACTICS = [
    "norm_num",
    "simp",
    "omega",
    "decide",
    "linarith",
    "nlinarith",
    "positivity",
    "ring_nf",
    "field_simp",
    "aesop",
    "tauto",
    "simp_all",
    "norm_num <;> nlinarith",  # simplify the numbers first, then nonlinear arithmetic
    "exact?",  # searches the whole library: slowest, so last
]
HAMMER_HEARTBEATS = 100_000  # less fuel than normal: if a tactic doesn't work fast, it won't work


@dataclass
class HammerResult:
    proved: bool
    tactic: str = ""  # the tactic that worked
    proof: str = ""  # proof text to keep (for exact?, the lemma it found)
    counterexample: str = ""  # e.g. "n := 4; issue: 16 ≤ 11 does not hold"
    testable: bool = True  # False when plausible can't generate values (e.g. for ℝ)


def hammer(
    statement: str,
    preamble: str = "",
    tactics: list[str] = TACTICS,
    timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT,
) -> HammerResult:
    """Try every tactic on `statement`, plus a counterexample search, in ONE Lean run."""
    decls = [Decl(f"mf_hammer_{i}", statement, tac) for i, tac in enumerate(tactics)]
    decls.append(Decl("mf_refute", statement, "plausible"))
    results = check_decls(decls, preamble, heartbeats=HAMMER_HEARTBEATS, timeout=timeout)

    out = HammerResult(proved=False)
    for i, tac in enumerate(tactics):
        if results[f"mf_hammer_{i}"].ok:
            out.proved = True
            out.tactic = tac
            out.proof = tac
            if tac == "exact?":  # keep the lemma it found, not the slow search
                out.proof = suggestion(results[f"mf_hammer_{i}"].infos) or tac
            break

    for err in results["mf_refute"].errors:
        if "Found a counter-example!" in err:
            out.counterexample = counterexample_text(err)
        if "testable" in err:
            out.testable = False
    return out


def counterexample_text(error: str) -> str:
    """Turn plausible's report into one line, e.g. 'n := 4; issue: 16 ≤ 11 does not hold'."""
    after = error.split("Found a counter-example!", 1)[1]
    lines = []
    for line in after.strip().split("\n"):
        if line.startswith("---") or line.startswith("("):  # end marker / "(0 shrinks)"
            break
        lines.append(line.strip())
    return "; ".join(lines)


def suggestion(infos: list[str]) -> str:
    """Read 'Try this: [apply] exact Nat.add_comm a b' and return 'exact Nat.add_comm a b'."""
    for text in infos:
        if text.startswith("Try this:"):
            found = text.split(":", 1)[1].strip()
            return found.removeprefix("[apply]").strip()
    return ""

    One Lean run for everything: hammer builds one candidate per tactic plus one plausible candidate, and hands them all to check_decls from M1. The first tactic in the list that worked wins, and since the list goes from fast to slow, the proof we keep is a cheap one.

    Why less fuel (HAMMER_HEARTBEATS = 100_000): a tactic that is going to work almost always works quickly, and fifteen slow failures would make every hammer call slow.

    exact? prints Try this: [apply] exact Nat.le_of_dvd hb h. suggestion keeps just exact Nat.le_of_dvd hb h, so the saved proof is instant instead of re-running a library search. str.removeprefix removes a prefix only if it's there.

    plausible plugs random values into the statement. When it finds a failure, Lean reports an error containing Found a counter-example! and counterexample_text squeezes it into one line. It can only sample types it knows how to generate (like ℕ and ℤ). For ℝ it reports that it can't build a "testable" instance, which we record as testable = False. A missing counterexample means "not refuted", never "proved".

  4. Create its tests.

    tests/test_hammer.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/hammer.py. The `lean` ones take ~30-60 s each."""

import pytest

from mathfinder.hammer import counterexample_text, hammer, suggestion

# ---------- fast tests: parse real Lean output, no Lean needed ----------

PLAUSIBLE_ERROR = (
    "\n===================\nFound a counter-example!\nn := 4\n"
    "issue: 16 ≤ 11 does not hold\n(0 shrinks)\n-------------------"
)


def test_counterexample_text_is_one_line():
    assert counterexample_text(PLAUSIBLE_ERROR) == "n := 4; issue: 16 ≤ 11 does not hold"


def test_suggestion_strips_try_this_and_apply():
    infos = ["something else", "Try this:\n  [apply] exact Nat.add_comm a b"]
    assert suggestion(infos) == "exact Nat.add_comm a b"


def test_suggestion_empty_when_missing():
    assert suggestion(["no suggestion here"]) == ""


# ---------- slow tests: these run Lean ----------


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_hammer_proves_nonlinear_inequality():
    result = hammer("(n : ℕ) (h : n > 3) : 2 * n < n * n")
    assert result.proved
    assert result.counterexample == ""


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_hammer_finds_counterexample_for_false_statement():
    result = hammer("(n : ℕ) : n ^ 2 ≤ 2 * n + 3")
    assert not result.proved
    assert "n :=" in result.counterexample


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_hammer_reports_untestable_types():
    # x^2 ≥ x is false for x = 1/2, but plausible can't sample real numbers.
    result = hammer("(x : ℝ) : x ^ 2 ≥ x")
    assert not result.proved
    assert not result.testable


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_exact_search_keeps_the_lemma_it_found():
    result = hammer("(a b : ℕ) (h : a ∣ b) (hb : 0 < b) : a ≤ b", tactics=["exact?"])
    assert result.proved
    assert result.proof.startswith("exact ")
    assert "?" not in result.proof

    The three fast tests use real messages copied from Lean, so the parsers are tested against what Lean actually prints. tactics=["exact?"] restricts the hammer to one tactic, so we know the library-search path is exercised.

    uv run pytest -q

Checkpoint

Try the hammer by hand on a true and a false statement:

uv run python -c "
from mathfinder.hammer import hammer
print(hammer('(n : ℕ) (h : n > 3) : 2 * n < n * n'))
print(hammer('(n : ℕ) : n ^ 2 ≤ 2 * n + 3'))
"

And the full suite:

uv run pytest -q
.........................                                                [100%]

The time varies a lot (2–4 minutes) with how warm your disk cache is. The count, 25, should match.

Common errors

  • test_hammer_finds_counterexample... fails with proved=True, tactic='exact?', proof='exact mf_hammer_0 ...': your Decl.render still writes theorem. Use example (step 1).
  • unknown tactic 'plausible': your Mathlib is older than late 2024 (it used to be called slim_check). Recreate lean_env as in Chapter 0.
  • The hammer takes minutes: a cold disk cache. The second run is much faster.

M3: Talking to Claude

What and why. The planner, the prover and the explainer all need to ask Claude questions. This chapter writes one small module, llm.py, with one function that matters: complete(system, prompt), which sends a question and returns the answer text. Behind it are two interchangeable backends: the claude command line tool (uses your Claude subscription, no key needed) and the Anthropic API (needs an API key). You switch between them with an environment variable, a named setting your terminal passes to every program it starts.

Two words you'll see

  • System prompt: standing instructions for the model ("You are a Lean 4 expert. Answer with JSON only.").
  • Prompt: the actual question for this call.

  1. Create the module.

    mathfinder/llm.py
    """Talk to Claude.

Two interchangeable backends, picked with the MATHFINDER_LLM environment variable:
  cli  (default) runs the `claude` command line tool: uses your Claude subscription, no key
  api            calls the Anthropic API with the official SDK: needs ANTHROPIC_API_KEY

Every other module only calls `complete(system, prompt)` or `complete_json(...)`,
so it never needs to know which backend is in use.
"""

import json
import os
import re
import subprocess
import tempfile

MODEL = "claude-opus-5-5"
CLI_TIMEOUT = 600  # seconds; a hard proof plan can take a few minutes to write
API_MAX_TOKENS = 64_000


class LLMError(Exception):
    """The model couldn't give us a usable answer (crash, timeout, refusal, bad JSON)."""


def complete_cli(system: str, prompt: str) -> str:
    cmd = [
        "claude", "-p",
        "--system-prompt", system,
        "--tools", "",  # text only: it may not run commands or edit files
        "--no-session-persistence",
        "--strict-mcp-config",
        "--setting-sources", "",  # ignore your personal settings and hooks
        "--output-format", "text",
    ]
    if os.environ.get("MATHFINDER_CLI_MODEL"):
        cmd += ["--model", os.environ["MATHFINDER_CLI_MODEL"]]
    try:
        proc = subprocess.run(
            cmd,
            input=prompt,  # the prompt goes in on stdin, so it can be any length
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            timeout=CLI_TIMEOUT,
            cwd=tempfile.gettempdir(),  # run outside the project so no project files leak in
        )
    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
        raise LLMError(f"claude CLI timed out after {CLI_TIMEOUT}s")
    if proc.returncode != 0:
        raise LLMError(f"claude CLI failed: {proc.stderr.strip()[-500:]}")
    text = proc.stdout.strip()
    if not text:
        raise LLMError("claude CLI returned an empty answer")
    return text


_client = None


def get_client():
    """Create the API client once and reuse it (it keeps connections open)."""
    global _client
    if _client is None:
        import anthropic  # imported here so the cli backend works even without a key

        _client = anthropic.Anthropic()  # reads ANTHROPIC_API_KEY from the environment
    return _client


def complete_api(system: str, prompt: str) -> str:
    with get_client().beta.messages.stream(
        model=MODEL,
        max_tokens=API_MAX_TOKENS,
        system=system,
        messages=[{"role": "user", "content": prompt}],
        output_config={"effort": "high"},
        betas=["server-side-fallback-2026-07-01"],
        fallbacks="default",  # if a safety check declines, the server retries on a fallback model
    ) as stream:
        message = stream.get_final_message()
    if message.stop_reason == "refusal":
        raise LLMError("the model declined this request")
    text = "".join(block.text for block in message.content if block.type == "text").strip()
    if not text:
        raise LLMError(f"empty answer (stop_reason={message.stop_reason})")
    return text


BACKENDS = {"cli": complete_cli, "api": complete_api}


def complete(system: str, prompt: str) -> str:
    """Ask Claude one question and return its text answer."""
    name = os.environ.get("MATHFINDER_LLM", "cli")
    if name not in BACKENDS:
        raise LLMError(f"unknown MATHFINDER_LLM={name!r}; use one of {sorted(BACKENDS)}")
    return BACKENDS[name](system, prompt)


def extract_json(text: str):
    """Pull a JSON value out of a model answer, with or without a ```json fence."""
    fenced = re.search(r"```(?:json)?\s*\n(.*?)\n```", text, re.DOTALL)
    if fenced:
        candidate = fenced.group(1)
    else:
        starts = [i for i in (text.find("{"), text.find("[")) if i != -1]
        if not starts:
            raise LLMError("no JSON found in the answer")
        start = min(starts)
        end = text.rfind("}" if text[start] == "{" else "]")
        candidate = text[start : end + 1]
    try:
        return json.loads(candidate)
    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
        raise LLMError(f"invalid JSON: {e}")


def complete_json(system: str, prompt: str, retries: int = 1):
    """Like complete(), but returns parsed JSON. On bad JSON, asks again with the error."""
    answer = complete(system, prompt)
    for attempt in range(retries + 1):
        try:
            return extract_json(answer)
        except LLMError as e:
            if attempt == retries:
                raise
            answer = complete(
                system,
                f"{prompt}\n\nYour previous answer could not be parsed ({e}). "
                "Reply again with ONLY the JSON, inside a ```json block.",
            )

    complete_cli runs claude -p ("print mode": answer once and exit) with subprocess.run. The prompt goes in on stdin (input=prompt) rather than as a command argument, because arguments have a length limit and prompts with Lean code can be long. --tools "" means Claude can only write text, not run commands. cwd=tempfile.gettempdir() runs it from a neutral folder so your project's files don't get mixed into the conversation.

    LLMError is our own exception class. raise LLMError(...) stops the current function and travels up until some caller handles it with try/except. Later the search catches it, so one failed call doesn't crash a whole proof attempt.

    complete_api uses the official SDK. with ... stream(...) as stream: is a context manager: the connection is opened at with and closed automatically at the end of the block, even on errors. We stream (receive the answer in pieces) because long answers would otherwise hit HTTP timeouts, and get_final_message() waits for all the pieces and returns the full message. effort: "high" asks for careful reasoning. fallbacks="default" (with its beta flag) means that if a safety check declines the request, the server retries it on a fallback model. We still check stop_reason == "refusal" in case everything declined.

    get_client creates the client once and stores it in a module-level variable (global _client). The import anthropic inside it means the CLI backend works without the API being set up.

    BACKENDS is a dictionary from name to function. complete looks up MATHFINDER_LLM (default "cli") and calls that function. Tests add a fake backend to this dictionary, which is why every other module calls llm.complete instead of picking a backend itself.

    extract_json: models often wrap JSON in a ```json fence or add a sentence around it. The regular expression ```(?:json)?\s*\n(.*?)\n``` captures the text between fences; re.DOTALL lets . match newlines too. Without a fence we take everything from the first { or [ to the last matching bracket. complete_json retries once, showing the model its parse error.

  2. Create the tests.

    tests/test_llm.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/llm.py.

Most tests use a fake backend, so they're instant and free.
The one `llm` test really calls Claude:  uv run pytest -m llm tests/test_llm.py
"""

from types import SimpleNamespace

import pytest

from mathfinder import llm


@pytest.fixture
def fake_replies(monkeypatch):
    """Install a fake backend that returns the given replies in order."""

    def install(*replies):
        queue = list(replies)
        calls = []

        def fake(system, prompt):
            calls.append(prompt)
            return queue.pop(0)

        monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", fake)
        monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
        return calls

    return install


def test_complete_uses_the_backend_from_the_env_var(fake_replies):
    fake_replies("hello")
    assert llm.complete("sys", "hi") == "hello"


def test_unknown_backend_is_a_clear_error(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "nope")
    with pytest.raises(llm.LLMError, match="unknown MATHFINDER_LLM"):
        llm.complete("sys", "hi")


def test_extract_json_from_fenced_block():
    text = 'Here is the plan:\n```json\n{"lemmas": [1, 2]}\n```\nGood luck!'
    assert llm.extract_json(text) == {"lemmas": [1, 2]}


def test_extract_json_without_fence():
    assert llm.extract_json('Sure. {"ok": true} That is all.') == {"ok": True}
    assert llm.extract_json("[1, 2, 3]") == [1, 2, 3]


def test_extract_json_failure_raises():
    with pytest.raises(llm.LLMError):
        llm.extract_json("no json at all")


def test_complete_json_retries_after_bad_json(fake_replies):
    calls = fake_replies("oops, not json", '```json\n{"fixed": 1}\n```')
    assert llm.complete_json("sys", "give json") == {"fixed": 1}
    assert len(calls) == 2
    assert "could not be parsed" in calls[1]


# ---------- the API backend, with a fake client (no key, no network) ----------


class FakeStream:
    def __init__(self, message, seen, kwargs):
        self.message = message
        seen.update(kwargs)

    def __enter__(self):
        return self

    def __exit__(self, *exc):
        return False

    def get_final_message(self):
        return self.message


def fake_client(message, seen):
    stream = lambda **kwargs: FakeStream(message, seen, kwargs)  # noqa: E731
    return SimpleNamespace(beta=SimpleNamespace(messages=SimpleNamespace(stream=stream)))


def text_message(text, stop_reason="end_turn"):
    block = SimpleNamespace(type="text", text=text)
    return SimpleNamespace(content=[block], stop_reason=stop_reason)


def test_api_backend_sends_the_right_request(monkeypatch):
    seen = {}
    monkeypatch.setattr(llm, "_client", fake_client(text_message("42"), seen))
    assert llm.complete_api("be brief", "6*7?") == "42"
    assert seen["model"] == "claude-opus-5-5"
    assert seen["output_config"] == {"effort": "high"}
    assert seen["betas"] == ["server-side-fallback-2026-07-01"]
    assert seen["fallbacks"] == "default"
    assert seen["messages"] == [{"role": "user", "content": "6*7?"}]


def test_api_backend_raises_on_refusal(monkeypatch):
    message = SimpleNamespace(content=[], stop_reason="refusal")
    monkeypatch.setattr(llm, "_client", fake_client(message, {}))
    with pytest.raises(llm.LLMError, match="declined"):
        llm.complete_api("sys", "hi")


# ---------- real call ----------


@pytest.mark.llm
def test_cli_backend_really_answers(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "cli")
    answer = llm.complete("You are terse.", "Reply with exactly the word: OK")
    assert "OK" in answer

    monkeypatch is a built-in pytest fixture that changes something just for one test and undoes it afterwards: setitem adds our fake backend to BACKENDS, setenv sets the environment variable, and setattr(llm, "_client", ...) swaps in a fake API client.

    SimpleNamespace makes a quick object with whatever attributes you give it, so fake_client(...).beta.messages.stream(...) works without a real SDK client. FakeStream implements __enter__/__exit__, the two methods that make a class usable in a with statement. The fake records the keyword arguments it receives, so the test can check we send exactly the request we mean to, with no key and no network.

  3. Run the fast tests. From now on, use this combined filter for "fast only":

    uv run pytest -q -m "not lean and not llm"

    Why not just -m "not lean"? The default -m 'not llm' in pyproject.toml is replaced (not combined) when you pass your own -m, so -m "not lean" would quietly run the real Claude test too.

  4. Make one real call through the CLI backend (about 5 seconds, uses a little of your Claude quota):

    uv run pytest -q -m llm tests/test_llm.py

  5. Optional: if you have an API key, try the API backend too. The key lives only in this terminal session:

    export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY="sk-ant-..."
MATHFINDER_LLM=api uv run pytest -q -m llm tests/test_llm.py

    VAR=value command sets a variable for that one command only.

Checkpoint

Ask Claude for a Lean proof, as JSON:

uv run python -c "
from mathfinder.llm import complete_json
print(complete_json('You are a Lean 4 + Mathlib expert. Answer with JSON only.',
  'Give a one-line Lean 4 tactic proof for: (a b : ℝ) : a ^ 2 + b ^ 2 ≥ 2 * a * b. Reply as {\"tactic\": \"...\"}'))
"

Expected (the exact tactic may differ):

{'tactic': 'nlinarith [sq_nonneg (a - b)]'}

Fast tests:

uv run pytest -q -m "not lean and not llm"
..................                                                       [100%]
18 passed, 16 deselected in 0.02s

Common errors

  • LLMError: claude CLI failed: ... not logged in: run claude once interactively and log in.
  • FileNotFoundError: 'claude': the CLI isn't installed or not on your PATH (Chapter 0, step 4).
  • anthropic.AuthenticationError with MATHFINDER_LLM=api: the key is missing or wrong in this terminal. Run the export again.
  • unknown MATHFINDER_LLM='API': names are lowercase (cli or api).

M4a: The planner and the critic's first check

What and why. This is where the search starts. We don't ask Claude for a whole proof, which for anything non-trivial usually has one broken step somewhere. We ask for a plan: an idea in a sentence or two, a few small lemmas (helper facts), and a short main proof that combines them. In game terms the planner is the policy: it proposes moves. Before anyone spends effort proving the lemmas, the critic asks Lean one question: if all these lemmas were true, would the main proof work? We find out by writing each lemma with sorry as its proof ("pretend this is proved") and checking the main proof against them. A plan that fails this check is broken however well its lemmas get proved, and we know that after a single Lean run.

  1. Give each candidate an optional private context in the checker. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/lean.py
    """Run Lean on generated code and read back what it says.

Starting Lean + loading Mathlib takes ~20 seconds, so we never check one proof
at a time. Instead we put many candidate theorems in ONE file, run Lean once,
and use line numbers to work out which messages belong to which theorem.
"""

import json
import os
import signal
import subprocess
import threading
import time
import uuid
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from mathfinder import LEAN_DIR

# autoImplicit false: an unknown name in a statement is an ERROR, not a silent new variable.
HEADER = "import Mathlib\n\nset_option autoImplicit false\n\nopen BigOperators Real Nat Topology\n\n"
DEFAULT_HEARTBEATS = 200_000  # Lean's "fuel" per theorem; stops runaway tactics
DEFAULT_TIMEOUT = 300  # seconds for one whole Lean run
ALLOWED_AXIOMS = {"propext", "Classical.choice", "Quot.sound"}

# At most this many Lean processes at once (each one uses a lot of memory).
MAX_PARALLEL = 2
_slots = threading.Semaphore(MAX_PARALLEL)


@dataclass
class Message:
    line: int  # 1-based line in the generated file
    severity: str  # "error", "warning" or "information"
    text: str
    kind: str = ""


@dataclass
class RunOutput:
    messages: list[Message]
    timed_out: bool
    seconds: float


@dataclass
class Decl:
    """One candidate proof of a statement.

    It is written as an `example`, not a `theorem`: Lean checks an example but
    never adds it to the environment, so one candidate can't cite another
    (failed!) candidate from the same file as if it were proved. `name` is only
    our label for finding its messages.

    `context` is optional Lean code that only this candidate can see, such as
    the lemmas of one proof plan. It goes inside a namespace of its own, so two
    plans can both have a lemma called `key` without clashing. Errors inside the
    context count as errors of this candidate.
    """

    name: str
    statement: str  # e.g. "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"
    proof: str  # tactic script, e.g. "simp"
    context: str = ""

    def render(self, heartbeats: int = DEFAULT_HEARTBEATS) -> str:
        body = indent(self.proof)
        example = (
            f"set_option maxHeartbeats {heartbeats} in\n"
            f"example {self.statement} := by\n{body}\n"
        )
        if not self.context:
            return f"-- {self.name}\n{example}"
        return (
            f"-- {self.name}\n"
            f"namespace {self.name}\n\n"
            f"{self.context.strip()}\n\n"
            f"{example}\n"
            f"end {self.name}\n"
        )


@dataclass
class DeclResult:
    name: str
    ok: bool  # True only if Lean accepted it with no errors and no sorry
    errors: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    used_sorry: bool = False
    infos: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)  # e.g. "Try this: exact foo" from exact?


@dataclass
class FinalResult:
    ok: bool
    errors: list[str]
    axioms: list[str]


def indent(text: str, spaces: int = 2) -> str:
    pad = " " * spaces
    return "\n".join(pad + line if line.strip() else line for line in text.strip("\n").split("\n"))


def run_lean(code: str, timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT) -> RunOutput:
    """Write `code` to a temporary .lean file, run Lean on it, parse its JSON messages."""
    tmp_dir = LEAN_DIR / ".mf_tmp"
    tmp_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    path = tmp_dir / f"check_{uuid.uuid4().hex}.lean"
    path.write_text(code)
    start = time.time()
    timed_out = False
    try:
        with _slots:
            proc = subprocess.Popen(
                ["lake", "env", "lean", "--json", str(path.relative_to(LEAN_DIR))],
                cwd=LEAN_DIR,
                stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
                text=True,
                start_new_session=True,  # so we can kill Lean and all its children
            )
            try:
                stdout, _ = proc.communicate(timeout=timeout)
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
                os.killpg(proc.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
                stdout, _ = proc.communicate()
                timed_out = True
    finally:
        path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
    return RunOutput(parse_messages(stdout), timed_out, time.time() - start)


def parse_messages(stdout: str) -> list[Message]:
    messages = []
    for line in stdout.splitlines():
        if not line.startswith("{"):
            continue
        data = json.loads(line)
        messages.append(
            Message(
                line=data["pos"]["line"],
                severity=data["severity"],
                text=data["data"],
                kind=data.get("kind", ""),
            )
        )
    return messages


def check_decls(
    decls: list[Decl],
    preamble: str = "",
    heartbeats: int = DEFAULT_HEARTBEATS,
    timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT,
) -> dict[str, DeclResult]:
    """Check many theorems in one Lean run. Returns one result per theorem name.

    `preamble` is extra Lean code placed before the theorems (for example,
    lemmas they are allowed to use). Problems inside the preamble are ignored
    unless they are errors, which make every theorem fail.
    """
    code = HEADER + preamble.strip() + "\n\n"
    ranges = {}  # name -> (first line, last line)
    for decl in decls:
        first = code.count("\n") + 1
        code += decl.render(heartbeats) + "\n"
        ranges[decl.name] = (first, code.count("\n"))

    out = run_lean(code, timeout)
    results = {d.name: DeclResult(d.name, ok=True) for d in decls}

    for msg in out.messages:
        owner = next((n for n, (a, b) in ranges.items() if a <= msg.line <= b), None)
        if owner is None:
            if msg.severity == "error":  # broken header/preamble: nothing can pass
                for r in results.values():
                    r.ok = False
                    r.errors.append(f"preamble: {msg.text}")
            continue
        result = results[owner]
        if msg.severity == "error":
            result.ok = False
            result.errors.append(msg.text)
        elif msg.kind == "hasSorry":
            result.ok = False
            result.used_sorry = True
        elif msg.severity == "information":
            result.infos.append(msg.text)

    if out.timed_out:
        for r in results.values():
            if r.ok:  # we can't trust a run that never finished
                r.ok = False
                r.errors.append(f"Lean timed out after {timeout}s")
    return results


def statement_errors(statement: str, timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT) -> list[str]:
    """Does this statement even make sense to Lean? Checks it with `sorry` as the proof."""
    result = check_decls([Decl("mf_statement", statement, "sorry")], timeout=timeout)
    return result["mf_statement"].errors


def verify_final(code: str, theorem: str, timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT) -> FinalResult:
    """The strict last check of a complete proof file.

    Passes only if Lean reports no errors, nothing uses sorry, and `theorem`
    depends on no axioms beyond Lean's three standard ones.
    """
    out = run_lean(HEADER + code.strip() + f"\n\n#print axioms {theorem}\n", timeout)
    errors = [m.text for m in out.messages if m.severity == "error"]
    if any(m.kind == "hasSorry" for m in out.messages):
        errors.append("proof uses sorry")
    if out.timed_out:
        errors.append(f"Lean timed out after {timeout}s")

    axioms = []
    report = [m.text for m in out.messages if m.text.startswith(f"'{theorem}' ")]
    if not report:
        errors.append(f"no axiom report for {theorem}")
    elif "depends on axioms:" in report[0]:
        inside = report[0].split("[", 1)[1].rsplit("]", 1)[0]
        axioms = [a.strip() for a in inside.split(",") if a.strip()]
    extra = [a for a in axioms if a not in ALLOWED_AXIOMS]
    if extra:
        errors.append(f"uses non-standard axioms: {extra}")
    return FinalResult(ok=not errors, errors=errors, axioms=axioms)

    What changed: Decl has a new field context: str = "", and render wraps the candidate in namespace name ... end name when there is a context. A Lean namespace is a name prefix: a lemma key written inside namespace plan_0 is really plan_0.key, so two plans can both call a lemma key in the same file without clashing. Everything stays inside the candidate's line range, so an error in one plan's lemmas counts against that plan only.

  2. Add a test for the new rendering. Replace the whole file:

    tests/test_lean.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/lean.py.

The ones marked `lean` really run Lean (about 25 seconds each).
Skip them with:  uv run pytest -m "not lean"
"""

import pytest

from mathfinder.lean import (
    Decl,
    check_decls,
    indent,
    parse_messages,
    run_lean,
    statement_errors,
    verify_final,
)

# ---------- fast tests: no Lean needed ----------


def test_indent_adds_two_spaces_per_line():
    assert indent("simp\nring") == "  simp\n  ring"


def test_render_wraps_heartbeats_and_indents_proof():
    text = Decl("t", "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n", "simp").render(heartbeats=1000)
    assert text == (
        "-- t\n"
        "set_option maxHeartbeats 1000 in\n"
        "example (n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n := by\n"
        "  simp\n"
    )


def test_render_with_context_uses_its_own_namespace():
    text = Decl("plan_0", ": True", "trivial", context="theorem key : True := trivial").render(1000)
    assert text == (
        "-- plan_0\n"
        "namespace plan_0\n\n"
        "theorem key : True := trivial\n\n"
        "set_option maxHeartbeats 1000 in\n"
        "example : True := by\n"
        "  trivial\n\n"
        "end plan_0\n"
    )


def test_parse_messages_reads_lean_json():
    line = (
        '{"caption":"","data":"declaration uses `sorry`","fileName":"x.lean",'
        '"kind":"hasSorry","pos":{"column":8,"line":14},"severity":"warning"}'
    )
    [msg] = parse_messages("some non-json noise\n" + line)
    assert (msg.line, msg.severity, msg.kind) == (14, "warning", "hasSorry")


# ---------- slow tests: these run Lean ----------


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def batch():
    """One Lean run that checks five different kinds of candidate at once."""
    preamble = "theorem helper (n : ℕ) : n * 1 = n := by simp"
    decls = [
        Decl("good", "(a b : ℝ) : a ^ 2 + b ^ 2 ≥ 2 * a * b", "nlinarith [sq_nonneg (a - b)]"),
        Decl("wrong", "(n : ℕ) : n + 1 > n + 2", "omega"),
        Decl("holey", "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n", "sorry"),
        Decl("nonsense", "(n : ℕ) : n = undefined_thing", "simp"),
        Decl("uses_helper", "(m : ℕ) : m * 1 = m", "exact helper m"),
    ]
    return check_decls(decls, preamble=preamble)


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_batch_correct_proof_passes(batch):
    assert batch["good"].ok
    assert batch["good"].errors == []


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_batch_wrong_proof_fails_with_lean_error(batch):
    assert not batch["wrong"].ok
    assert "omega could not prove the goal" in batch["wrong"].errors[0]


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_batch_sorry_is_not_a_proof(batch):
    assert not batch["holey"].ok
    assert batch["holey"].used_sorry


@pytest.mark.lean

    assert not batch["nonsense"].ok
    assert any("undefined_thing" in e for e in batch["nonsense"].errors)


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_batch_can_use_preamble_lemmas(batch):
    assert batch["uses_helper"].ok


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_candidates_cannot_cite_each_other():
    # "first" fails. If candidates were theorems, "second" could cite it as proved.
    decls = [
        Decl("first", "(n : ℕ) : n = n + 1", "simp"),
        Decl("second", "(n : ℕ) : n = n + 1", "exact?"),
    ]
    results = check_decls(decls)
    assert not results["first"].ok
    assert not results["second"].ok


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_statement_errors_accepts_good_statement():
    assert statement_errors("(n : ℕ) : n ^ 3 - n ≥ 0") == []


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_verify_final_accepts_real_proof():
    code = "theorem amgm2 (a b : ℝ) : a ^ 2 + b ^ 2 ≥ 2 * a * b := by\n  nlinarith [sq_nonneg (a - b)]"
    result = verify_final(code, "amgm2")
    assert result.ok, result.errors
    assert set(result.axioms) <= {"propext", "Classical.choice", "Quot.sound"}


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_verify_final_rejects_sorry():
    result = verify_final("theorem t : 1 = 2 := by\n  sorry", "t")
    assert not result.ok
    assert "proof uses sorry" in result.errors


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_verify_final_rejects_custom_axiom():
    code = "axiom cheat : False\n\ntheorem t : 1 = 2 := cheat.elim"
    result = verify_final(code, "t")
    assert not result.ok
    assert "cheat" in result.axioms
