    assert out.timed_out

    What changed: one new test, test_render_with_context_uses_its_own_namespace.

  3. Create the planner.

    mathfinder/planner.py
    """The planner (the "policy"): ask Claude for proof PLANS, not whole proofs.

A plan is a short informal sketch, a list of small lemmas, and a main proof that
puts the lemmas together. Small lemmas are much easier to prove one at a time
(often the hammer does it with no AI at all), and when one fails we know exactly
which step of the idea is broken.
"""

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from mathfinder import llm

NAME_RE = re.compile(r"^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_']*$")  # a valid simple Lean name


@dataclass
class Lemma:
    name: str  # Lean name, e.g. "sq_sub_nonneg"
    statement: str  # Lean, without the name: "(a b : ℝ) : 0 ≤ (a - b) ^ 2"
    informal: str  # one sentence of math, for people
    status: str = "open"  # open | proved | refuted | stuck
    proof: str = ""  # tactic proof, once found
    feedback: str = ""  # why it failed (Lean error, counterexample, ...)


@dataclass
class Plan:
    sketch: str  # the idea in 1-3 sentences
    lemmas: list[Lemma]
    main_proof: str  # tactic proof of the theorem; may use the lemmas by name
    feedback: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)  # what the critic said


SYSTEM = """You are an expert in Lean 4 and Mathlib, planning formal proofs.
You never write a whole long proof at once. You split the theorem into a few small lemmas
that are each easy to prove on their own, then prove the theorem from those lemmas.

Rules:
- Lean 4 syntax and current Mathlib names only (no Lean 3).
- A lemma "statement" is everything that would come after `theorem name`:
  binders and hypotheses, then a colon, then the claim. Example: "(a b : ℝ) : 0 ≤ (a - b) ^ 2".
- Every lemma must be TRUE as stated, and as small as possible (ideally one tactic).
- A lemma may use earlier lemmas in the list.
- "main_proof" is a tactic proof of the theorem (no `by`), and uses the lemmas by name.
  Example: "nlinarith [sq_sub_nonneg a b]".
- Lemma names: snake_case, letters/digits/underscores only.
- If the theorem is easy, a plan may have zero lemmas.
- Never use sorry, admit or axiom.
- Make the plans genuinely different from each other (different ideas, not reworded copies).
- If the user gave hints, follow them: they come from a person working with you.

Reply with ONLY a JSON object inside a ```json block:
{"plans": [{"sketch": "...", "lemmas": [{"name": "...", "statement": "...", "informal": "..."}],
            "main_proof": "..."}]}"""


def build_prompt(theorem: str, n: int, hints: list[str], feedback: str) -> str:
    parts = [f"Theorem to prove (Lean statement):\n  theorem main {theorem}", f"Give {n} different plans."]
    if hints:
        parts.append("Hints from the user (highest priority, follow them):\n" + "\n".join(f"- {h}" for h in hints))
    if feedback:
        parts.append(f"What happened to earlier plans (avoid these mistakes):\n{feedback}")
    return "\n\n".join(parts)


def parse_plans(data) -> list[Plan]:
    """Turn the model's JSON into Plan objects, dropping any plan that is malformed."""
    plans = []
    for raw in data.get("plans", []) if isinstance(data, dict) else []:
        try:
            lemmas = [
                Lemma(name=l["name"].strip(), statement=l["statement"].strip(), informal=l.get("informal", ""))
                for l in raw.get("lemmas", [])
            ]
            plan = Plan(sketch=raw.get("sketch", ""), lemmas=lemmas, main_proof=raw["main_proof"].strip())
        except (KeyError, AttributeError, TypeError):
            continue  # missing field or wrong type: skip this plan
        names = [l.name for l in lemmas]
        if all(NAME_RE.match(n) for n in names) and len(set(names)) == len(names) and plan.main_proof:
            plans.append(plan)
    return plans


def propose_plans(theorem: str, n: int = 2, hints: list[str] = (), feedback: str = "") -> list[Plan]:
    """Ask Claude for `n` plans to prove `theorem` (a Lean statement without a name)."""
    data = llm.complete_json(SYSTEM, build_prompt(theorem, n, list(hints), feedback))
    return parse_plans(data)

    Lemma and Plan are the records the whole search passes around. status starts as "open" and later becomes proved, refuted or stuck.

    SYSTEM is the standing instruction. It fixes the exact format we can feed straight into Lean: a lemma "statement" is what comes after theorem name, which is the same format Decl uses. It also asks for genuinely different plans, because two rewordings of one idea fail together.

    build_prompt adds the user's hints under a "highest priority" heading, and any feedback from earlier failed plans. This is the hook for working together (M7) and for revising plans (M4c).

    parse_plans never trusts the model's JSON. Each plan goes through try/except (KeyError, AttributeError, TypeError), which skips plans with missing fields or wrong types, and plans with invalid or duplicated lemma names are dropped. NAME_RE is a regular expression for a simple Lean name: a letter or underscore, then letters, digits, _ or '.

  4. Create the critic (just its first check for now).

    mathfinder/critic.py
    """The critic: let Lean judge a plan before anyone tries to prove its lemmas.

Check 1 (this file, so far): does the plan even fit together? We put each lemma in
as a theorem with `sorry` as its proof, then check the main proof. If that
fails, the plan is broken no matter how the lemmas get proved: a lemma
statement doesn't type-check, a name is wrong, or the lemmas aren't enough.
All plans are checked in ONE Lean run.
"""

from mathfinder.lean import Decl, check_decls
from mathfinder.planner import Plan

FORBIDDEN = ("sorry", "admit", "axiom")


def lemma_context(plan: Plan) -> str:
    """The plan's lemmas as Lean theorems with `sorry` proofs: "pretend these are proved"."""
    return "\n\n".join(f"theorem {l.name} {l.statement} := by\n  sorry" for l in plan.lemmas)


def check_skeletons(theorem: str, plans: list[Plan]) -> list[list[str]]:
    """For each plan, the list of problems with its skeleton (empty list = the plan fits together)."""
    problems = [[] for _ in plans]
    decls = []
    for i, plan in enumerate(plans):
        cheat = [w for w in FORBIDDEN if w in plan.main_proof]
        if cheat:
            problems[i].append(f"main proof uses forbidden {cheat}")
            continue
        decls.append(Decl(f"plan_{i}", theorem, plan.main_proof, context=lemma_context(plan)))

    if decls:
        results = check_decls(decls)
        for i in range(len(plans)):
            result = results.get(f"plan_{i}")
            if result is not None:
                # The lemmas use sorry on purpose, so only real errors count here.
                problems[i].extend(result.errors)

    for plan, found in zip(plans, problems):
        if found:
            plan.feedback.append("skeleton does not compile: " + " | ".join(e[:300] for e in found))
    return problems

    lemma_context turns a plan's lemmas into Lean theorems proved by sorry. check_skeletons makes one Decl per plan: the statement is the main theorem, the proof is the plan's main proof, and the context is its sorried lemmas. Then all plans are checked in one Lean run. The lemmas use sorry on purpose, so here we only count real errors, which is why the main proof is first scanned for sorry/admit/axiom as plain text. zip(plans, problems) walks the two lists side by side.

  5. Create the tests.

    tests/test_planner.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/planner.py and the skeleton check in mathfinder/critic.py."""

import pytest

from mathfinder import llm
from mathfinder.critic import check_skeletons
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma, Plan, build_prompt, parse_plans, propose_plans

AMGM = "(a b : ℝ) : a ^ 2 + b ^ 2 ≥ 2 * a * b"

GOOD_JSON = """Here you go:
```json
{"plans": [
  {"sketch": "Square of a difference is nonnegative.",
   "lemmas": [{"name": "key", "statement": "(a b : ℝ) : 0 ≤ (a - b) ^ 2", "informal": "squares are nonnegative"}],
   "main_proof": "nlinarith [key a b]"},
  {"sketch": "Bad names.",
   "lemmas": [{"name": "not a name!", "statement": ": True", "informal": ""}],
   "main_proof": "trivial"},
  {"sketch": "Missing main proof.", "lemmas": []}
]}
```"""


# ---------- fast tests ----------


def test_parse_plans_keeps_only_wellformed_plans():
    plans = parse_plans(llm.extract_json(GOOD_JSON))
    assert len(plans) == 1
    assert plans[0].lemmas[0].name == "key"
    assert plans[0].main_proof == "nlinarith [key a b]"


def test_parse_plans_rejects_duplicate_lemma_names():
    data = {"plans": [{"sketch": "", "main_proof": "simp", "lemmas": [
        {"name": "a", "statement": ": True"}, {"name": "a", "statement": ": True"}]}]}
    assert parse_plans(data) == []


def test_prompt_puts_user_hints_first_class():
    prompt = build_prompt(AMGM, 2, ["use (a-b)^2 ≥ 0"], "plan 1 failed: lemma false")
    assert "theorem main (a b : ℝ)" in prompt
    assert "- use (a-b)^2 ≥ 0" in prompt
    assert "plan 1 failed" in prompt


def test_propose_plans_with_fake_llm(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", lambda system, prompt: GOOD_JSON)
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
    plans = propose_plans(AMGM)
    assert [p.sketch for p in plans] == ["Square of a difference is nonnegative."]


def test_sorry_in_main_proof_is_rejected_without_lean():
    plan = Plan("cheat", [], "sorry")
    assert "forbidden" in check_skeletons(AMGM, [plan])[0][0]


# ---------- Lean tests ----------


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_skeleton_check_in_one_lean_run():
    key = Lemma("key", "(a b : ℝ) : 0 ≤ (a - b) ^ 2", "")
    good = Plan("works", [key], "nlinarith [key a b]")
    wrong_name = Plan("cites a lemma that isn't there", [key], "nlinarith [nope a b]")
    bad_statement = Plan("lemma doesn't type-check", [Lemma("key", "(a : ℝ) : a = undefined_x", "")], "simp")
    not_enough = Plan("lemma too weak", [Lemma("key", "(a : ℝ) : 0 ≤ a ^ 2", "")], "linarith [key a]")

    problems = check_skeletons(AMGM, [good, wrong_name, bad_statement, not_enough])

    assert problems[0] == []  # both plans name their lemma "key": namespaces keep them apart
    assert any("nope" in p for p in problems[1])
    assert any("undefined_x" in p for p in problems[2])
    assert problems[3] != []
    assert good.feedback == [] and wrong_name.feedback != []


# ---------- real Claude + Lean ----------


@pytest.mark.llm
def test_real_planner_produces_a_plan_that_fits_together():
    plans = propose_plans(AMGM, n=2)
    assert plans, "planner returned no well-formed plans"
    problems = check_skeletons(AMGM, plans)
    assert any(p == [] for p in problems), problems

    The Lean test checks four hand-written plans in one run: one that works, one citing a lemma that doesn't exist, one whose lemma statement doesn't type-check, and one whose lemma is too weak for the main proof. The llm test asks the real Claude for plans and checks that at least one fits together.

Checkpoint

What we got (yours will be worded differently, since the model is creative):

PLAN: Use the nonnegativity of (a - b)^2 and close with nlinarith.
   main: nlinarith [sq_nonneg (a - b)]
   skeleton OK
PLAN: Prove a lemma that a^2 + b^2 - 2ab equals (a-b)^2, which is nonnegative, then conclude by linarith.
   lemma diff_eq_sq : (a b : ℝ) : a ^ 2 + b ^ 2 - 2 * a * b = (a - b) ^ 2
   lemma diff_nonneg : (a b : ℝ) : 0 ≤ a ^ 2 + b ^ 2 - 2 * a * b
   main: have h := diff_nonneg a b
linarith
   skeleton OK

"skeleton OK" doesn't mean proved yet: the lemmas are still sorried. Proving them is M4b.

Fast tests:

uv run pytest -q -m "not lean and not llm"
........................                                                 [100%]
24 passed, 18 deselected in 0.03s

Common errors

  • The planner returns []: every plan was malformed. Print llm.complete(SYSTEM, build_prompt(...)) to see the raw answer.
  • Every skeleton fails with unknown identifier for its own lemmas: your render puts the lemmas after the example, or outside the namespace.
  • LLMError: invalid JSON twice: the model wrapped JSON in prose with stray braces. It's rare; rerun.

M4b: The prover

What and why. The planner breaks a theorem into lemmas. The prover's job is to get one lemma proved, using the cheapest tool that works. It climbs a ladder: cache (proved this before? free), then the hammer (automatic tactics, no AI), then Claude (several proofs at once, all checked in one Lean run), then repair rounds: Claude sees its failed proofs together with Lean's exact error messages and fixes them. This feedback loop is the main reason pairing an LLM with Lean works: the model rarely gets a proof right the first time, but Lean says precisely what's wrong. If the hammer finds a counterexample, the lemma is refuted and we stop, because no amount of effort proves a false statement.

  1. Ignore the cache folder in git. Replace the whole file:

    .gitignore
    .venv/
__pycache__/
*.pyc
.pytest_cache/
runs/
lean_env/.lake/
lean_env/.mf_tmp/
cache_get.log
cache/

    What changed: the last line, cache/. The cache is generated data, so it's rebuilt automatically wherever you run the project.

  2. Create the prover.

    mathfinder/prover.py
    """The prover: get ONE lemma proved, as cheaply as possible.

  1. cache   - proved this exact lemma before? reuse it, free
  2. hammer  - a dozen automatic tactics in one Lean run, no AI
               (also finds counterexamples: then the lemma is false, stop)
  3. LLM     - ask Claude for several proofs, check them all in ONE Lean run
  4. repair  - show Claude its failed proofs WITH Lean's error messages, ask for fixes

The lemma ends up "proved" (with a proof), "refuted" (with a counterexample) or
"stuck" (with the most useful Lean error, so the planner can rethink).
"""

import json

from mathfinder import PROJECT_ROOT, llm
from mathfinder.hammer import hammer
from mathfinder.lean import Decl, check_decls
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma

CACHE_FILE = PROJECT_ROOT / "cache" / "lemmas.json"
ATTEMPTS = 4  # proofs asked for per LLM call
REPAIR_ROUNDS = 2  # extra rounds that see Lean's errors
MAX_ERROR = 600  # characters of each Lean error shown to the model

SYSTEM = """You are an expert in Lean 4 and Mathlib. You write tactic proofs that compile.
Rules:
- Lean 4 syntax, current Mathlib lemma names (e.g. Finset.sum_range_succ, sq_nonneg, Nat.succ_le_iff).
- Write ONLY the tactic script that goes after `:= by` (do not write `by` or the statement).
- Never use sorry, admit, or axiom.
- You may use the earlier lemmas listed, by name.
- Each proof should be a genuinely different approach.
Reply with ONLY a JSON object inside a ```json block: {"proofs": ["<tactic script>", ...]}"""


# ---------- cache ----------


def cache_key(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma]) -> str:
    """Same statement AND same available lemmas = same problem. Whitespace doesn't matter."""
    context = " ; ".join(f"{l.name} {l.statement}" for l in earlier)
    return " ".join(f"{context} ⊢ {lemma.statement}".split())


def load_cache() -> dict:
    if CACHE_FILE.exists():
        return json.loads(CACHE_FILE.read_text())
    return {}


def save_to_cache(key: str, proof: str) -> None:
    cache = load_cache()
    cache[key] = proof
    CACHE_FILE.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    CACHE_FILE.write_text(json.dumps(cache, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))


# ---------- talking to the model ----------


def preamble_for(earlier: list[Lemma]) -> str:
    """Earlier lemmas the proof may use: real proofs where we have them, sorry otherwise."""
    parts = []
    for l in earlier:
        proof = l.proof if l.status == "proved" else "sorry"
        body = "\n".join("  " + line for line in proof.split("\n"))
        parts.append(f"theorem {l.name} {l.statement} := by\n{body}")
    return "\n\n".join(parts)


def build_prompt(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma], failures: list[tuple[str, str]] = ()) -> str:
    parts = [f"Prove this lemma:\n  theorem {lemma.name} {lemma.statement}"]
    if lemma.informal:
        parts.append(f"In words: {lemma.informal}")
    if earlier:
        parts.append("Earlier lemmas you may use:\n" + "\n".join(f"  theorem {l.name} {l.statement}" for l in earlier))
    for proof, error in failures:
        parts.append(f"This attempt FAILED:\n{proof}\nLean said:\n{error[:MAX_ERROR]}")
    if failures:
        parts.append("Fix these mistakes. Use the error messages.")
    parts.append(f"Give {ATTEMPTS} different proofs.")
    return "\n\n".join(parts)


def parse_proofs(data) -> list[str]:
    """Keep only usable proof strings; strip a leading `by` the model may add."""
    proofs = []
    raw = data.get("proofs", []) if isinstance(data, dict) else []
    for p in raw:
        if not isinstance(p, str) or not p.strip():
            continue
        p = p.strip()
        if p.startswith("by"):
            p = p[2:].strip()
        if any(word in p for word in ("sorry", "admit", "axiom")):
            continue
        proofs.append(p)
    return proofs


def ask_for_proofs(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma], failures=()) -> list[str]:
    try:
        return parse_proofs(llm.complete_json(SYSTEM, build_prompt(lemma, earlier, failures)))
    except llm.LLMError:
        return []  # one bad model answer shouldn't end the search


def try_proofs(lemma: Lemma, proofs: list[str], preamble: str):
    """Check all proofs in one Lean run. Returns (working proof or None, [(proof, error), ...])."""
    decls = [Decl(f"attempt_{i}", lemma.statement, p) for i, p in enumerate(proofs)]
    results = check_decls(decls, preamble)
    failures = []
    for i, p in enumerate(proofs):
        result = results[f"attempt_{i}"]
        if result.ok:
            return p, []
        failures.append((p, "\n".join(result.errors) or "proof uses sorry"))
    return None, failures


# ---------- the whole pipeline ----------


def prove_lemma(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma] = (), use_cache: bool = True) -> Lemma:
    """Try to prove `lemma` (which may use the `earlier` lemmas). Updates and returns it."""
    earlier = list(earlier)
    key = cache_key(lemma, earlier)
    if use_cache and key in load_cache():
        lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", load_cache()[key], "from cache"
        return lemma

    preamble = preamble_for(earlier)
    hit = hammer(lemma.statement, preamble)
    if hit.proved:
        lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", hit.proof, f"hammer: {hit.tactic}"
        save_to_cache(key, hit.proof)
        return lemma
    if hit.counterexample:
        lemma.status, lemma.feedback = "refuted", f"counterexample: {hit.counterexample}"
        return lemma

    failures = []
    for round_number in range(1 + REPAIR_ROUNDS):
        proofs = ask_for_proofs(lemma, earlier, failures[:2])  # show the model at most 2 failures
        if not proofs:
            continue
        found, failures = try_proofs(lemma, proofs, preamble)
        if found:
            how = "LLM" if round_number == 0 else f"LLM after {round_number} repair round(s)"
            lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", found, how
            save_to_cache(key, found)
            return lemma

    lemma.status = "stuck"
    lemma.feedback = failures[0][1][:MAX_ERROR] if failures else "no usable proof attempts"
    return lemma

    The cache is a JSON file mapping a key to a proof. The key is the lemma statement plus the earlier lemmas it can see, because a proof like exact key a b only works when a lemma named key exists. " ".join(text.split()) collapses all whitespace, so two spellings that differ only in spaces share one entry. ensure_ascii=False keeps symbols like ℕ readable in the file instead of ℕ.

    preamble_for puts the earlier lemmas in front of the attempts: with their real proof if we have one, otherwise with sorry. Using an unproved earlier lemma is fine here, because each lemma gets proved on its own, and the final check (M4c) only accepts the theorem once every lemma has a real proof.

    build_prompt shows the lemma, what it means in words, the lemmas it may use, and on repair rounds each failed attempt next to Lean's error (cut to MAX_ERROR characters to keep the prompt small). parse_proofs never trusts the model: it drops non-strings, empty strings and anything containing sorry/admit/axiom, and removes a leading by (we add our own).

    try_proofs turns every proof into a candidate and checks them all in one Lean run. It returns the first one that works, or the list of (proof, error) pairs to feed the next repair round.

    prove_lemma is the ladder itself. for round_number in range(1 + REPAIR_ROUNDS) gives one first try plus two repair rounds. failures[:2] shows the model at most two failures, since more makes the prompt long without helping much. A stuck lemma keeps the most useful Lean error in feedback, so the planner can see why it got stuck.

  3. Create the tests.

    tests/test_prover.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/prover.py.

The Lean tests use a FAKE model with scripted answers, so they're free and
repeatable while still checking every proof with real Lean.
"""

import json

import pytest

from mathfinder import llm, prover
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma

ODD_SUM = "(n : ℕ) : ∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2"  # needs induction: the hammer can't
GOOD = "induction n with\n| zero => simp\n| succ k ih =>\n  rw [Finset.sum_range_succ, ih]\n  ring"
WRONG = "simp"


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def temp_cache(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """Every test gets its own empty cache file, so tests can't affect each other."""
    monkeypatch.setattr(prover, "CACHE_FILE", tmp_path / "lemmas.json")


@pytest.fixture
def scripted_model(monkeypatch):
    """A fake model that gives the scripted proof lists in order and records each prompt."""

    def install(*answers):
        prompts = []

        def fake(system, prompt):
            prompts.append(prompt)
            assert len(prompts) <= len(answers), "the model was called more times than expected"
            proofs = answers[len(prompts) - 1]
            return "```json\n" + json.dumps({"proofs": proofs}) + "\n```"

        monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", fake)
        monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
        return prompts

    return install


# ---------- fast tests ----------


def test_parse_proofs_cleans_model_output():
    data = {"proofs": ["by simp", "  ring  ", "sorry", 42, "", "exact foo"]}
    assert prover.parse_proofs(data) == ["simp", "ring", "exact foo"]


def test_cache_key_ignores_whitespace_but_not_context():
    a = Lemma("x", "(n : ℕ) :  n = n", "")
    b = Lemma("y", "(n : ℕ) : n = n", "")
    helper = Lemma("h", ": True", "")
    assert prover.cache_key(a, []) == prover.cache_key(b, [])
    assert prover.cache_key(a, []) != prover.cache_key(a, [helper])


def test_repair_prompt_shows_lean_errors():
    lemma = Lemma("odd_sum", ODD_SUM, "sum of first n odd numbers")
    prompt = prover.build_prompt(lemma, [], [("simp", "simp made no progress")])
    assert "FAILED" in prompt and "simp made no progress" in prompt


def test_preamble_uses_real_proofs_when_known():
    done = Lemma("a", ": 1 = 1", "", status="proved", proof="rfl")
    open_ = Lemma("b", ": 2 = 2", "")
    assert prover.preamble_for([done, open_]) == (
        "theorem a : 1 = 1 := by\n  rfl\n\ntheorem b : 2 = 2 := by\n  sorry"
    )


# ---------- Lean tests (fake model, real Lean) ----------


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_false_lemma_is_refuted_without_asking_the_model(scripted_model):
    prompts = scripted_model()  # no answers: any model call fails the test
    lemma = prover.prove_lemma(Lemma("bad", "(n : ℕ) : n ^ 2 ≤ 2 * n + 3", ""))
    assert lemma.status == "refuted"
    assert "counterexample" in lemma.feedback
    assert prompts == []


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_repair_round_fixes_a_wrong_proof_then_cache_reuses_it(scripted_model):
    prompts = scripted_model([WRONG], [GOOD])
    lemma = prover.prove_lemma(Lemma("odd_sum", ODD_SUM, "sum of the first n odd numbers is n^2"))
    assert lemma.status == "proved", lemma.feedback
    assert lemma.proof == GOOD
    assert lemma.feedback == "LLM after 1 repair round(s)"
    assert "Lean said" in prompts[1]  # the second call saw the real Lean error

    again = prover.prove_lemma(Lemma("odd_sum_again", ODD_SUM, ""))
    assert again.status == "proved" and again.feedback == "from cache"
    assert len(prompts) == 2  # no new model call


# ---------- real Claude + Lean ----------


@pytest.mark.llm
def test_real_model_proves_a_lemma_the_hammer_cannot():
    lemma = prover.prove_lemma(Lemma("odd_sum", ODD_SUM, "the sum of the first n odd numbers is n^2"))
    assert lemma.status == "proved", lemma.feedback
    assert lemma.feedback.startswith("LLM")

    autouse=True applies the temp_cache fixture to every test in the file automatically. tmp_path is a built-in pytest fixture: a fresh empty folder per test.

    scripted_model is a fake Claude that gives pre-written answers in order: first a wrong proof (simp), then the right one. That lets a free, repeatable test check the whole repair loop with real Lean: the first proof fails, the second prompt contains Lean's error, the second proof passes, and asking again hits the cache without calling the model. The refutation test gives the fake model no answers, so any model call fails the test. That proves false lemmas never cost an LLM call.

    The induction proof in GOOD is worth reading. induction n with splits into the case zero (empty sum = 0 = 0², simp) and succ k ih, where ih is the statement for k. Finset.sum_range_succ peels off the last term, ih replaces the rest with k ^ 2, and ring checks k² + (2k + 1) = (k + 1)².

  4. Run the fast tests, then the Lean tests (about a minute):

    uv run pytest -q -m "not lean and not llm"
uv run pytest -q tests/test_prover.py

  5. Let the real Claude prove a lemma the hammer can't (about 40 seconds):

    uv run pytest -q -m llm tests/test_prover.py

Checkpoint

uv run python -c "
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma
from mathfinder.prover import prove_lemma
lemma = prove_lemma(Lemma('odd_sum', '(n : ℕ) : ∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2', 'sum of the first n odd numbers is n^2'))
print(lemma.status, '|', lemma.feedback)
print(lemma.proof)
"

What we got (about 40 s: the hammer fails, then Claude's first batch works):

proved | LLM
induction n with
| zero => simp
| succ k ih =>
  rw [Finset.sum_range_succ, ih]
  ring

Run the exact same command again. Now it's instant, and the first line says proved | from cache.

Fast tests:

............................                                             [100%]
28 passed, 21 deselected in 0.04s

Common errors

  • stuck with unknown identifier 'Finset.sum_range_succ'-style errors every time: the model used an old (Lean 3 / old Mathlib) name. The repair rounds usually fix this. If not, it's a sign the lemma should be split smaller.
  • A test fails with the model was called more times than expected: the code asked Claude when it shouldn't have (for example, the hammer didn't run first).
  • Stale results after you change a lemma's meaning but not its text: delete cache/lemmas.json.

M4c: The search loop and the command line

What and why. We now have all the pieces: a hammer, a planner, a critic and a prover. search.py runs them in a loop: (0) maybe automation proves the whole theorem; (1) the planner proposes plans; (2) the critic drops the ones that don't fit together; (3) the rest are tried cheapest first (fewest lemmas), each lemma going to the prover; (4) a plan with every lemma proved is assembled into one file and gets the strict final check; (5) everything that went wrong becomes feedback for the next round of planning. That last step is what makes it a search and not a lottery: each round knows which ideas failed and why. Finally, a command line lets you run it from the terminal.

Events

The search never prints anything itself. It reports what happens as events, small dictionaries like {"kind": "lemma_proved", "name": "odd_sum", ...}, passed to an on_event function you supply. The command line prints them; later the website will stream the same events to your browser, and the trace logger will save them for training. One search, many watchers.

  1. Keep plan lemmas from clashing with Mathlib's names. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/prover.py
    """The prover: get ONE lemma proved, as cheaply as possible.

  1. cache   - proved this exact lemma before? reuse it, free
  2. hammer  - a dozen automatic tactics in one Lean run, no AI
               (also finds counterexamples: then the lemma is false, stop)
  3. LLM     - ask Claude for several proofs, check them all in ONE Lean run
  4. repair  - show Claude its failed proofs WITH Lean's error messages, ask for fixes

The lemma ends up "proved" (with a proof), "refuted" (with a counterexample) or
"stuck" (with the most useful Lean error, so the planner can rethink).
"""

import json

from mathfinder import PROJECT_ROOT, llm
from mathfinder.hammer import hammer
from mathfinder.lean import Decl, check_decls
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma

CACHE_FILE = PROJECT_ROOT / "cache" / "lemmas.json"
ATTEMPTS = 4  # proofs asked for per LLM call
REPAIR_ROUNDS = 2  # extra rounds that see Lean's errors
MAX_ERROR = 600  # characters of each Lean error shown to the model

SYSTEM = """You are an expert in Lean 4 and Mathlib. You write tactic proofs that compile.
Rules:
- Lean 4 syntax, current Mathlib lemma names (e.g. Finset.sum_range_succ, sq_nonneg, Nat.succ_le_iff).
- Write ONLY the tactic script that goes after `:= by` (do not write `by` or the statement).
- Never use sorry, admit, or axiom.
- You may use the earlier lemmas listed, by name.
- Each proof should be a genuinely different approach.
Reply with ONLY a JSON object inside a ```json block: {"proofs": ["<tactic script>", ...]}"""


# ---------- cache ----------


def cache_key(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma]) -> str:
    """Same statement AND same available lemmas = same problem. Whitespace doesn't matter."""
    context = " ; ".join(f"{l.name} {l.statement}" for l in earlier)
    return " ".join(f"{context} ⊢ {lemma.statement}".split())


def load_cache() -> dict:
    if CACHE_FILE.exists():
        return json.loads(CACHE_FILE.read_text())
    return {}


def save_to_cache(key: str, proof: str) -> None:
    cache = load_cache()
    cache[key] = proof
    CACHE_FILE.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    CACHE_FILE.write_text(json.dumps(cache, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))


# ---------- talking to the model ----------


def preamble_for(earlier: list[Lemma]) -> str:
    """Earlier lemmas the proof may use: real proofs where we have them, sorry otherwise.

    They go inside `namespace MathFinder` (left open, which Lean allows at the end of
    a file), so a plan lemma called e.g. `sq_nonneg` can't clash with Mathlib's.
    """
    if not earlier:
        return ""
    parts = []
    for l in earlier:
        proof = l.proof if l.status == "proved" else "sorry"
        body = "\n".join("  " + line for line in proof.split("\n"))
        parts.append(f"theorem {l.name} {l.statement} := by\n{body}")
    return "namespace MathFinder\n\n" + "\n\n".join(parts)


def build_prompt(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma], failures: list[tuple[str, str]] = ()) -> str:
    parts = [f"Prove this lemma:\n  theorem {lemma.name} {lemma.statement}"]
    if lemma.informal:
        parts.append(f"In words: {lemma.informal}")
    if earlier:
        parts.append("Earlier lemmas you may use:\n" + "\n".join(f"  theorem {l.name} {l.statement}" for l in earlier))
    for proof, error in failures:
        parts.append(f"This attempt FAILED:\n{proof}\nLean said:\n{error[:MAX_ERROR]}")
    if failures:
        parts.append("Fix these mistakes. Use the error messages.")
    parts.append(f"Give {ATTEMPTS} different proofs.")
    return "\n\n".join(parts)


def parse_proofs(data) -> list[str]:
    """Keep only usable proof strings; strip a leading `by` the model may add."""
    proofs = []
    raw = data.get("proofs", []) if isinstance(data, dict) else []
    for p in raw:
        if not isinstance(p, str) or not p.strip():
            continue
        p = p.strip()
        if p.startswith("by"):
            p = p[2:].strip()
        if any(word in p for word in ("sorry", "admit", "axiom")):
            continue
        proofs.append(p)
    return proofs


def ask_for_proofs(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma], failures=()) -> list[str]:
    try:
        return parse_proofs(llm.complete_json(SYSTEM, build_prompt(lemma, earlier, failures)))
    except llm.LLMError:
        return []  # one bad model answer shouldn't end the search


def try_proofs(lemma: Lemma, proofs: list[str], preamble: str):
    """Check all proofs in one Lean run. Returns (working proof or None, [(proof, error), ...])."""
    decls = [Decl(f"attempt_{i}", lemma.statement, p) for i, p in enumerate(proofs)]
    results = check_decls(decls, preamble)
    failures = []
    for i, p in enumerate(proofs):
        result = results[f"attempt_{i}"]
        if result.ok:
            return p, []
        failures.append((p, "\n".join(result.errors) or "proof uses sorry"))
    return None, failures


# ---------- the whole pipeline ----------


def prove_lemma(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma] = (), use_cache: bool = True) -> Lemma:
    """Try to prove `lemma` (which may use the `earlier` lemmas). Updates and returns it."""
    earlier = list(earlier)
    key = cache_key(lemma, earlier)
    if use_cache and key in load_cache():
        lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", load_cache()[key], "from cache"
        return lemma

    preamble = preamble_for(earlier)
    hit = hammer(lemma.statement, preamble)
    if hit.proved:
        lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", hit.proof, f"hammer: {hit.tactic}"
        save_to_cache(key, hit.proof)
        return lemma
    if hit.counterexample:
        lemma.status, lemma.feedback = "refuted", f"counterexample: {hit.counterexample}"
        return lemma

    failures = []
    for round_number in range(1 + REPAIR_ROUNDS):
        proofs = ask_for_proofs(lemma, earlier, failures[:2])  # show the model at most 2 failures
        if not proofs:
            continue
        found, failures = try_proofs(lemma, proofs, preamble)
        if found:
            how = "LLM" if round_number == 0 else f"LLM after {round_number} repair round(s)"
            lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", found, how
            save_to_cache(key, found)
            return lemma

    lemma.status = "stuck"
    lemma.feedback = failures[0][1][:MAX_ERROR] if failures else "no usable proof attempts"
    return lemma

    What changed: only preamble_for. The earlier lemmas now go inside namespace MathFinder. Without it, a plan lemma the model happens to call sq_nonneg would collide with Mathlib's sq_nonneg ("already declared") and every attempt would fail. Lean allows a namespace to stay open at the end of a file, so the candidates that follow are inside it too and can use the lemmas by their short names.

  2. Update the prover test for the new preamble. Replace the whole file:

    tests/test_prover.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/prover.py.

The Lean tests use a FAKE model with scripted answers, so they're free and
repeatable while still checking every proof with real Lean.
"""

import json

import pytest

from mathfinder import llm, prover
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma

ODD_SUM = "(n : ℕ) : ∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2"  # needs induction: the hammer can't
GOOD = "induction n with\n| zero => simp\n| succ k ih =>\n  rw [Finset.sum_range_succ, ih]\n  ring"
WRONG = "simp"


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def temp_cache(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """Every test gets its own empty cache file, so tests can't affect each other."""
    monkeypatch.setattr(prover, "CACHE_FILE", tmp_path / "lemmas.json")


@pytest.fixture
def scripted_model(monkeypatch):
    """A fake model that gives the scripted proof lists in order and records each prompt."""

    def install(*answers):
        prompts = []

        def fake(system, prompt):
            prompts.append(prompt)
            assert len(prompts) <= len(answers), "the model was called more times than expected"
            proofs = answers[len(prompts) - 1]
            return "```json\n" + json.dumps({"proofs": proofs}) + "\n```"

        monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", fake)
        monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
        return prompts

    return install

    assert prover.cache_key(a, []) != prover.cache_key(a, [helper])

        "namespace MathFinder\n\n"
        "theorem a : 1 = 1 := by\n  rfl\n\ntheorem b : 2 = 2 := by\n  sorry"
    )
    assert prover.preamble_for([]) == ""

# ---------- real Claude + Lean ----------


@pytest.mark.llm

    What changed: test_preamble_uses_real_proofs_when_known expects the namespace MathFinder line, and checks that no earlier lemmas means an empty preamble.

  3. Create the search.

    mathfinder/search.py
    """The search: plan, check, prove, verify, and replan with what we learned.

  0. maybe the hammer proves the whole theorem directly (or refutes it)
  1. the planner proposes a few plans
  2. the critic throws out plans that don't fit together (one Lean run)
  3. remaining plans are tried cheapest-first (fewest lemmas); each lemma goes to
     the prover, and a refuted lemma kills its plan immediately
  4. a plan with every lemma proved is assembled into one file and checked by
     the strict final verification (no sorry, only standard axioms)
  5. otherwise everything that went wrong becomes feedback for the next round

Everything that happens is reported through `on_event`, so the command line,
the web page and the trace logger can all watch the same search.
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass

from mathfinder.critic import check_skeletons
from mathfinder.hammer import hammer
from mathfinder.lean import verify_final
from mathfinder.planner import Plan, propose_plans
from mathfinder.prover import prove_lemma

NAMESPACE = "MathFinder"


@dataclass
class SearchResult:
    proved: bool
    lean_code: str = ""  # the complete verified proof (everything after the file header)
    plan: Plan | None = None
    rounds: int = 0
    message: str = ""


def assemble(theorem: str, plan: Plan) -> str:
    """One Lean file: every lemma with its real proof, then the main theorem."""
    blocks = [f"namespace {NAMESPACE}"]
    for l in plan.lemmas:
        body = "\n".join("  " + line for line in l.proof.split("\n"))
        blocks.append(f"theorem {l.name} {l.statement} := by\n{body}")
    main_body = "\n".join("  " + line for line in plan.main_proof.split("\n"))
    blocks.append(f"theorem main {theorem} := by\n{main_body}")
    blocks.append(f"end {NAMESPACE}")
    return "\n\n".join(blocks) + "\n"


def score(plan: Plan) -> float:
    """Fraction of lemmas proved; a refuted lemma makes the whole plan worthless."""
    if any(l.status == "refuted" for l in plan.lemmas):
        return -1.0
    if not plan.lemmas:
        return 0.0
    return sum(l.status == "proved" for l in plan.lemmas) / len(plan.lemmas)


def summarize(plan: Plan) -> str:
    """What happened to one plan, written for the planner's next round."""
    lines = [f"Plan: {plan.sketch}  (main proof: {plan.main_proof!r})"]
    lines += [f"  - {note}" for note in plan.feedback]
    for l in plan.lemmas:
        lines.append(f"  - lemma {l.name} {l.statement}: {l.status.upper()}  {l.feedback[:300]}")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def prove_plan(plan: Plan, emit) -> bool:
    """Prove the plan's lemmas in order. Stops at the first refuted lemma."""
    for i, lemma in enumerate(plan.lemmas):
        emit("lemma_start", name=lemma.name, statement=lemma.statement)
        prove_lemma(lemma, plan.lemmas[:i])
        emit("lemma_" + lemma.status, name=lemma.name, statement=lemma.statement, feedback=lemma.feedback)
        if lemma.status == "refuted":
            return False
    return all(l.status == "proved" for l in plan.lemmas)


def try_direct(theorem: str, emit) -> SearchResult | None:
    """Step 0: maybe automation proves (or refutes) the whole theorem."""
    hit = hammer(theorem)
    if hit.counterexample:
        emit("refuted", counterexample=hit.counterexample)
        return SearchResult(False, message=f"the statement is false: {hit.counterexample}")
    if hit.proved:
        plan = Plan(f"Automation: {hit.tactic}", [], hit.proof)
        code = assemble(theorem, plan)
        if verify_final(code, f"{NAMESPACE}.main").ok:
            emit("done", lean_code=code, plan=plan.sketch)
            return SearchResult(True, code, plan, 0, "proved directly by automation")
    return None


def search(theorem: str, hints=(), rounds: int = 3, plans_per_round: int = 2, on_event=None) -> SearchResult:
    """Search for a verified proof of `theorem` (a Lean statement without a name)."""

    def emit(kind, **data):
        if on_event:
            on_event({"kind": kind, **data})

    emit("start", theorem=theorem)
    direct = try_direct(theorem, emit)
    if direct:
        return direct

    feedback = []  # what went wrong so far, fed to the planner
    best = None
    for r in range(1, rounds + 1):
        emit("round", number=r)
        plans = propose_plans(theorem, plans_per_round, list(hints), "\n".join(feedback[-6:]))
        emit("plans", sketches=[p.sketch for p in plans])
        problems = check_skeletons(theorem, plans)

        fitting = []
        for plan, found in zip(plans, problems):
            if found:
                emit("plan_rejected", sketch=plan.sketch, reason=found[0][:300])
                feedback.append(summarize(plan))
            else:
                fitting.append(plan)

        for plan in sorted(fitting, key=lambda p: len(p.lemmas)):  # cheapest first
            emit("plan_start", sketch=plan.sketch, lemmas=[l.name for l in plan.lemmas])
            if prove_plan(plan, emit):
                code = assemble(theorem, plan)
                final = verify_final(code, f"{NAMESPACE}.main")
                if final.ok:
                    emit("done", lean_code=code, plan=plan.sketch)
                    return SearchResult(True, code, plan, r, "verified")
                plan.feedback.append("final check failed: " + " | ".join(final.errors)[:400])
            emit("plan_failed", sketch=plan.sketch, score=score(plan))
            feedback.append(summarize(plan))
            if best is None or score(plan) > score(best):
                best = plan

    emit("gave_up", best=best.sketch if best else None)
    message = f"no verified proof after {rounds} rounds"
    if best:
        message += f"; closest plan proved {score(best):.0%} of its lemmas"
    return SearchResult(False, plan=best, rounds=rounds, message=message)

    assemble writes the final file: every lemma with its real proof, then theorem main, all inside namespace MathFinder. That's why the final check asks about MathFinder.main, the theorem's full name.

    score is the fraction of a plan's lemmas that got proved, used to remember the closest plan. A refuted lemma means the plan's idea is wrong, so it scores -1.

    summarize turns a failed plan into text for the planner: each lemma with its status (PROVED / REFUTED / STUCK) and the reason. feedback[-6:] sends only the six most recent summaries, so the prompt doesn't grow forever.

    emit is a function defined inside search (a closure): it can use on_event from the enclosing function. **data collects keyword arguments into a dictionary, so emit("round", number=1) sends {"kind": "round", "number": 1}.

    sorted(fitting, key=lambda p: len(p.lemmas)) is the simple form of best-first search: try the plan that looks cheapest first. A lambda is a tiny unnamed function. SearchResult | None in a type hint means "a SearchResult, or nothing".

  4. Create the command line.

    mathfinder/cli.py
    """Command line:  uv run python -m mathfinder prove --lean "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"

(LaTeX input arrives in M5; for now you give the Lean statement directly.)
"""

import argparse
import sys

from mathfinder.search import search

SYMBOL = {"lemma_proved": "✓", "lemma_refuted": "✗", "lemma_stuck": "…"}


def print_event(event: dict) -> None:
    kind = event["kind"]
    if kind == "start":
        print(f"\nTheorem: {event['theorem']}")
    elif kind == "refuted":
        print(f"✗ The statement is FALSE. Counterexample: {event['counterexample']}")
    elif kind == "round":
        print(f"\n— round {event['number']}: asking the planner —")
    elif kind == "plan_rejected":
        print(f"  ✗ plan doesn't fit together: {event['sketch']}\n      {event['reason'][:160]}")
    elif kind == "plan_start":
        print(f"  ▶ plan: {event['sketch']}  (lemmas: {', '.join(event['lemmas']) or 'none'})")
    elif kind in SYMBOL:
        print(f"      {SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
    elif kind == "plan_failed":
        print(f"    plan failed (score {event['score']:.2f})")
    elif kind == "done":
        print(f"\n✓ VERIFIED by Lean. Plan: {event['plan']}\n")
        print(event["lean_code"])
    elif kind == "gave_up":
        print("\nNo verified proof found.")
    sys.stdout.flush()  # show progress immediately, even when output is piped to a file


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="mathfinder")
    commands = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    prove = commands.add_parser("prove", help="search for a proof")
    prove.add_argument("--lean", required=True, help='Lean statement without a name, e.g. "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"')
    prove.add_argument("--hint", action="append", default=[], help="a hint for the planner (repeatable)")
    prove.add_argument("--rounds", type=int, default=3)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    if args.command == "prove":
        result = search(args.lean, hints=args.hint, rounds=args.rounds, on_event=print_event)
        print(f"\nResult: {'PROVED' if result.proved else 'not proved'} ({result.message})")
        return 0 if result.proved else 1
    return 2

    argparse reads command line arguments. add_subparsers gives us sub-commands (prove now; bench and export later). action="append" lets you repeat --hint. main returns an exit code: 0 means success, so scripts can check whether the proof was found.

  5. Make the package runnable with python -m mathfinder.

    mathfinder/__main__.py
    """Lets you run the package as a program:  python -m mathfinder ..."""

import sys

from mathfinder.cli import main

sys.exit(main())

    When you run python -m somepackage, Python runs that package's __main__.py.

  6. Create the tests.

    tests/test_search.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/search.py and mathfinder/cli.py."""

import json

import pytest

from mathfinder import cli, llm, planner, prover
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma, Plan
from mathfinder.search import SearchResult, assemble, score, search

ODD_SUM = "(n : ℕ) : ∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2"
INDUCTION = "induction n with\n| zero => simp\n| succ k ih =>\n  rw [Finset.sum_range_succ, ih]\n  ring"


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def temp_cache(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(prover, "CACHE_FILE", tmp_path / "lemmas.json")


# ---------- fast tests ----------


def test_assemble_builds_one_namespaced_file():
    plan = Plan("idea", [Lemma("key", "(a : ℝ) : 0 ≤ a ^ 2", "", status="proved", proof="positivity")], "nlinarith [key a]")
    assert assemble("(a : ℝ) : a ^ 2 ≥ 0", plan) == (
        "namespace MathFinder\n\n"
        "theorem key (a : ℝ) : 0 ≤ a ^ 2 := by\n  positivity\n\n"
        "theorem main (a : ℝ) : a ^ 2 ≥ 0 := by\n  nlinarith [key a]\n\n"
        "end MathFinder\n"
    )


def test_score_is_fraction_proved_and_refuted_is_worst():
    proved = Lemma("a", ": True", "", status="proved")
    stuck = Lemma("b", ": True", "", status="stuck")
    refuted = Lemma("c", ": True", "", status="refuted")
    assert score(Plan("", [proved, stuck], "")) == 0.5
    assert score(Plan("", [proved, refuted], "")) == -1.0


def test_cli_passes_arguments_to_search(monkeypatch, capsys):
    seen = {}

    def fake_search(theorem, hints, rounds, on_event):
        seen.update(theorem=theorem, hints=hints, rounds=rounds)
        on_event({"kind": "start", "theorem": theorem})
        return SearchResult(True, message="verified")

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "search", fake_search)
    code = cli.main(["prove", "--lean", "(n : ℕ) : n = n", "--hint", "use rfl", "--rounds", "1"])
    assert code == 0
    assert seen == {"theorem": "(n : ℕ) : n = n", "hints": ["use rfl"], "rounds": 1}
    assert "Result: PROVED" in capsys.readouterr().out


# ---------- Lean tests ----------


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_false_theorem_is_refuted_before_any_planning(monkeypatch):
    def no_model(system, prompt):
        raise AssertionError("the model should not be called for a false statement")

    monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", no_model)
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
    events = []
    result = search("(n : ℕ) : n ^ 2 ≤ 2 * n + 3", on_event=events.append)
    assert not result.proved
    assert "false" in result.message
    assert [e["kind"] for e in events] == ["start", "refuted"]


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_full_search_with_scripted_model(monkeypatch):
    """Plan → skeleton check → hammer proves one lemma, 'the model' proves the other → final check."""
    theorem = "(n : ℕ) : (∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2) ∧ n ≤ n ^ 2"
    plan_json = {"plans": [{
        "sketch": "Prove both halves separately.",
        "lemmas": [
            {"name": "odd_sum", "statement": ODD_SUM, "informal": "sum of odd numbers"},
            {"name": "le_sq", "statement": "(n : ℕ) : n ≤ n ^ 2", "informal": "n ≤ n²"},
        ],
        "main_proof": "exact ⟨odd_sum n, le_sq n⟩",
    }]}

    def scripted(system, prompt):
        if system == planner.SYSTEM:
            return "```json\n" + json.dumps(plan_json) + "\n```"
        return "```json\n" + json.dumps({"proofs": [INDUCTION]}) + "\n```"

    monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", scripted)
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
    events = []
    result = search(theorem, rounds=1, on_event=events.append)

    assert result.proved, result.message
    assert "theorem main" in result.lean_code and "sorry" not in result.lean_code
    kinds = [e["kind"] for e in events]
    assert kinds[-1] == "done"
    assert kinds.count("lemma_proved") == 2

    capsys is a pytest fixture that captures what the code prints. The scripted full-search test tells the planner and the prover apart by their system prompt (system == planner.SYSTEM), so one fake model can play both roles. It checks a real two-lemma plan: the hammer proves le_sq, the "model" proves odd_sum by induction, and the assembled file passes the strict final check.

uv run pytest -q tests/test_search.py

  8. Prove your first theorem from the command line:

    uv run python -m mathfinder prove --lean "(n : ℕ) : ∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2"

Checkpoint

The command from the last step printed this for us (about 40 s; your plans will be worded differently):

— round 1: asking the planner —
  ✗ plan doesn't fit together: Use Mathlib's telescoping principle Finset.sum_range_induction ...
      Application type mismatch: The argument
  fun k => odd_step k ...
  ▶ plan: Induct on n. The base case is trivial. In the successor case, peel off the last term
          with Finset.sum_range_succ, rewrite with the induction hypothesis, and close with ring.  (lemmas: none)

✓ VERIFIED by Lean. Plan: Induct on n. ...

namespace MathFinder

theorem main (n : ℕ) : ∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2 := by
  induction n with
  | zero => simp
  | succ k ih =>
    rw [Finset.sum_range_succ, ih]
    ring

end MathFinder


Result: PROVED (verified)

Look at what happened: the critic caught a broken plan in one Lean run, before any time went into proving its lemmas, and the second plan went through.

A false statement stops immediately, with no planning at all:

uv run python -m mathfinder prove --lean "(n : ℕ) : n ^ 2 ≤ 2 * n + 3"
Theorem: (n : ℕ) : n ^ 2 ≤ 2 * n + 3
✗ The statement is FALSE. Counterexample: n := 4; issue: 16 ≤ 11 does not hold

Result: not proved (the statement is false: n := 4; issue: 16 ≤ 11 does not hold)

Common errors

  • error: the following arguments are required: --lean: LaTeX input arrives in M5; for now give the Lean statement.
  • Your shell mangles ∑ or ℕ: always put the statement in double quotes. You can also write Nat instead of ℕ.
  • Every plan fails the final check with unknown constant 'MathFinder.main': your assemble is missing the namespace MathFinder / end MathFinder lines.

M4d: When a lemma is stuck, search for it

What and why. Sometimes a plan is right but one of its lemmas is still too big: the hammer fails, and Claude's proofs keep failing even after repair rounds. Until now that lemma just became feedback ("lemma X got stuck"). A better move is the one a mathematician makes: treat the stuck lemma as a new, smaller theorem and plan a proof for it. That's recursive decomposition. The search calls itself on the stuck lemma (a sub-search). If the sub-search finds a proof, its helper lemmas are spliced into the parent plan and the parent carries on. MAX_DEPTH = 1 stops this after one level, so a hard problem can't spawn searches forever.

  1. Teach the search to call itself. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/search.py
    """The search: plan, check, prove, verify, and replan with what we learned.

  0. maybe the hammer proves the whole theorem directly (or refutes it)
  1. the planner proposes a few plans
  2. the critic throws out plans that don't fit together (one Lean run)
  3. remaining plans are tried cheapest-first (fewest lemmas); each lemma goes to
     the prover, and a refuted lemma kills its plan immediately
  4. a plan with every lemma proved is assembled into one file and checked by
     the strict final verification (no sorry, only standard axioms)
  5. otherwise everything that went wrong becomes feedback for the next round

A lemma the prover gets stuck on becomes a smaller search of its own (a
"sub-search", at most MAX_DEPTH levels deep). If the sub-search proves it, its
helper lemmas are spliced into the parent plan.

Everything that happens is reported through `on_event`, so the command line,
the web page and the trace logger can all watch the same search.
"""

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass

from mathfinder.critic import check_skeletons
from mathfinder.hammer import hammer
from mathfinder.lean import verify_final
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma, Plan, propose_plans
from mathfinder.prover import prove_lemma

NAMESPACE = "MathFinder"
MAX_DEPTH = 1  # a stuck lemma may get a sub-search; a stuck lemma inside that may not


@dataclass
class SearchResult:
    proved: bool
    lean_code: str = ""  # the complete verified proof (everything after the file header)
    plan: Plan | None = None
    rounds: int = 0
    message: str = ""


def assemble(theorem: str, plan: Plan) -> str:
    """One Lean file: every lemma with its real proof, then the main theorem."""
    blocks = [f"namespace {NAMESPACE}"]
    for l in plan.lemmas:
        body = "\n".join("  " + line for line in l.proof.split("\n"))
        blocks.append(f"theorem {l.name} {l.statement} := by\n{body}")
    main_body = "\n".join("  " + line for line in plan.main_proof.split("\n"))
    blocks.append(f"theorem main {theorem} := by\n{main_body}")
    blocks.append(f"end {NAMESPACE}")
    return "\n\n".join(blocks) + "\n"


def score(plan: Plan) -> float:
    """Fraction of lemmas proved; a refuted lemma makes the whole plan worthless."""
    if any(l.status == "refuted" for l in plan.lemmas):
        return -1.0
    if not plan.lemmas:
        return 0.0
    return sum(l.status == "proved" for l in plan.lemmas) / len(plan.lemmas)


def summarize(plan: Plan) -> str:
    """What happened to one plan, written for the planner's next round."""
    lines = [f"Plan: {plan.sketch}  (main proof: {plan.main_proof!r})"]
    lines += [f"  - {note}" for note in plan.feedback]
    for l in plan.lemmas:
        lines.append(f"  - lemma {l.name} {l.statement}: {l.status.upper()}  {l.feedback[:300]}")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def splice(plan: Plan, lemma: Lemma, sub: Plan) -> int:
    """Put a sub-search's lemmas into `plan` just before `lemma`, and use the sub-search's
    main proof as `lemma`'s proof. Sub-lemmas are renamed `<lemma>_<name>` so names stay
    unique. Returns how many lemmas were inserted."""
    renames = {l.name: f"{lemma.name}_{l.name}" for l in sub.lemmas}

    def rename(text: str) -> str:
        for old, new in renames.items():
            text = re.sub(rf"\b{re.escape(old)}\b", new, text)  # \b = whole words only
        return text

    inserted = [
        Lemma(renames[l.name], rename(l.statement), l.informal, "proved", rename(l.proof), "from sub-search")
        for l in sub.lemmas
    ]
    i = plan.lemmas.index(lemma)
    plan.lemmas[i:i] = inserted  # slice assignment: insert the list at position i
    lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", rename(sub.main_proof), "proved by a sub-search"
    return len(inserted)


def prove_plan(plan: Plan, emit, on_event=None, hints=(), depth: int = 0) -> bool:
    """Prove the plan's lemmas in order. Stops at the first refuted lemma.
    A stuck lemma gets a sub-search while depth < MAX_DEPTH."""
    i = 0
    while i < len(plan.lemmas):  # a while loop, because splice() can insert lemmas
        lemma = plan.lemmas[i]
        emit("lemma_start", name=lemma.name, statement=lemma.statement)
        prove_lemma(lemma, plan.lemmas[:i])
        if lemma.status == "stuck" and depth < MAX_DEPTH:
            emit("subsearch_start", name=lemma.name, statement=lemma.statement)
            sub = search(lemma.statement, hints, rounds=1, on_event=on_event, depth=depth + 1)
            if sub.proved:
                i += splice(plan, lemma, sub.plan)  # skip past the inserted (already proved) lemmas
        emit("lemma_" + lemma.status, name=lemma.name, statement=lemma.statement, feedback=lemma.feedback)
        if lemma.status == "refuted":
            return False
        i += 1
    return all(l.status == "proved" for l in plan.lemmas)


def try_direct(theorem: str, emit) -> SearchResult | None:
    """Step 0: maybe automation proves (or refutes) the whole theorem."""
    hit = hammer(theorem)
    if hit.counterexample:
        emit("refuted", counterexample=hit.counterexample)
        return SearchResult(False, message=f"the statement is false: {hit.counterexample}")
    if hit.proved:
        plan = Plan(f"Automation: {hit.tactic}", [], hit.proof)
        code = assemble(theorem, plan)
        if verify_final(code, f"{NAMESPACE}.main").ok:
            emit("done", lean_code=code, plan=plan.sketch)
            return SearchResult(True, code, plan, 0, "proved directly by automation")
    return None


def search(
    theorem: str, hints=(), rounds: int = 3, plans_per_round: int = 2, on_event=None, depth: int = 0
) -> SearchResult:
    """Search for a verified proof of `theorem` (a Lean statement without a name).
    `depth` is 0 for the user's theorem, 1 for a sub-search on a stuck lemma, ..."""

    def emit(kind, **data):
        if on_event:
            on_event({"kind": kind, "depth": depth, **data})

    emit("start", theorem=theorem)
    if depth == 0:  # in a sub-search, the prover has already run the hammer on this statement
        direct = try_direct(theorem, emit)
        if direct:
            return direct

    feedback = []  # what went wrong so far, fed to the planner
    best = None
    for r in range(1, rounds + 1):
        emit("round", number=r)
        plans = propose_plans(theorem, plans_per_round, list(hints), "\n".join(feedback[-6:]))
        emit("plans", sketches=[p.sketch for p in plans])
        problems = check_skeletons(theorem, plans)

        fitting = []
        for plan, found in zip(plans, problems):
            if found:
                emit("plan_rejected", sketch=plan.sketch, reason=found[0][:300])
                feedback.append(summarize(plan))
            else:
                fitting.append(plan)

        for plan in sorted(fitting, key=lambda p: len(p.lemmas)):  # cheapest first
            emit("plan_start", sketch=plan.sketch, lemmas=[l.name for l in plan.lemmas])
            if prove_plan(plan, emit, on_event, hints, depth):
                code = assemble(theorem, plan)
                final = verify_final(code, f"{NAMESPACE}.main")
                if final.ok:
                    emit("done", lean_code=code, plan=plan.sketch)
                    return SearchResult(True, code, plan, r, "verified")
                plan.feedback.append("final check failed: " + " | ".join(final.errors)[:400])
            emit("plan_failed", sketch=plan.sketch, score=score(plan))
            feedback.append(summarize(plan))
            if best is None or score(plan) > score(best):
                best = plan

    emit("gave_up", best=best.sketch if best else None)
    message = f"no verified proof after {rounds} rounds"
    if best:
        message += f"; closest plan proved {score(best):.0%} of its lemmas"
    return SearchResult(False, plan=best, rounds=rounds, message=message)

    What changed:

    • search takes a new depth argument (0 for your theorem, 1 inside a sub-search), and every event now carries "depth" so watchers can show sub-searches indented. A sub-search skips step 0 (the direct hammer), because the prover just ran the hammer on that same statement.
    • prove_plan uses a while loop instead of for: a sub-search can insert lemmas into the list being walked, and changing a list while a for loop runs over it skips or repeats items. With an explicit index i we decide exactly where to continue. It also gets on_event, hints and depth so it can start the sub-search with the same watchers and hints.
    • A function calling itself (here search → prove_plan → search) is recursion. It always needs a stopping rule, and ours is depth < MAX_DEPTH.
    • splice renames each sub-lemma to <stuck lemma>_<name> (so a sub-lemma step of odd_sum becomes odd_sum_step) and rewrites every mention of it. rf"\b{re.escape(old)}\b" is a regular expression for the whole word only: \b is a word boundary, so renaming step leaves steps alone, and re.escape stops symbols in the name being read as regex syntax. plan.lemmas[i:i] = inserted is slice assignment: it inserts a whole list at position i. The stuck lemma's proof becomes the sub-search's main proof.

  2. Show sub-searches indented on the command line. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/cli.py
    """Command line:  uv run python -m mathfinder prove --lean "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"

(LaTeX input arrives in M5; for now you give the Lean statement directly.)
"""

import argparse
import sys

from mathfinder.search import search

SYMBOL = {"lemma_proved": "✓", "lemma_refuted": "✗", "lemma_stuck": "…"}


def print_event(event: dict) -> None:
    kind = event["kind"]
    if event.get("depth", 0) > 0:  # events from a sub-search are indented under their lemma
        indent = "        " * event["depth"]
        if kind in SYMBOL:
            print(f"{indent}{SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
        elif kind == "plan_start":
            print(f"{indent}▶ sub-plan: {event['sketch']}")
        elif kind == "done":
            print(f"{indent}✓ sub-search proved it")
        sys.stdout.flush()
        return
    if kind == "start":
        print(f"\nTheorem: {event['theorem']}")
    elif kind == "subsearch_start":
        print(f"      ↳ {event['name']} is stuck: starting a sub-search on it")
    elif kind == "refuted":
        print(f"✗ The statement is FALSE. Counterexample: {event['counterexample']}")
    elif kind == "round":
        print(f"\n— round {event['number']}: asking the planner —")
    elif kind == "plan_rejected":
        print(f"  ✗ plan doesn't fit together: {event['sketch']}\n      {event['reason'][:160]}")
    elif kind == "plan_start":
        print(f"  ▶ plan: {event['sketch']}  (lemmas: {', '.join(event['lemmas']) or 'none'})")
    elif kind in SYMBOL:
        print(f"      {SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
    elif kind == "plan_failed":
        print(f"    plan failed (score {event['score']:.2f})")
    elif kind == "done":
        print(f"\n✓ VERIFIED by Lean. Plan: {event['plan']}\n")
        print(event["lean_code"])
    elif kind == "gave_up":
        print("\nNo verified proof found.")
    sys.stdout.flush()  # show progress immediately, even when output is piped to a file


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="mathfinder")
    commands = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    prove = commands.add_parser("prove", help="search for a proof")
    prove.add_argument("--lean", required=True, help='Lean statement without a name, e.g. "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"')
    prove.add_argument("--hint", action="append", default=[], help="a hint for the planner (repeatable)")
    prove.add_argument("--rounds", type=int, default=3)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    if args.command == "prove":
        result = search(args.lean, hints=args.hint, rounds=args.rounds, on_event=print_event)
        print(f"\nResult: {'PROVED' if result.proved else 'not proved'} ({result.message})")
        return 0 if result.proved else 1
    return 2

    What changed: print_event first handles events with depth > 0 (indented, and only the interesting ones), then prints a ↳ ... starting a sub-search line for the new subsearch_start event. event.get("depth", 0) reads the key, or gives 0 if it's missing.

  3. Add tests. Replace the whole file:

    tests/test_search.py

@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def temp_cache(tmp_path, monkeypatch):

# ---------- fast tests ----------


def test_assemble_builds_one_namespaced_file():

    assert assemble("(a : ℝ) : a ^ 2 ≥ 0", plan) == (
        "namespace MathFinder\n\n"

        "theorem main (a : ℝ) : a ^ 2 ≥ 0 := by\n  nlinarith [key a]\n\n"
        "end MathFinder\n"
    )


def test_score_is_fraction_proved_and_refuted_is_worst():

    stuck = Lemma("b", ": True", "", status="stuck")
    refuted = Lemma("c", ": True", "", status="refuted")
    assert score(Plan("", [proved, stuck], "")) == 0.5
    assert score(Plan("", [proved, refuted], "")) == -1.0


def test_splice_inserts_renamed_sub_lemmas_before_the_stuck_lemma():
    first = Lemma("first", ": True", "", status="proved", proof="trivial")
    stuck = Lemma("hard", "(n : ℕ) : P n", "", status="stuck")
    plan = Plan("", [first, stuck], "exact hard")
    sub = Plan("", [Lemma("step", "(k : ℕ) : Q k", "", status="proved", proof="simp")], "exact step n")

    assert splice(plan, stuck, sub) == 1
    assert [l.name for l in plan.lemmas] == ["first", "hard_step", "hard"]
    assert stuck.status == "proved" and stuck.proof == "exact hard_step n"


def test_cli_passes_arguments_to_search(monkeypatch, capsys):
    seen = {}

    def fake_search(theorem, hints, rounds, on_event):
        seen.update(theorem=theorem, hints=hints, rounds=rounds)
        on_event({"kind": "start", "theorem": theorem})
        return SearchResult(True, message="verified")

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "search", fake_search)
    code = cli.main(["prove", "--lean", "(n : ℕ) : n = n", "--hint", "use rfl", "--rounds", "1"])
    assert code == 0
    assert seen == {"theorem": "(n : ℕ) : n = n", "hints": ["use rfl"], "rounds": 1}
    assert "Result: PROVED" in capsys.readouterr().out


def test_cli_indents_sub_search_events(capsys):
    cli.print_event({"kind": "subsearch_start", "depth": 0, "name": "hard", "statement": ": P"})
    cli.print_event({"kind": "lemma_proved", "depth": 1, "name": "step", "statement": ": Q", "feedback": "hammer: simp"})
    out = capsys.readouterr().out.splitlines()
    assert out[0] == "      ↳ hard is stuck: starting a sub-search on it"
    assert out[1] == "        ✓ step : : Q   [hammer: simp]"


# ---------- Lean tests ----------


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_false_theorem_is_refuted_before_any_planning(monkeypatch):
    def no_model(system, prompt):
        raise AssertionError("the model should not be called for a false statement")

    monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", no_model)
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")

    result = search("(n : ℕ) : n ^ 2 ≤ 2 * n + 3", on_event=events.append)
    assert not result.proved
    assert "false" in result.message
    assert [e["kind"] for e in events] == ["start", "refuted"]


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_full_search_with_scripted_model(monkeypatch):
    """Plan → skeleton check → hammer proves one lemma, 'the model' proves the other → final check."""
    theorem = "(n : ℕ) : (∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2) ∧ n ≤ n ^ 2"
    plan_json = {"plans": [{
        "sketch": "Prove both halves separately.",
        "lemmas": [
            {"name": "odd_sum", "statement": ODD_SUM, "informal": "sum of odd numbers"},
            {"name": "le_sq", "statement": "(n : ℕ) : n ≤ n ^ 2", "informal": "n ≤ n²"},
        ],
        "main_proof": "exact ⟨odd_sum n, le_sq n⟩",
    }]}

    def scripted(system, prompt):
        if system == planner.SYSTEM:
            return "```json\n" + json.dumps(plan_json) + "\n```"
        return "```json\n" + json.dumps({"proofs": [INDUCTION]}) + "\n```"

    monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", scripted)
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
    events = []
    result = search(theorem, rounds=1, on_event=events.append)

    assert result.proved, result.message
    assert "theorem main" in result.lean_code and "sorry" not in result.lean_code
    kinds = [e["kind"] for e in events]
    assert kinds[-1] == "done"
    assert kinds.count("lemma_proved") == 2


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_stuck_lemma_is_proved_by_a_sub_search(monkeypatch):
    """The 'model' can't prove odd_sum directly, so a sub-search splits it into a smaller step."""
    theorem = "(n : ℕ) : (∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2) ∧ n ≤ n ^ 2"
    top_plan = {"plans": [{
        "sketch": "Prove both halves separately.",
        "lemmas": [
            {"name": "odd_sum", "statement": ODD_SUM, "informal": ""},
            {"name": "le_sq", "statement": "(n : ℕ) : n ≤ n ^ 2", "informal": ""},
        ],
        "main_proof": "exact ⟨odd_sum n, le_sq n⟩",
    }]}
    sub_plan = {"plans": [{
        "sketch": "Induction, using the step (k+1)^2 = k^2 + (2k+1).",
        "lemmas": [{"name": "step", "statement": "(k : ℕ) : (k + 1) ^ 2 = k ^ 2 + (2 * k + 1)", "informal": ""}],
        "main_proof": "induction n with\n| zero => simp\n| succ k ih =>\n  rw [Finset.sum_range_succ, ih, step k]",
    }]}

    def scripted(system, prompt):
        if system == planner.SYSTEM:
            chosen = top_plan if "∧" in prompt else sub_plan
            return "```json\n" + json.dumps(chosen) + "\n```"
        return "```json\n" + json.dumps({"proofs": ["simp"]}) + "\n```"  # the prover always fails

    monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", scripted)
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
    monkeypatch.setattr(prover, "REPAIR_ROUNDS", 0)  # fail fast: one LLM round per lemma
    events = []
    result = search(theorem, rounds=1, on_event=events.append)

    assert result.proved, result.message
    assert "theorem odd_sum_step" in result.lean_code  # the sub-lemma, renamed and spliced in
    assert any(e["kind"] == "subsearch_start" for e in events)
    assert any(e["kind"] == "done" and e["depth"] == 1 for e in events)

    What changed: three new tests. test_splice_... checks the renaming and insertion without Lean. test_cli_indents_sub_search_events checks the printing. test_stuck_lemma_is_proved_by_a_sub_search scripts a model whose prover always fails (it only ever suggests simp), so odd_sum must get stuck. The sub-search's planner then proposes a helper step: (k+1)² = k² + (2k+1), which the hammer proves, plus an induction that uses it. monkeypatch.setattr(prover, "REPAIR_ROUNDS", 0) makes the doomed attempts fail after one round instead of three.

  4. Run the fast tests, then the search Lean tests (about 2½ minutes):

    uv run pytest -q -m "not lean and not llm"
uv run pytest -q tests/test_search.py

Checkpoint

uv run pytest -q tests/test_search.py -rA

Expected (times vary):

PASSED tests/test_search.py::test_assemble_builds_one_namespaced_file
PASSED tests/test_search.py::test_score_is_fraction_proved_and_refuted_is_worst
PASSED tests/test_search.py::test_splice_inserts_renamed_sub_lemmas_before_the_stuck_lemma
PASSED tests/test_search.py::test_cli_passes_arguments_to_search
