8 passed in 141.79s (0:02:21)

With the real model you'll see a sub-search on the command line as lines like ↳ odd_sum is stuck: starting a sub-search on it followed by indented sub-plan and lemma lines.

Common errors

  • RecursionError or a search that never ends: the depth < MAX_DEPTH check is missing, or you pass depth instead of depth + 1 to the sub-search.
  • The final check fails with unknown identifier 'step': splice didn't rename inside the stuck lemma's new proof (rename(sub.main_proof)).
  • Lemmas get proved twice or skipped: prove_plan must do i += splice(...) and then the normal i += 1.

M5: From LaTeX to a Lean statement you approve

What and why. People write theorems in LaTeX, but Lean needs an exact formal statement. Translating is the most dangerous step in the whole system, because Lean will happily prove a statement that doesn't say what you meant. Two classic traps: on the natural numbers ℕ, 2 - 5 = 0 (subtraction stops at zero), and dropping an assumption like "n ≥ 1" quietly changes the claim. A perfect proof of the wrong statement is worthless. So we check three ways and then ask you:

  1. Lean must accept the statement (it type-checks, with sorry as the proof). If not, the error goes back to the model to fix.
  2. A separate model call translates the Lean back into LaTeX without seeing your original, so it can't "helpfully" paper over a mistranslation.
  3. A judge compares your LaTeX with that back-translation, looking specifically for the classic traps.
  4. You approve, reject, or edit the Lean statement yourself. Nothing is searched until you say yes.

  1. Create the formalizer.

    mathfinder/formalize.py
    """Formalization: turn a theorem written in LaTeX into a Lean statement you can trust.

This is the most dangerous step in the whole system: Lean will happily prove a
statement that doesn't say what you meant. (On ℕ, 2 - 5 = 0. Forget "n ≥ 1" and
the claim changes.) So we use three separate checks before any search starts:

  1. Lean must accept the statement (it type-checks, with `sorry` as the proof)
  2. a SEPARATE model call translates the Lean back into LaTeX without seeing your
     original, so it can't paper over a mistake
  3. a judge compares your LaTeX with that back-translation

And then YOU approve it. Nothing is proved until a person says "yes, that's my theorem".
"""

from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from mathfinder import llm
from mathfinder.lean import statement_errors

REPAIR_ROUNDS = 2

SYSTEM_FORMALIZE = """You translate theorems written in LaTeX into Lean 4 + Mathlib statements.
Rules:
- Write only what comes after `theorem name`: binders and hypotheses, a colon, then the claim.
  Example: "(a b : ℝ) (ha : 0 < a) : a * b ≤ a * b + a". If there are no variables, start with ": ".
- Choose the number system the LaTeX means (ℕ, ℤ, ℚ, ℝ, ℂ). Be careful: subtraction and division on ℕ
  truncate, so prefer ℤ or ℝ when the original involves negatives or fractions.
- Make every assumption explicit as a hypothesis. Do not add assumptions the original doesn't have,
  and do not drop any.
- Lean 4 syntax and current Mathlib names only. Sums: ∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, f i.
Reply with ONLY a JSON object inside a ```json block: {"statement": "...", "notes": "..."}"""

SYSTEM_BACK = """You translate Lean 4 + Mathlib statements into LaTeX, LITERALLY.
Say exactly what the Lean says, including the number types (ℕ, ℤ, ℝ) and every hypothesis.
Do not guess what the author intended and do not fix anything.
Reply with ONLY a JSON object inside a ```json block: {"latex": "..."}"""

SYSTEM_JUDGE = """You check whether a formal statement says the same thing as the original theorem.
You get: the original LaTeX, and a literal LaTeX translation of the formal Lean statement.
Watch for: wrong number type (ℕ vs ℤ vs ℝ), truncated subtraction or division on ℕ, missing or
extra hypotheses, wrong quantifiers, off-by-one ranges in sums, strict vs non-strict inequalities.
Reply with ONLY a JSON object inside a ```json block: {"same_meaning": true or false, "reason": "..."}"""


@dataclass
class Formalization:
    latex: str
    statement: str = ""  # the Lean statement (what comes after `theorem name`)
    back_translation: str = ""  # literal LaTeX of the Lean statement
    faithful: bool = False  # the judge's opinion
    judgment: str = ""  # the judge's reason
    errors: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)  # Lean errors, if it never type-checked

    @property
    def ok(self) -> bool:
        return bool(self.statement) and not self.errors


def clean_statement(text: str) -> str:
    """Accept answers like 'theorem foo (n : ℕ) : P n := by sorry' and keep just '(n : ℕ) : P n'."""
    text = text.strip()
    for keyword in ("theorem ", "lemma ", "example "):
        if text.startswith(keyword):
            rest = text[len(keyword):].lstrip()
            if keyword != "example " and not rest.startswith(("(", "{", "[", ":")):
                rest = rest.split(None, 1)[1] if " " in rest else ""  # drop the name
            text = rest
            break
    return text.split(":=", 1)[0].strip()


def formalize(latex: str) -> Formalization:
    """LaTeX in; a checked Lean statement with a back-translation and a judgment out."""
    result = Formalization(latex)
    prompt = f"Theorem in LaTeX:\n{latex}"
    for attempt in range(1 + REPAIR_ROUNDS):
        data = llm.complete_json(SYSTEM_FORMALIZE, prompt)
        result.statement = clean_statement(str(data.get("statement", "")))
        result.errors = statement_errors(result.statement) if result.statement else ["empty statement"]
        if not result.errors:
            break
        prompt += (
            f"\n\nYour statement:\n  {result.statement}\nfailed in Lean with:\n"
            f"{result.errors[0][:600]}\nFix it and reply again."
        )
    if result.errors:
        return result

    back = llm.complete_json(SYSTEM_BACK, f"theorem main {result.statement}")
    result.back_translation = str(back.get("latex", ""))
    result.faithful, result.judgment = judge(latex, result.back_translation)
    return result


def judge(original: str, back_translation: str) -> tuple[bool, str]:
    """Do these two say the same thing? Returns (same_meaning, reason)."""
    verdict = llm.complete_json(
        SYSTEM_JUDGE,
        f"Original:\n{original}\n\nLiteral translation of the formal statement:\n{back_translation}",
    )
    return verdict.get("same_meaning") is True, str(verdict.get("reason", ""))

    Three system prompts, three jobs. SYSTEM_FORMALIZE spells out the traps (ℕ subtraction, missing hypotheses) and the exact output format. SYSTEM_BACK says literally and "do not fix anything", because a back-translator that guesses intent would hide errors. SYSTEM_JUDGE gets a checklist of what to compare.

    @property makes ok read like a field (result.ok, no parentheses) while actually computing it from statement and errors.

    clean_statement forgives answers like theorem foo (n : ℕ) : ... := by sorry: it strips the keyword, the name (unless the text right after the keyword already starts with a binder or a colon) and everything from := on. text.split(None, 1) splits once at the first run of whitespace.

    formalize has the same shape as the prover's repair loop: ask, check with Lean, and on failure append the statement and Lean's error to the prompt and ask again. verdict.get("same_meaning") is True counts only a real JSON true as yes; a vague answer counts as no.

  2. Create its tests.

    tests/test_formalize.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/formalize.py."""

import json

import pytest

from mathfinder import formalize as fm
from mathfinder import llm

# ---------- fast tests ----------


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "answer, expected",
    [
        ("(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n", "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"),
        ("theorem foo (n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n := by sorry", "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"),
        ("lemma bar : 1 + 1 = 2", ": 1 + 1 = 2"),
        ("example (x : ℝ) : x = x := rfl", "(x : ℝ) : x = x"),
        ("theorem (a : ℤ) : a = a", "(a : ℤ) : a = a"),
    ],
)
def test_clean_statement(answer, expected):
    assert fm.clean_statement(answer) == expected


# ---------- Lean test, scripted model ----------


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_formalize_repairs_a_broken_statement_then_judges_it(monkeypatch):
    formal_answers = ["(n : ℕ) : n = undefined_q", "(n : ℕ) : ∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2"]
    seen = []

    def scripted(system, prompt):
        seen.append(system)
        if system == fm.SYSTEM_FORMALIZE:
            return "```json\n" + json.dumps({"statement": formal_answers.pop(0)}) + "\n```"
        if system == fm.SYSTEM_BACK:
            assert "sum of the first" not in prompt  # the back-translator never sees the original
            return '```json\n{"latex": "For all $n \\\\in \\\\mathbb{N}$, $\\\\sum_{i<n} (2i+1) = n^2$."}\n```'
        return '```json\n{"same_meaning": true, "reason": "identical"}\n```'

    monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", scripted)
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
    result = fm.formalize(r"The sum of the first $n$ odd numbers is $n^2$.")

    assert result.ok and result.faithful
    assert result.statement.endswith("= n ^ 2")
    assert seen == [fm.SYSTEM_FORMALIZE, fm.SYSTEM_FORMALIZE, fm.SYSTEM_BACK, fm.SYSTEM_JUDGE]


# ---------- real model ----------


@pytest.mark.llm
def test_real_formalization_of_a_simple_theorem():
    result = fm.formalize(r"For every integer $n$, $6 \mid n^3 - n$.")
    assert result.ok, result.errors
    assert "ℤ" in result.statement or "Int" in result.statement
    assert result.faithful, result.judgment


@pytest.mark.llm
def test_real_judge_catches_natural_number_truncation():
    same, reason = fm.judge(
        r"For every integer $n$, $(n - 3)^2 \ge 0$.",
        r"For every natural number $n \in \mathbb{N}$, $(n - 3)^2 \ge 0$, where subtraction is truncated at 0.",
    )
    assert not same, reason

    @pytest.mark.parametrize runs one test function once per row of inputs: five clean_statement cases in five lines. The scripted Lean test makes the "model" give a broken statement first (undefined_q), so the repair path really runs against Lean. It also asserts that the back-translator's prompt doesn't contain the original. The last real test feeds the judge an ℕ-vs-ℤ trap and expects "not the same".

  3. Add the approval step to the command line. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/cli.py
    """Command line.

  uv run python -m mathfinder prove "For every integer $n$, $6 \\mid n^3 - n$."
  uv run python -m mathfinder prove --lean "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"

With LaTeX, you first see the Lean statement, its back-translation and the
judge's opinion, and nothing is searched until you approve.
"""

import argparse
import sys

from mathfinder.formalize import formalize
from mathfinder.lean import statement_errors
from mathfinder.search import search

SYMBOL = {"lemma_proved": "✓", "lemma_refuted": "✗", "lemma_stuck": "…"}


def print_event(event: dict) -> None:
    kind = event["kind"]
    if event.get("depth", 0) > 0:  # events from a sub-search are indented under their lemma
        indent = "        " * event["depth"]
        if kind in SYMBOL:
            print(f"{indent}{SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
        elif kind == "plan_start":
            print(f"{indent}▶ sub-plan: {event['sketch']}")
        elif kind == "done":
            print(f"{indent}✓ sub-search proved it")
        sys.stdout.flush()
        return
    if kind == "start":
        print(f"\nTheorem: {event['theorem']}")
    elif kind == "subsearch_start":
        print(f"      ↳ {event['name']} is stuck: starting a sub-search on it")
    elif kind == "refuted":
        print(f"✗ The statement is FALSE. Counterexample: {event['counterexample']}")
    elif kind == "round":
        print(f"\n— round {event['number']}: asking the planner —")
    elif kind == "plan_rejected":
        print(f"  ✗ plan doesn't fit together: {event['sketch']}\n      {event['reason'][:160]}")
    elif kind == "plan_start":
        print(f"  ▶ plan: {event['sketch']}  (lemmas: {', '.join(event['lemmas']) or 'none'})")
    elif kind in SYMBOL:
        print(f"      {SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
    elif kind == "plan_failed":
        print(f"    plan failed (score {event['score']:.2f})")
    elif kind == "done":
        print(f"\n✓ VERIFIED by Lean. Plan: {event['plan']}\n")
        print(event["lean_code"])
    elif kind == "gave_up":
        print("\nNo verified proof found.")
    sys.stdout.flush()  # show progress immediately, even when output is piped to a file


def approve(latex: str, auto_yes: bool = False, ask=input) -> str | None:
    """Formalize `latex`, show it, and return the statement the user approved (None = no)."""
    print("Formalizing (LaTeX → Lean)...")
    f = formalize(latex)
    if not f.ok:
        print("Could not produce a statement Lean accepts:\n  " + f.errors[0][:400])
        return None
    print(f"\nFormal statement:  theorem main {f.statement}")
    print(f"Back-translation:  {f.back_translation}")
    print(f"Judge:             {'same meaning' if f.faithful else 'MEANING MAY DIFFER'}: {f.judgment}")

    statement = f.statement
    if auto_yes and f.faithful:
        return statement
    if auto_yes:
        print("(--yes ignored: the judge thinks the meaning may differ, so please look.)")
    while True:
        try:
            answer = ask("\nProve this statement? [y]es / [n]o / [e]dit: ").strip().lower()
        except EOFError:  # no keyboard attached (e.g. running in a script)
            return None
        if answer == "y":
            return statement
        if answer == "n":
            return None
        if answer == "e":
            new = ask("Lean statement (what comes after `theorem main`): ").strip()
            errors = statement_errors(new)
            if errors:
                print("Lean rejected it: " + errors[0][:300])
            else:
                statement = new
                print(f"Now:  theorem main {statement}")


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="mathfinder")
    commands = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    prove = commands.add_parser("prove", help="search for a proof")
    prove.add_argument("latex", nargs="?", help="the theorem in LaTeX (in quotes)")
    prove.add_argument("--lean", help='skip formalization: a Lean statement, e.g. "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"')
    prove.add_argument("--yes", action="store_true", help="don't ask for approval if the judge is happy")
    prove.add_argument("--hint", action="append", default=[], help="a hint for the planner (repeatable)")
    prove.add_argument("--rounds", type=int, default=3)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    if args.command == "prove":
        if args.lean:
            statement = args.lean
        elif args.latex:
            statement = approve(args.latex, args.yes)
            if statement is None:
                print("Not searching.")
                return 1
        else:
            parser.error("give a theorem in LaTeX, or --lean STATEMENT")
        result = search(statement, hints=args.hint, rounds=args.rounds, on_event=print_event)
        print(f"\nResult: {'PROVED' if result.proved else 'not proved'} ({result.message})")
        return 0 if result.proved else 1
    return 2

    What changed: prove now takes the theorem in LaTeX as a positional argument (nargs="?" makes it optional, because --lean still works and skips formalization). There's a new --yes flag, and a new function approve.

    approve prints the formal statement, the back-translation and the judge's verdict, then loops on [y]es / [n]o / [e]dit. An edited statement is checked by Lean before it replaces the old one. --yes is honoured only when the judge agrees: if it thinks the meaning may differ, you're asked anyway. ask=input is a default argument holding a function: normally it's Python's input, and tests pass a scripted user instead. except EOFError handles running with no keyboard (in a script), which counts as "no".

  4. Create the approval tests.

    tests/test_cli.py
    """Tests for the approval step in mathfinder/cli.py. No Lean or model needed: both are faked."""

import pytest

from mathfinder import cli
from mathfinder.formalize import Formalization
from mathfinder.search import SearchResult


@pytest.fixture
def fake_formalize(monkeypatch):
    def install(faithful=True):
        result = Formalization("latex", "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n", "For all integers n, 6 | n^3 - n.", faithful, "ok")
        monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "formalize", lambda latex: result)

    return install


def scripted_user(*answers):
    """Stands in for input(): gives the answers in order."""
    queue = list(answers)
    return lambda question: queue.pop(0)


def test_yes_returns_the_statement(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize()
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=scripted_user("y")) == "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"


def test_no_returns_none(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize()
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=scripted_user("n")) is None


def test_edit_is_checked_by_lean_before_it_is_used(fake_formalize, monkeypatch):
    fake_formalize()
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "statement_errors", lambda s: ["unknown identifier 'oops'"] if "oops" in s else [])
    user = scripted_user("e", "(n : ℤ) : oops", "e", "(n : ℕ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n", "y")
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=user) == "(n : ℕ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"


def test_auto_yes_only_when_the_judge_agrees(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize(faithful=True)
    assert cli.approve("latex", auto_yes=True, ask=scripted_user()) is not None  # never asks
    fake_formalize(faithful=False)
    assert cli.approve("latex", auto_yes=True, ask=scripted_user("n")) is None  # asks anyway


def test_no_keyboard_means_no(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize()

    def no_keyboard(question):
        raise EOFError

    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=no_keyboard) is None


def test_main_with_latex_and_yes_runs_the_search(fake_formalize, monkeypatch):
    fake_formalize()
    seen = {}

    def fake_search(statement, hints, rounds, on_event):
        seen["statement"] = statement
        return SearchResult(True, message="verified")

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "search", fake_search)
    assert cli.main(["prove", r"For every integer $n$, $6 \mid n^3 - n$.", "--yes"]) == 0
    assert seen["statement"] == "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"

    scripted_user("e", "(n : ℤ) : oops", "e", ..., "y") plays a person who edits once badly (Lean rejects it), edits again correctly, then approves. These tests fake both formalize and Lean, so they're instant.

  5. Run the fast tests, then the real formalization tests (about 30 s of Claude):

    uv run pytest -q -m "not lean and not llm"
uv run pytest -q -m llm tests/test_formalize.py

  6. Prove a theorem written in LaTeX. Use single quotes so your shell leaves $ and \ alone:

    uv run python -m mathfinder prove 'For all positive reals $a, b$: $\frac{a}{b} + \frac{b}{a} \ge 2$.'

Checkpoint

This is what we got (with --yes added, so it didn't stop to ask; about 2 minutes):

Formalizing (LaTeX → Lean)...

Formal statement:  theorem main (a b : ℝ) (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) : a / b + b / a ≥ 2
Back-translation:  Let $a, b \in \mathbb{R}$ with $0 < a$ and $0 < b$. Then $\frac{a}{b} + \frac{b}{a} \geq 2$.
Judge:             same meaning: Both statements quantify over real a, b with a > 0 and b > 0 ...

— round 1: asking the planner —
  ▶ plan: Show a/b + b/a - 2 = (a-b)^2/(a*b). The right side is nonnegative ...  (lemmas: diff_identity, frac_sq_nonneg)
      ✓ diff_identity : (a b : ℝ) (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) : a / b + b / a - 2 = (a - b) ^ 2 / (a * b)   [LLM]
      ✓ frac_sq_nonneg : (a b : ℝ) (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) : 0 ≤ (a - b) ^ 2 / (a * b)   [hammer: positivity]

✓ VERIFIED by Lean. ...

theorem diff_identity (a b : ℝ) (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) : a / b + b / a - 2 = (a - b) ^ 2 / (a * b) := by
  have ha' : a ≠ 0 := ne_of_gt ha
  have hb' : b ≠ 0 := ne_of_gt hb
  field_simp
  ring
...
Result: PROVED (verified)

Notice the formalizer turned "positive reals" into two explicit hypotheses ha : 0 < a and hb : 0 < b. Without them, a / b with b = 0 is 0 in Lean and the statement would be false.

Fast tests:

............................................                             [100%]
44 passed, 27 deselected in 0.06s

Common errors

  • The LaTeX arrives mangled ($a disappears): you used double quotes, and your shell expanded $a as a variable. Use single quotes.
  • Could not produce a statement Lean accepts: unusual notation. Simplify the LaTeX, or write the Lean yourself and use --lean.
  • The judge says MEANING MAY DIFFER: read its reason. It's usually right (ℕ vs ℤ, a missing hypothesis). Choose e and fix the statement.

M6: Explaining the proof in plain mathematics

What and why. A Lean proof is certain but hard to read. Once Lean has checked it, we ask Claude to write it up the way a textbook would: the theorem, the key idea, then one step per lemma. Every step is tagged with its Lean name, like (`mod_six_cases`), so you can always trace a sentence back to the exact part of the formal proof it describes. The explainer can't make the proof wrong, because the proof is already verified. Its only job is to be clear and honest, so the prompt tells it to describe only what the Lean proof actually does. Every proved theorem is also saved to disk: the exact Lean file (which you can check again any time) and the explanation.

  1. Create the explainer.

    mathfinder/explain.py
    """The explainer: turn a verified Lean proof back into mathematics a person can read.

Lean has already checked the proof, so the explanation's only job is to be
clear and honest: one step per lemma, each tagged with its Lean name, so you
can always see which part of the formal proof a sentence is talking about.
"""

from mathfinder import llm
from mathfinder.planner import Plan

SYSTEM = """You explain formal Lean 4 proofs to people, in plain mathematics.
Write Markdown with LaTeX math ($...$ inline, $$...$$ for displays). Use exactly this structure:

## Theorem
The statement, in LaTeX.

## Idea
One to three sentences: the key insight.

## Proof
One numbered step per lemma, in order, each starting with **Step k** (`lemma_name`),
then a final step starting with **Conclusion** (`main`) that combines them.
In each step say WHAT is shown and WHY it is true, the way a careful textbook would.
Mention a Lean tactic only if it helps the reader (e.g. "by induction on n").

Rules: explain only what the Lean proof actually does. Do not invent steps it doesn't take.
Reply with the Markdown only."""


def build_prompt(theorem: str, plan: Plan, lean_code: str) -> str:
    lemma_lines = "\n".join(f"- `{l.name}`: {l.informal or l.statement}" for l in plan.lemmas) or "- (none)"
    return (
        f"Theorem (as the user wrote it):\n{theorem}\n\n"
        f"Proof idea from the planner: {plan.sketch}\n\n"
        f"Lemmas, in order:\n{lemma_lines}\n\n"
        f"The complete Lean proof, verified by Lean:\n```lean\n{lean_code}\n```"
    )


def missing_steps(markdown: str, plan: Plan) -> list[str]:
    """Lean names that the explanation never mentions as `name`."""
    names = [l.name for l in plan.lemmas] + ["main"]
    return [n for n in names if f"`{n}`" not in markdown]


def explain(theorem: str, plan: Plan, lean_code: str) -> str:
    """A Markdown explanation of a verified proof. Asks once more if it skipped a lemma."""
    prompt = build_prompt(theorem, plan, lean_code)
    markdown = llm.complete(SYSTEM, prompt)
    missing = missing_steps(markdown, plan)
    if missing:
        markdown = llm.complete(
            SYSTEM,
            f"{prompt}\n\nYour previous explanation had no step for: {', '.join(missing)}. "
            "Write it again with one step for every lemma, tagged with its name in backticks.",
        )
    return markdown

    SYSTEM fixes the structure (Theorem / Idea / Proof) and the tagging format, so the output is predictable enough to check. build_prompt gives the model your original wording of the theorem, the planner's idea, each lemma's meaning in words, and the full verified Lean code in a ```lean block. ... or "- (none)" uses or to pick a fallback when the joined string is empty.

    missing_steps is a cheap, mechanical check: every lemma name (and main) must appear in backticks somewhere. If one doesn't, explain asks once more and names what was skipped. We use llm.complete (plain text) here, not complete_json, because the answer is Markdown, not JSON.

  2. Create its tests.

    tests/test_explain.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/explain.py."""

import pytest

from mathfinder import explain as ex
from mathfinder import llm
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma, Plan

PLAN = Plan(
    "Write a/b + b/a - 2 as (a-b)^2/(ab), which is nonnegative.",
    [
        Lemma("diff_identity", "(a b : ℝ) (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) : a / b + b / a - 2 = (a - b) ^ 2 / (a * b)",
              "a/b + b/a - 2 = (a-b)^2/(ab)", status="proved", proof="field_simp\nring"),
        Lemma("frac_sq_nonneg", "(a b : ℝ) (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) : 0 ≤ (a - b) ^ 2 / (a * b)",
              "(a-b)^2/(ab) is nonnegative", status="proved", proof="positivity"),
    ],
    "have h1 := diff_identity a b ha hb\nhave h2 := frac_sq_nonneg a b ha hb\nlinarith",
)
CODE = "namespace MathFinder\n\ntheorem diff_identity ...\n\nend MathFinder\n"
THEOREM = r"For all positive reals $a, b$: $\frac{a}{b} + \frac{b}{a} \ge 2$."


def test_prompt_contains_the_lean_proof_and_lemma_meanings():
    prompt = ex.build_prompt(THEOREM, PLAN, CODE)
    assert "```lean\nnamespace MathFinder" in prompt
    assert "- `frac_sq_nonneg`: (a-b)^2/(ab) is nonnegative" in prompt


def test_missing_steps_finds_unmentioned_lemmas():
    text = "**Step 1** (`diff_identity`) ... **Conclusion** (`main`) ..."
    assert ex.missing_steps(text, PLAN) == ["frac_sq_nonneg"]


def test_explain_asks_again_when_a_step_is_missing(monkeypatch):
    answers = ["Step 1 (`diff_identity`) only.", "(`diff_identity`) (`frac_sq_nonneg`) (`main`)"]
    prompts = []

    def fake(system, prompt):
        prompts.append(prompt)
        return answers.pop(0)

    monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", fake)
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
    assert ex.explain(THEOREM, PLAN, CODE) == "(`diff_identity`) (`frac_sq_nonneg`) (`main`)"
    assert "no step for: frac_sq_nonneg, main" in prompts[1]


@pytest.mark.llm
def test_real_explanation_has_a_step_per_lemma():
    markdown = ex.explain(THEOREM, PLAN, CODE)
    assert ex.missing_steps(markdown, PLAN) == []
    assert "## Proof" in markdown

    The fake-model test gives a first answer that skips frac_sq_nonneg and main, then checks that the second prompt names exactly those two. The real test checks the model's answer passes missing_steps and has a ## Proof section.

  3. Explain and save every proof the command line finds. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/cli.py
    """Command line.

  uv run python -m mathfinder prove "For every integer $n$, $6 \\mid n^3 - n$."
  uv run python -m mathfinder prove --lean "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"

With LaTeX, you first see the Lean statement, its back-translation and the
judge's opinion, and nothing is searched until you approve.
Every proved theorem is saved in runs/<date-time>/ as proof.lean + explanation.md.
"""

import argparse
import sys
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

from mathfinder import PROJECT_ROOT
from mathfinder.explain import explain
from mathfinder.formalize import formalize
from mathfinder.lean import HEADER, statement_errors
from mathfinder.llm import LLMError
from mathfinder.search import search

RUNS_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / "runs"

SYMBOL = {"lemma_proved": "✓", "lemma_refuted": "✗", "lemma_stuck": "…"}


def print_event(event: dict) -> None:
    kind = event["kind"]
    if event.get("depth", 0) > 0:  # events from a sub-search are indented under their lemma
        indent = "        " * event["depth"]
        if kind in SYMBOL:
            print(f"{indent}{SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
        elif kind == "plan_start":
            print(f"{indent}▶ sub-plan: {event['sketch']}")
        elif kind == "done":
            print(f"{indent}✓ sub-search proved it")
        sys.stdout.flush()
        return
    if kind == "start":
        print(f"\nTheorem: {event['theorem']}")
    elif kind == "subsearch_start":
        print(f"      ↳ {event['name']} is stuck: starting a sub-search on it")
    elif kind == "refuted":
        print(f"✗ The statement is FALSE. Counterexample: {event['counterexample']}")
    elif kind == "round":
        print(f"\n— round {event['number']}: asking the planner —")
    elif kind == "plan_rejected":
        print(f"  ✗ plan doesn't fit together: {event['sketch']}\n      {event['reason'][:160]}")
    elif kind == "plan_start":
        print(f"  ▶ plan: {event['sketch']}  (lemmas: {', '.join(event['lemmas']) or 'none'})")
    elif kind in SYMBOL:
        print(f"      {SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
    elif kind == "plan_failed":
        print(f"    plan failed (score {event['score']:.2f})")
    elif kind == "done":
        print(f"\n✓ VERIFIED by Lean. Plan: {event['plan']}\n")
        print(event["lean_code"])
    elif kind == "gave_up":
        print("\nNo verified proof found.")
    sys.stdout.flush()  # show progress immediately, even when output is piped to a file


def approve(latex: str, auto_yes: bool = False, ask=input) -> str | None:
    """Formalize `latex`, show it, and return the statement the user approved (None = no)."""
    print("Formalizing (LaTeX → Lean)...")
    f = formalize(latex)
    if not f.ok:
        print("Could not produce a statement Lean accepts:\n  " + f.errors[0][:400])
        return None
    print(f"\nFormal statement:  theorem main {f.statement}")
    print(f"Back-translation:  {f.back_translation}")
    print(f"Judge:             {'same meaning' if f.faithful else 'MEANING MAY DIFFER'}: {f.judgment}")

    statement = f.statement
    if auto_yes and f.faithful:
        return statement
    if auto_yes:
        print("(--yes ignored: the judge thinks the meaning may differ, so please look.)")
    while True:
        try:
            answer = ask("\nProve this statement? [y]es / [n]o / [e]dit: ").strip().lower()
        except EOFError:  # no keyboard attached (e.g. running in a script)
            return None
        if answer == "y":
            return statement
        if answer == "n":
            return None
        if answer == "e":
            new = ask("Lean statement (what comes after `theorem main`): ").strip()
            errors = statement_errors(new)
            if errors:
                print("Lean rejected it: " + errors[0][:300])
            else:
                statement = new
                print(f"Now:  theorem main {statement}")


def save_run(theorem: str, lean_code: str, explanation: str) -> Path:
    """Save a proved theorem as runs/<date-time>/{theorem.txt, proof.lean, explanation.md}."""
    folder = RUNS_DIR / datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d-%H%M%S")
    folder.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    (folder / "theorem.txt").write_text(theorem + "\n")
    (folder / "proof.lean").write_text(HEADER + lean_code)  # a complete file Lean can check on its own
    (folder / "explanation.md").write_text(explanation + "\n")
    return folder


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="mathfinder")
    commands = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    prove = commands.add_parser("prove", help="search for a proof")
    prove.add_argument("latex", nargs="?", help="the theorem in LaTeX (in quotes)")
    prove.add_argument("--lean", help='skip formalization: a Lean statement, e.g. "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"')
    prove.add_argument("--yes", action="store_true", help="don't ask for approval if the judge is happy")
    prove.add_argument("--hint", action="append", default=[], help="a hint for the planner (repeatable)")
    prove.add_argument("--rounds", type=int, default=3)
    prove.add_argument("--no-explain", action="store_true", help="skip the plain-math explanation")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    if args.command == "prove":
        if args.lean:
            statement = args.lean
        elif args.latex:
            statement = approve(args.latex, args.yes)
            if statement is None:
                print("Not searching.")
                return 1
        else:
            parser.error("give a theorem in LaTeX, or --lean STATEMENT")
        result = search(statement, hints=args.hint, rounds=args.rounds, on_event=print_event)
        print(f"\nResult: {'PROVED' if result.proved else 'not proved'} ({result.message})")
        if not result.proved:
            return 1
        explanation = ""
        if not args.no_explain:
            print("\nWriting the explanation...")
            try:
                explanation = explain(args.latex or statement, result.plan, result.lean_code)
                print("\n" + explanation)
            except LLMError as e:  # the proof is still valid; only the write-up failed
                print(f"(could not write an explanation: {e})")
        print(f"\nSaved to {save_run(args.latex or statement, result.lean_code, explanation)}")
        return 0
    return 2

    What changed: new imports, RUNS_DIR, a new function save_run, a new --no-explain flag, and the end of main, which explains, prints, and saves after a successful search.

    save_run makes a folder named after the current date and time (datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d-%H%M%S") gives e.g. 20261114-144642). proof.lean gets the same HEADER Lean used, so it's a complete file you can check by itself. Path objects join with /: folder / "proof.lean".

    The explanation is optional. If the model fails (LLMError), we print a note and still save the proof. A verified proof is the valuable part and must never be lost because a write-up failed.

  4. Update the command line tests. Replace the whole file:

    tests/test_cli.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/cli.py: approval, explanation and saving. Lean and the model are faked."""

import pytest

from mathfinder import cli
from mathfinder.formalize import Formalization
from mathfinder.llm import LLMError
from mathfinder.planner import Plan
from mathfinder.search import SearchResult


@pytest.fixture
def fake_formalize(monkeypatch):
    def install(faithful=True):
        result = Formalization("latex", "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n", "For all integers n, 6 | n^3 - n.", faithful, "ok")
        monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "formalize", lambda latex: result)

    return install


def scripted_user(*answers):
    """Stands in for input(): gives the answers in order."""
    queue = list(answers)
    return lambda question: queue.pop(0)


def test_yes_returns_the_statement(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize()
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=scripted_user("y")) == "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"


def test_no_returns_none(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize()
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=scripted_user("n")) is None


def test_edit_is_checked_by_lean_before_it_is_used(fake_formalize, monkeypatch):
    fake_formalize()
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "statement_errors", lambda s: ["unknown identifier 'oops'"] if "oops" in s else [])
    user = scripted_user("e", "(n : ℤ) : oops", "e", "(n : ℕ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n", "y")
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=user) == "(n : ℕ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"


def test_auto_yes_only_when_the_judge_agrees(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize(faithful=True)
    assert cli.approve("latex", auto_yes=True, ask=scripted_user()) is not None  # never asks
    fake_formalize(faithful=False)
    assert cli.approve("latex", auto_yes=True, ask=scripted_user("n")) is None  # asks anyway


def test_no_keyboard_means_no(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize()

    def no_keyboard(question):
        raise EOFError

    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=no_keyboard) is None


def test_main_with_latex_and_yes_runs_the_search_explains_and_saves(fake_formalize, monkeypatch, tmp_path):
    fake_formalize()
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "RUNS_DIR", tmp_path)
    seen = {}

    def fake_search(statement, hints, rounds, on_event):
        seen["statement"] = statement
        return SearchResult(True, "theorem main ... := by\n  decide\n", Plan("check all cases", [], "decide"))

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "search", fake_search)
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "explain", lambda theorem, plan, code: "## Theorem\nexplained")
    latex = r"For every integer $n$, $6 \mid n^3 - n$."
    assert cli.main(["prove", latex, "--yes"]) == 0
    assert seen["statement"] == "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"

    [run] = tmp_path.iterdir()  # exactly one run folder was created
    assert (run / "theorem.txt").read_text() == latex + "\n"
    assert (run / "proof.lean").read_text().startswith("import Mathlib")
    assert (run / "explanation.md").read_text() == "## Theorem\nexplained\n"


def test_a_failed_explanation_still_saves_the_proof(monkeypatch, tmp_path, capsys):
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "RUNS_DIR", tmp_path)
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "search", lambda *a, **k: SearchResult(True, "code\n", Plan("p", [], "simp")))

    def broken_explain(theorem, plan, code):
        raise LLMError("model unavailable")

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "explain", broken_explain)
    assert cli.main(["prove", "--lean", "(n : ℕ) : n = n"]) == 0
    assert "could not write an explanation" in capsys.readouterr().out
    assert len(list(tmp_path.iterdir())) == 1
