    tests/test_search.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/search.py and mathfinder/cli.py."""

import json

import pytest

from mathfinder import cli, llm, planner, prover
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma, Plan
from mathfinder.search import SearchResult, assemble, score, search, splice

ODD_SUM = "(n : ℕ) : ∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2"
INDUCTION = "induction n with\n| zero => simp\n| succ k ih =>\n  rw [Finset.sum_range_succ, ih]\n  ring"


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def temp_cache(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(prover, "CACHE_FILE", tmp_path / "lemmas.json")


# ---------- fast tests ----------


def test_assemble_builds_one_namespaced_file():
    plan = Plan("idea", [Lemma("key", "(a : ℝ) : 0 ≤ a ^ 2", "", status="proved", proof="positivity")], "nlinarith [key a]")
    assert assemble("(a : ℝ) : a ^ 2 ≥ 0", plan) == (
        "namespace MathFinder\n\n"
        "theorem key (a : ℝ) : 0 ≤ a ^ 2 := by\n  positivity\n\n"
        "theorem main (a : ℝ) : a ^ 2 ≥ 0 := by\n  nlinarith [key a]\n\n"
        "end MathFinder\n"
    )


def test_score_is_fraction_proved_and_refuted_is_worst():
    proved = Lemma("a", ": True", "", status="proved")
    stuck = Lemma("b", ": True", "", status="stuck")
    refuted = Lemma("c", ": True", "", status="refuted")
    assert score(Plan("", [proved, stuck], "")) == 0.5
    assert score(Plan("", [proved, refuted], "")) == -1.0


def test_splice_inserts_renamed_sub_lemmas_before_the_stuck_lemma():
    first = Lemma("first", ": True", "", status="proved", proof="trivial")
    stuck = Lemma("hard", "(n : ℕ) : P n", "", status="stuck")
    plan = Plan("", [first, stuck], "exact hard")
    sub = Plan("", [Lemma("step", "(k : ℕ) : Q k", "", status="proved", proof="simp")], "exact step n")

    assert splice(plan, stuck, sub) == 1
    assert [l.name for l in plan.lemmas] == ["first", "hard_step", "hard"]
    assert stuck.status == "proved" and stuck.proof == "exact hard_step n"


def test_cli_passes_arguments_to_search(monkeypatch, capsys, tmp_path):
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "RUNS_DIR", tmp_path)  # don't write into the real runs/ folder
    seen = {}

    def fake_search(theorem, hints, rounds, on_event):
        seen.update(theorem=theorem, hints=hints, rounds=rounds)
        on_event({"kind": "start", "theorem": theorem})
        return SearchResult(True, message="verified")

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "search", fake_search)
    code = cli.main(["prove", "--lean", "(n : ℕ) : n = n", "--hint", "use rfl", "--rounds", "1", "--no-explain"])
    assert code == 0
    assert seen == {"theorem": "(n : ℕ) : n = n", "hints": ["use rfl"], "rounds": 1}
    assert "Result: PROVED" in capsys.readouterr().out


def test_cli_indents_sub_search_events(capsys):
    cli.print_event({"kind": "subsearch_start", "depth": 0, "name": "hard", "statement": ": P"})
    cli.print_event({"kind": "lemma_proved", "depth": 1, "name": "step", "statement": ": Q", "feedback": "hammer: simp"})
    out = capsys.readouterr().out.splitlines()
    assert out[0] == "      ↳ hard is stuck: starting a sub-search on it"
    assert out[1] == "        ✓ step : : Q   [hammer: simp]"


# ---------- Lean tests ----------


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_false_theorem_is_refuted_before_any_planning(monkeypatch):
    def no_model(system, prompt):
        raise AssertionError("the model should not be called for a false statement")

    monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", no_model)
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
    events = []
    result = search("(n : ℕ) : n ^ 2 ≤ 2 * n + 3", on_event=events.append)
    assert not result.proved
    assert "false" in result.message
    assert [e["kind"] for e in events] == ["start", "refuted"]


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_full_search_with_scripted_model(monkeypatch):
    """Plan → skeleton check → hammer proves one lemma, 'the model' proves the other → final check."""
    theorem = "(n : ℕ) : (∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2) ∧ n ≤ n ^ 2"
    plan_json = {"plans": [{
        "sketch": "Prove both halves separately.",
        "lemmas": [
            {"name": "odd_sum", "statement": ODD_SUM, "informal": "sum of odd numbers"},
            {"name": "le_sq", "statement": "(n : ℕ) : n ≤ n ^ 2", "informal": "n ≤ n²"},
        ],
        "main_proof": "exact ⟨odd_sum n, le_sq n⟩",
    }]}

    def scripted(system, prompt):
        if system == planner.SYSTEM:
            return "```json\n" + json.dumps(plan_json) + "\n```"
        return "```json\n" + json.dumps({"proofs": [INDUCTION]}) + "\n```"

    monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", scripted)
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
    events = []
    result = search(theorem, rounds=1, on_event=events.append)

    assert result.proved, result.message
    assert "theorem main" in result.lean_code and "sorry" not in result.lean_code
    kinds = [e["kind"] for e in events]
    assert kinds[-1] == "done"
    assert kinds.count("lemma_proved") == 2


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_stuck_lemma_is_proved_by_a_sub_search(monkeypatch):
    """The 'model' can't prove odd_sum directly, so a sub-search splits it into a smaller step."""
    theorem = "(n : ℕ) : (∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2) ∧ n ≤ n ^ 2"
    top_plan = {"plans": [{
        "sketch": "Prove both halves separately.",
        "lemmas": [
            {"name": "odd_sum", "statement": ODD_SUM, "informal": ""},
            {"name": "le_sq", "statement": "(n : ℕ) : n ≤ n ^ 2", "informal": ""},
        ],
        "main_proof": "exact ⟨odd_sum n, le_sq n⟩",
    }]}
    sub_plan = {"plans": [{
        "sketch": "Induction, using the step (k+1)^2 = k^2 + (2k+1).",
        "lemmas": [{"name": "step", "statement": "(k : ℕ) : (k + 1) ^ 2 = k ^ 2 + (2 * k + 1)", "informal": ""}],
        "main_proof": "induction n with\n| zero => simp\n| succ k ih =>\n  rw [Finset.sum_range_succ, ih, step k]",
    }]}

    def scripted(system, prompt):
        if system == planner.SYSTEM:
            chosen = top_plan if "∧" in prompt else sub_plan
            return "```json\n" + json.dumps(chosen) + "\n```"
        return "```json\n" + json.dumps({"proofs": ["simp"]}) + "\n```"  # the prover always fails

    monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", scripted)
    monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
    monkeypatch.setattr(prover, "REPAIR_ROUNDS", 0)  # fail fast: one LLM round per lemma
    events = []
    result = search(theorem, rounds=1, on_event=events.append)

    assert result.proved, result.message
    assert "theorem odd_sum_step" in result.lean_code  # the sub-lemma, renamed and spliced in
    assert any(e["kind"] == "subsearch_start" for e in events)
    assert any(e["kind"] == "done" and e["depth"] == 1 for e in events)

    What changed: test_cli_passes_arguments_to_search sets cli.RUNS_DIR to a temporary folder and passes --no-explain. Without that, its fake "successful" search would trigger a real explanation call.

  6. Run the tests, then one real explanation (about 10 s):

    uv run pytest -q -m "not lean and not llm"
uv run pytest -q -m llm tests/test_explain.py

  7. Prove, explain and save a theorem:

    uv run python -m mathfinder prove 'For every integer $n$, $6 \mid n^3 - n$.' --yes

Checkpoint

After the verified Lean proof, we got (about 1½ minutes in total; your wording will differ):

Writing the explanation...

## Theorem
For every integer $n$,
$$6 \mid n^3 - n.$$

## Idea
Divisibility by $6$ means the remainder modulo $6$ is $0$. The remainder of $n^3 - n$ modulo $6$ depends only
on the remainder $r$ of $n$ modulo $6$. So it suffices to check the six possible values $r = 0, 1, \dots, 5$.

## Proof

1. **Step 1** (`mod_six_cases`). For every integer $n$, the remainder $n \bmod 6$ is one of $0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5$.
   ...
2. **Conclusion** (`main`). The proof first rewrites the goal $6 \mid n^3 - n$ into ... $(n^3 - n) \bmod 6 = 0$ ...
   | $r$ | $r^3 - r$ | $\bmod 6$ |
   ...
Saved to /Users/you/Desktop/math_proofs/runs/20261114-144642

Check the saved proof again, by itself, with Lean (no output means it's correct):

cp runs/20261114-144642/proof.lean lean_env/.mf_tmp/saved_check.lean
cd lean_env && lake env lean .mf_tmp/saved_check.lean && echo OK; cd ..
OK

Use your own folder name from the "Saved to" line. We copy the file into lean_env because lake env lean must run inside the Lean project to find Mathlib.

Fast tests:

................................................                         [100%]
48 passed, 28 deselected in 0.07s

Common errors

  • The explanation prints but Saved to never appears: an exception happened in save_run. Check you can write to the project folder.
  • Math shows as raw $...$: that's expected in the terminal. The website (M10) renders it, and any Markdown viewer with math support shows it nicely.
  • Tests create folders in your real runs/: a test is missing monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "RUNS_DIR", tmp_path).

M7: Working together

What and why. So far you hand over a theorem and wait. But you often know something the model doesn't: "try induction", "that lemma is false", "use these three squares". This chapter makes the search a partner. A Session is a shared notebook between you and the search. You can write in it at any time: add a hint, suggest your own lemma, reject a lemma, pin an approach you like, pause, resume, or stop. The search reads it at checkpoints (before every round, plan and lemma). And when the search runs out of ideas, it doesn't quietly give up: it asks you a specific question ("I can't prove this step: ... Lean said: ..."), and your answer buys it another round.

Two things happening at once: threads

The search and your typing must run at the same time, so they run in two threads: two flows of work inside one program. That needs some care when both touch the same data. This chapter uses three standard tools from Python's library:

  • Lock: with self._lock: lets only one thread at a time inside that block, so the search never reads the hints list while you're halfway through adding to it.
  • Event: a flag threads can wait on. pause() clears it, resume() sets it, and the search's checkpoint() calls wait(), which simply blocks while the flag is cleared.
  • Queue: a thread-safe mailbox. ask() waits on queue.get(timeout=...) until answer() puts something in.

  1. Create the session.

    mathfinder/collab.py
    """Working together: a Session is the shared notebook between you and the search.

The search runs in one thread; you (the terminal, or later the web page) act
from another. You can change the session at ANY time and the search picks the
change up at its next checkpoint (before each round, plan and lemma):

  add_hint("try induction on n")        - free text, given to the planner with top priority
  add_lemma("key", "(a : ℝ) : 0 ≤ a^2") - a lemma YOU think helps (it still gets proved)
  reject_lemma("bad_step")              - "this lemma is false / useless": plans using it are dropped
  pin("induction on n")                 - "keep going in this direction"
  pause() / resume()                    - the search waits at its next checkpoint
  edit_statement(...) / stop()          - end this search (the caller can start a new one)

And the search can ASK you: ask(question) waits until you call answer(...).
"""

import queue
import threading

from mathfinder.planner import Lemma, Plan


def normalize(statement: str) -> str:
    return " ".join(statement.split())


class Session:
    def __init__(self, theorem: str, answer_timeout: float = 600):
        self.theorem = theorem
        self.answer_timeout = answer_timeout  # seconds the search waits for your answer
        self.question = None  # the question currently waiting for an answer, if any
        self._hints = []
        self._rejected = set()  # lemma names and normalized statements
        self._lock = threading.Lock()  # only one thread at a time may change the lists above
        self._running = threading.Event()  # "set" = not paused
        self._running.set()
        self._stop_reason = None
        self._answers = queue.Queue()

    # ---------- things the user does ----------

    def add_hint(self, text: str) -> None:
        with self._lock:
            self._hints.append(text.strip())

    def add_lemma(self, name: str, statement: str, informal: str = "") -> None:
        self.add_hint(
            f"The user suggests this lemma; use it if it helps (it must still be proved): "
            f"theorem {name} {statement}" + (f"  -- {informal}" if informal else "")
        )

    def reject_lemma(self, name_or_statement: str) -> None:
        with self._lock:
            self._rejected.add(normalize(name_or_statement))
            self._hints.append(f"Do NOT use this lemma, the user says it is false or useless: {name_or_statement}")

    def pin(self, approach: str) -> None:
        self.add_hint(f"The user likes this approach; keep developing it: {approach}")

    def pause(self) -> None:
        self._running.clear()

    def resume(self) -> None:
        self._running.set()

    def stop(self, reason: str = "stopped by the user") -> None:
        self._stop_reason = reason
        self._running.set()  # wake the search if it is paused, so it can notice the stop
        self._answers.put(None)  # and if it is waiting for an answer

    def edit_statement(self, new_statement: str) -> None:
        self.theorem = new_statement
        self.stop("the statement was edited")

    def answer(self, text: str) -> None:
        self._answers.put(text)

    # ---------- things the search does ----------

    def hints(self) -> list[str]:
        with self._lock:
            return list(self._hints)  # a copy, so the caller can't change ours by accident

    def is_rejected(self, lemma: Lemma) -> bool:
        with self._lock:
            return lemma.name in self._rejected or normalize(lemma.statement) in self._rejected

    def uses_rejected(self, plan: Plan) -> str | None:
        """The name of the first rejected lemma this plan uses, or None."""
        for lemma in plan.lemmas:
            if self.is_rejected(lemma):
                return lemma.name
        return None

    def checkpoint(self) -> str | None:
        """Wait while paused. Returns a reason if the search should stop now, else None."""
        self._running.wait()
        return self._stop_reason

    def ask(self, question: str) -> str | None:
        """Wait for the user's answer (None after answer_timeout seconds, or if stopped)."""
        self.question = question
        try:
            answer = self._answers.get(timeout=self.answer_timeout)
        except queue.Empty:
            answer = None
        self.question = None
        return answer

    Everything becomes planner hints. A suggested lemma becomes the hint "The user suggests this lemma ... (it must still be proved)", so your lemma can never skip Lean's check. A pin becomes "keep developing this approach". A rejection does two things: it tells the planner not to use the lemma, and uses_rejected drops any plan that uses it anyway, matched by name or by statement with spacing normalized.

    stop() also sets the running flag and puts None in the answer queue. A paused search, or one waiting for your answer, would otherwise never notice the stop. edit_statement stops the search with a reason; the caller (the website, in M9) starts a new search on the new statement.

    hints() returns list(self._hints), a copy, so the search can't be surprised by the list changing under it.

  2. Teach the search to use a session. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/search.py
    """The search: plan, check, prove, verify, and replan with what we learned.

  0. maybe the hammer proves the whole theorem directly (or refutes it)
  1. the planner proposes a few plans
  2. the critic throws out plans that don't fit together (one Lean run)
  3. remaining plans are tried cheapest-first (fewest lemmas); each lemma goes to
     the prover, and a refuted lemma kills its plan immediately
  4. a plan with every lemma proved is assembled into one file and checked by
     the strict final verification (no sorry, only standard axioms)
  5. otherwise everything that went wrong becomes feedback for the next round

A lemma the prover gets stuck on becomes a smaller search of its own (a
"sub-search", at most MAX_DEPTH levels deep). If the sub-search proves it, its
helper lemmas are spliced into the parent plan.

With a collab.Session, you work together: your hints, lemmas and rejections are
read every round, pause/stop are honoured at checkpoints, and before giving up
the search ASKS you a specific question (your answer buys another round).

Everything that happens is reported through `on_event`, so the command line,
the web page and the trace logger can all watch the same search.
"""

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass

from mathfinder.critic import check_skeletons
from mathfinder.hammer import hammer
from mathfinder.lean import verify_final
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma, Plan, propose_plans
from mathfinder.prover import prove_lemma

NAMESPACE = "MathFinder"
MAX_DEPTH = 1  # a stuck lemma may get a sub-search; a stuck lemma inside that may not
MAX_QUESTIONS = 2  # how many times the search may ask the user before giving up


@dataclass
class SearchResult:
    proved: bool
    lean_code: str = ""  # the complete verified proof (everything after the file header)
    plan: Plan | None = None
    rounds: int = 0
    message: str = ""


def assemble(theorem: str, plan: Plan) -> str:
    """One Lean file: every lemma with its real proof, then the main theorem."""
    blocks = [f"namespace {NAMESPACE}"]
    for l in plan.lemmas:
        body = "\n".join("  " + line for line in l.proof.split("\n"))
        blocks.append(f"theorem {l.name} {l.statement} := by\n{body}")
    main_body = "\n".join("  " + line for line in plan.main_proof.split("\n"))
    blocks.append(f"theorem main {theorem} := by\n{main_body}")
    blocks.append(f"end {NAMESPACE}")
    return "\n\n".join(blocks) + "\n"


def score(plan: Plan) -> float:
    """Fraction of lemmas proved; a refuted lemma makes the whole plan worthless."""
    if any(l.status == "refuted" for l in plan.lemmas):
        return -1.0
    if not plan.lemmas:
        return 0.0
    return sum(l.status == "proved" for l in plan.lemmas) / len(plan.lemmas)


def summarize(plan: Plan) -> str:
    """What happened to one plan, written for the planner's next round."""
    lines = [f"Plan: {plan.sketch}  (main proof: {plan.main_proof!r})"]
    lines += [f"  - {note}" for note in plan.feedback]
    for l in plan.lemmas:
        lines.append(f"  - lemma {l.name} {l.statement}: {l.status.upper()}  {l.feedback[:300]}")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def splice(plan: Plan, lemma: Lemma, sub: Plan) -> int:
    """Put a sub-search's lemmas into `plan` just before `lemma`, and use the sub-search's
    main proof as `lemma`'s proof. Sub-lemmas are renamed `<lemma>_<name>` so names stay
    unique. Returns how many lemmas were inserted."""
    renames = {l.name: f"{lemma.name}_{l.name}" for l in sub.lemmas}

    def rename(text: str) -> str:
        for old, new in renames.items():
            text = re.sub(rf"\b{re.escape(old)}\b", new, text)  # \b = whole words only
        return text

    inserted = [
        Lemma(renames[l.name], rename(l.statement), l.informal, "proved", rename(l.proof), "from sub-search")
        for l in sub.lemmas
    ]
    i = plan.lemmas.index(lemma)
    plan.lemmas[i:i] = inserted  # slice assignment: insert the list at position i
    lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", rename(sub.main_proof), "proved by a sub-search"
    return len(inserted)


def prove_plan(plan: Plan, emit, on_event=None, hints=(), depth: int = 0, session=None) -> bool:
    """Prove the plan's lemmas in order. Stops at the first refuted lemma.
    A stuck lemma gets a sub-search while depth < MAX_DEPTH."""
    i = 0
    while i < len(plan.lemmas):  # a while loop, because splice() can insert lemmas
        if session and session.checkpoint():  # paused? wait here. stopped? give up this plan
            return False
        lemma = plan.lemmas[i]
        emit("lemma_start", name=lemma.name, statement=lemma.statement)
        prove_lemma(lemma, plan.lemmas[:i])
        if lemma.status == "stuck" and depth < MAX_DEPTH:
            emit("subsearch_start", name=lemma.name, statement=lemma.statement)
            sub = search(lemma.statement, hints, rounds=1, on_event=on_event, depth=depth + 1, session=session)
            if sub.proved:
                i += splice(plan, lemma, sub.plan)  # skip past the inserted (already proved) lemmas
        emit("lemma_" + lemma.status, name=lemma.name, statement=lemma.statement, feedback=lemma.feedback)
        if lemma.status == "refuted":
            return False
        i += 1
    return all(l.status == "proved" for l in plan.lemmas)


def build_question(theorem: str, best: Plan | None) -> str:
    """A specific question for the user: the step we're stuck on, and what Lean said."""
    stuck = [l for l in best.lemmas if l.status == "stuck"] if best else []
    if stuck:
        l = stuck[0]
        return (
            f"I can't prove this step:\n  {l.name} : {l.statement}\n"
            f"Lean said: {l.feedback[:300]}\n"
            "Any idea how to prove it, or a different approach to the whole theorem?"
        )
    return f"I couldn't find a proof of\n  {theorem}\nWhich approach would you try?"


def try_direct(theorem: str, emit) -> SearchResult | None:
    """Step 0: maybe automation proves (or refutes) the whole theorem."""
    hit = hammer(theorem)
    if hit.counterexample:
        emit("refuted", counterexample=hit.counterexample)
        return SearchResult(False, message=f"the statement is false: {hit.counterexample}")
    if hit.proved:
        plan = Plan(f"Automation: {hit.tactic}", [], hit.proof)
        code = assemble(theorem, plan)
        if verify_final(code, f"{NAMESPACE}.main").ok:
            emit("done", lean_code=code, plan=plan.sketch)
            return SearchResult(True, code, plan, 0, "proved directly by automation")
    return None


def search(
    theorem: str,
    hints=(),
    rounds: int = 3,
    plans_per_round: int = 2,
    on_event=None,
    depth: int = 0,
    session=None,
) -> SearchResult:
    """Search for a verified proof of `theorem` (a Lean statement without a name).
    `depth` is 0 for the user's theorem, 1 for a sub-search on a stuck lemma, ...
    `session` (optional) is a collab.Session shared with the user."""

    def emit(kind, **data):
        if on_event:
            on_event({"kind": kind, "depth": depth, **data})

    def stop_reason():
        reason = session.checkpoint() if session else None
        if reason:
            emit("stopped", reason=reason)
        return reason

    emit("start", theorem=theorem)
    if depth == 0:  # in a sub-search, the prover has already run the hammer on this statement
        direct = try_direct(theorem, emit)
        if direct:
            return direct

    feedback = []  # what went wrong so far, fed to the planner
    best = None
    r = 0
    questions = 0
    while True:
        if r == rounds:  # out of rounds: ask the user before giving up
            if session is None or depth > 0 or questions == MAX_QUESTIONS:
                break
            question = build_question(theorem, best)
            emit("ask_user", question=question)
            questions += 1
            answer = session.ask(question)
            if not answer:
                emit("no_answer")
                break
            session.add_hint(answer)
            emit("user_answer", text=answer)
            rounds += 1  # the answer buys one more round
        r += 1
        if stop_reason():
            return SearchResult(False, plan=best, rounds=r, message=f"stopped: {session.checkpoint()}")

        emit("round", number=r)
        all_hints = list(hints) + (session.hints() if session else [])
        plans = propose_plans(theorem, plans_per_round, all_hints, "\n".join(feedback[-6:]))
        emit("plans", sketches=[p.sketch for p in plans])
        problems = check_skeletons(theorem, plans)

        fitting = []
        for plan, found in zip(plans, problems):
            rejected = session.uses_rejected(plan) if session else None
            if found:
                emit("plan_rejected", sketch=plan.sketch, reason=found[0][:300])
                feedback.append(summarize(plan))
            elif rejected:
                emit("plan_rejected", sketch=plan.sketch, reason=f"uses {rejected}, which you rejected")
            else:
                fitting.append(plan)

        for plan in sorted(fitting, key=lambda p: len(p.lemmas)):  # cheapest first
            if stop_reason():
                return SearchResult(False, plan=best, rounds=r, message=f"stopped: {session.checkpoint()}")
            emit("plan_start", sketch=plan.sketch, lemmas=[l.name for l in plan.lemmas])
            if prove_plan(plan, emit, on_event, all_hints, depth, session):
                code = assemble(theorem, plan)
                final = verify_final(code, f"{NAMESPACE}.main")
                if final.ok:
                    emit("done", lean_code=code, plan=plan.sketch)
                    return SearchResult(True, code, plan, r, "verified")
                plan.feedback.append("final check failed: " + " | ".join(final.errors)[:400])
            emit("plan_failed", sketch=plan.sketch, score=score(plan))
            feedback.append(summarize(plan))
            if best is None or score(plan) > score(best):
                best = plan

    if session and session.checkpoint():
        emit("stopped", reason=session.checkpoint())
        return SearchResult(False, plan=best, rounds=r, message=f"stopped: {session.checkpoint()}")
    emit("gave_up", best=best.sketch if best else None)
    message = f"no verified proof after {r} rounds"
    if best:
        message += f"; closest plan proved {score(best):.0%} of its lemmas"
    return SearchResult(False, plan=best, rounds=r, message=message)

    What changed:

    • search takes session=None. Each round it plans with hints + session.hints(), so a hint you type mid-search is used from the next round. Plans that use a rejected lemma are dropped with the reason "uses X, which you rejected".
    • stop_reason() is called before every round and plan, and prove_plan checks before every lemma. That's where a pause waits and a stop ends the search.
    • The for loop over rounds became while True, with a counter r. When r reaches rounds, it builds a question with build_question, sends it as an ask_user event and waits. A real answer becomes a hint, and rounds += 1 extends the loop by one. That's why it couldn't stay a for loop over a fixed range. It asks at most MAX_QUESTIONS times, and only at depth 0 (sub-searches never ask).
    • build_question picks the first stuck lemma of the best plan and quotes Lean's error, so the question is specific enough to answer.

  3. Create the collaboration tests.

    tests/test_collab.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/collab.py and how search() works with a Session.

Lean and the model are replaced by fakes, so these run instantly. Threads play
the user acting while the search runs.
"""

import threading
import time
from types import SimpleNamespace

import pytest

from mathfinder import search as search_module
from mathfinder.collab import Session
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma, Plan

# ---------- the Session on its own ----------


def test_hints_lemmas_and_pins_become_planner_hints():
    s = Session("(n : ℕ) : P n")
    s.add_hint("try induction on n")
    s.add_lemma("key", "(n : ℕ) : Q n", "Q always holds")
    s.pin("strong induction")
    hints = s.hints()
    assert hints[0] == "try induction on n"
    assert "theorem key (n : ℕ) : Q n  -- Q always holds" in hints[1]
    assert "strong induction" in hints[2]


def test_rejected_lemma_is_found_by_name_or_statement():
    s = Session("t")
    s.reject_lemma("bad")
    s.reject_lemma("(x : ℝ)   :  x = 0")  # spacing doesn't matter
    good = Plan("", [Lemma("fine", ": True", "")], "")
    by_name = Plan("", [Lemma("bad", ": True", "")], "")
    by_statement = Plan("", [Lemma("other", "(x : ℝ) : x = 0", "")], "")
    assert s.uses_rejected(good) is None
    assert s.uses_rejected(by_name) == "bad"
    assert s.uses_rejected(by_statement) == "other"


def test_pause_blocks_the_checkpoint_until_resume():
    s = Session("t")
    s.pause()
    passed = threading.Event()
    threading.Thread(target=lambda: (s.checkpoint(), passed.set()), daemon=True).start()
    time.sleep(0.2)
    assert not passed.is_set()  # still waiting
    s.resume()
    assert passed.wait(2)


def test_ask_waits_for_an_answer_from_another_thread():
    s = Session("t")
    threading.Timer(0.1, lambda: s.answer("use AM-GM")).start()
    assert s.ask("Any idea?") == "use AM-GM"
    assert s.question is None


def test_ask_gives_up_after_the_timeout():
    assert Session("t", answer_timeout=0.1).ask("Any idea?") is None


def test_stop_wakes_a_paused_search():
    s = Session("t")
    s.pause()
    s.stop()
    assert s.checkpoint() == "stopped by the user"


# ---------- search() with a session (fake Lean, fake model) ----------


@pytest.fixture
def fake_world(monkeypatch):
    """Lemmas named 'good*' get proved, 'hard*' get stuck. The planner is scripted per round."""
    seen_hints = []

    def install(plans_per_round):
        rounds = list(plans_per_round)

        def fake_propose(theorem, n, hints, feedback):
            seen_hints.append(list(hints))
            return rounds.pop(0) if rounds else []

        def fake_prove(lemma, earlier):
            lemma.status = "proved" if lemma.name.startswith("good") else "stuck"
            lemma.proof = "simp" if lemma.status == "proved" else ""
            lemma.feedback = "" if lemma.status == "proved" else "simp made no progress"
            return lemma

        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "try_direct", lambda theorem, emit: None)
        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "propose_plans", fake_propose)
        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "check_skeletons", lambda t, plans: [[] for _ in plans])
        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "prove_lemma", fake_prove)
        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "verify_final", lambda code, name: SimpleNamespace(ok=True, errors=[]))
        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "MAX_DEPTH", 0)  # no sub-searches in these tests
        return seen_hints

    return install


def plan(*names):
    return Plan(f"plan with {', '.join(names)}", [Lemma(n, f"(n : ℕ) : {n} n", "") for n in names], "simp")


def test_session_hints_reach_the_planner_and_rejected_plans_are_dropped(fake_world):
    seen_hints = fake_world([[plan("bad_idea"), plan("good_a")]])
    session = Session("t")
    session.add_hint("use induction")
    session.reject_lemma("bad_idea")
    events = []
    result = search_module.search("t", rounds=1, on_event=events.append, session=session)
    assert result.proved
    assert "use induction" in seen_hints[0]
    rejected = [e for e in events if e["kind"] == "plan_rejected"]
    assert rejected[0]["reason"] == "uses bad_idea, which you rejected"


def test_search_asks_the_user_and_the_answer_buys_another_round(fake_world):
    seen_hints = fake_world([[plan("hard_step")], [plan("good_b")]])
    session = Session("t")
    events = []

    def user_watches(event):
        events.append(event)
        if event["kind"] == "ask_user":  # answer from "another person", a moment later
            threading.Timer(0.1, lambda: session.answer("split it into two cases")).start()

    result = search_module.search("t", rounds=1, on_event=user_watches, session=session)
    assert result.proved and result.rounds == 2
    question = next(e["question"] for e in events if e["kind"] == "ask_user")
    assert "hard_step" in question and "simp made no progress" in question
    assert "split it into two cases" in seen_hints[1]  # the planner saw the answer in round 2


def test_no_answer_means_giving_up(fake_world):
    fake_world([[plan("hard_step")]])
    session = Session("t", answer_timeout=0.1)
    events = []
    result = search_module.search("t", rounds=1, on_event=events.append, session=session)
    assert not result.proved
    kinds = [e["kind"] for e in events]
    assert kinds[-3:] == ["ask_user", "no_answer", "gave_up"]


def test_editing_the_statement_stops_the_search(fake_world):
    fake_world([[plan("good_a")]])
    session = Session("t")
    session.edit_statement("(n : ℕ) : n = n")
    result = search_module.search("t", rounds=1, session=session)
    assert not result.proved
    assert result.message == "stopped: the statement was edited"
    assert session.theorem == "(n : ℕ) : n = n"

    The Session tests use real threads: threading.Timer(0.1, f) runs f in a new thread after 0.1 s, playing "the user answers a moment later". The search tests replace Lean and the model with fakes using monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, ...), so the whole ask → answer → extra round → proved story runs in under a second. Lemma names decide the fake outcome: good* gets proved, hard* gets stuck.

  4. Add the interactive mode to the command line. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/cli.py
    """Command line.

  uv run python -m mathfinder prove "For every integer $n$, $6 \\mid n^3 - n$."
  uv run python -m mathfinder prove --lean "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"

With LaTeX, you first see the Lean statement, its back-translation and the
judge's opinion, and nothing is searched until you approve.
Every proved theorem is saved in runs/<date-time>/ as proof.lean + explanation.md.
With --interactive you can type hints and commands while the search runs.
"""

import argparse
import select
import sys
import threading
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

from mathfinder import PROJECT_ROOT
from mathfinder.collab import Session
from mathfinder.explain import explain
from mathfinder.formalize import formalize
from mathfinder.lean import HEADER, statement_errors
from mathfinder.llm import LLMError
from mathfinder.search import search

RUNS_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / "runs"

SYMBOL = {"lemma_proved": "✓", "lemma_refuted": "✗", "lemma_stuck": "…"}


def print_event(event: dict) -> None:
    kind = event["kind"]
    if event.get("depth", 0) > 0:  # events from a sub-search are indented under their lemma
        indent = "        " * event["depth"]
        if kind in SYMBOL:
            print(f"{indent}{SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
        elif kind == "plan_start":
            print(f"{indent}▶ sub-plan: {event['sketch']}")
        elif kind == "done":
            print(f"{indent}✓ sub-search proved it")
        sys.stdout.flush()
        return
    if kind == "start":
        print(f"\nTheorem: {event['theorem']}")
    elif kind == "subsearch_start":
        print(f"      ↳ {event['name']} is stuck: starting a sub-search on it")
    elif kind == "refuted":
        print(f"✗ The statement is FALSE. Counterexample: {event['counterexample']}")
    elif kind == "round":
        print(f"\n— round {event['number']}: asking the planner —")
    elif kind == "plan_rejected":
        print(f"  ✗ plan doesn't fit together: {event['sketch']}\n      {event['reason'][:160]}")
    elif kind == "plan_start":
        print(f"  ▶ plan: {event['sketch']}  (lemmas: {', '.join(event['lemmas']) or 'none'})")
    elif kind in SYMBOL:
        print(f"      {SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
    elif kind == "plan_failed":
        print(f"    plan failed (score {event['score']:.2f})")
    elif kind == "done":
        print(f"\n✓ VERIFIED by Lean. Plan: {event['plan']}\n")
        print(event["lean_code"])
    elif kind == "gave_up":
        print("\nNo verified proof found.")
    elif kind == "ask_user":
        print(f"\n❓ {event['question']}\n   (type your answer and press Enter)")
    elif kind == "user_answer":
        print(f"  ← thanks, trying again with your idea: {event['text']}")
    elif kind == "no_answer":
        print("  (no answer)")
    elif kind == "stopped":
        print(f"\n■ Stopped: {event['reason']}")
    sys.stdout.flush()  # show progress immediately, even when output is piped to a file


def approve(latex: str, auto_yes: bool = False, ask=input) -> str | None:
    """Formalize `latex`, show it, and return the statement the user approved (None = no)."""
    print("Formalizing (LaTeX → Lean)...")
    f = formalize(latex)
    if not f.ok:
        print("Could not produce a statement Lean accepts:\n  " + f.errors[0][:400])
        return None
    print(f"\nFormal statement:  theorem main {f.statement}")
    print(f"Back-translation:  {f.back_translation}")
    print(f"Judge:             {'same meaning' if f.faithful else 'MEANING MAY DIFFER'}: {f.judgment}")

    statement = f.statement
    if auto_yes and f.faithful:
        return statement
    if auto_yes:
        print("(--yes ignored: the judge thinks the meaning may differ, so please look.)")
    while True:
        try:
            answer = ask("\nProve this statement? [y]es / [n]o / [e]dit: ").strip().lower()
        except EOFError:  # no keyboard attached (e.g. running in a script)
            return None
        if answer == "y":
            return statement
        if answer == "n":
            return None
        if answer == "e":
            new = ask("Lean statement (what comes after `theorem main`): ").strip()
            errors = statement_errors(new)
            if errors:
                print("Lean rejected it: " + errors[0][:300])
            else:
                statement = new
                print(f"Now:  theorem main {statement}")


COMMANDS = """While it runs you can type:
  hint <text>                     e.g.  hint try induction on n
  lemma <name> <lean statement>   e.g.  lemma key (a b : ℝ) : 0 ≤ (a - b) ^ 2
  reject <lemma name>             plans using that lemma are dropped
  pin <approach>                  "keep going in this direction"
  pause | resume | stop
When the search asks you a question, just type your answer."""


def handle_command(session: Session, line: str) -> str:
    """Apply one typed line to the session. Returns a short confirmation to print."""
    line = line.strip()
    word, _, rest = line.partition(" ")  # "hint try x" -> ("hint", " ", "try x")
    if session.question is not None and word not in ("pause", "resume", "stop"):
        session.answer(line)  # a question is open: the whole line is the answer
        return "answer sent"
    if word == "hint" and rest:
        session.add_hint(rest)
        return f"hint added: {rest}"
    if word == "lemma" and " " in rest:
        name, statement = rest.split(" ", 1)
        session.add_lemma(name, statement)
        return f"lemma suggested: {name}"
    if word == "reject" and rest:
        session.reject_lemma(rest)
        return f"rejected: {rest}"
    if word == "pin" and rest:
        session.pin(rest)
        return f"pinned: {rest}"
    if word == "pause":
        session.pause()
        return "paused (type resume to continue)"
    if word == "resume":
        session.resume()
        return "resumed"
    if word == "stop":
        session.stop()
        return "stopping..."
    return "unknown command. " + COMMANDS


def interactive_search(statement: str, hints: list[str], rounds: int):
    """Run the search in a background thread while this thread reads your commands."""
    session = Session(statement)
    for h in hints:
        session.add_hint(h)
    box = {}
    worker = threading.Thread(
        target=lambda: box.setdefault("result", search(statement, rounds=rounds, on_event=print_event, session=session)),
        daemon=True,  # don't keep Python alive just for this thread
    )
    worker.start()
    print(COMMANDS)
    keyboard_open = True
    while worker.is_alive():
        if not keyboard_open:
            worker.join(0.5)
            continue
        ready, _, _ = select.select([sys.stdin], [], [], 0.5)  # wait up to 0.5 s for a typed line
        if ready:
            line = sys.stdin.readline()
            if line == "":  # end of input (Ctrl-D, or a pipe that closed)
                keyboard_open = False
            elif line.strip():
                print(f"  [{handle_command(session, line)}]")
    return box["result"]


def save_run(theorem: str, lean_code: str, explanation: str) -> Path:
    """Save a proved theorem as runs/<date-time>/{theorem.txt, proof.lean, explanation.md}."""
    folder = RUNS_DIR / datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d-%H%M%S")
    folder.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    (folder / "theorem.txt").write_text(theorem + "\n")
    (folder / "proof.lean").write_text(HEADER + lean_code)  # a complete file Lean can check on its own
    (folder / "explanation.md").write_text(explanation + "\n")
    return folder


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="mathfinder")
    commands = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    prove = commands.add_parser("prove", help="search for a proof")
    prove.add_argument("latex", nargs="?", help="the theorem in LaTeX (in quotes)")
    prove.add_argument("--lean", help='skip formalization: a Lean statement, e.g. "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"')
    prove.add_argument("--yes", action="store_true", help="don't ask for approval if the judge is happy")
    prove.add_argument("--hint", action="append", default=[], help="a hint for the planner (repeatable)")
    prove.add_argument("--rounds", type=int, default=3)
    prove.add_argument("--no-explain", action="store_true", help="skip the plain-math explanation")
    prove.add_argument("--interactive", action="store_true", help="type hints and commands while it searches")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    if args.command == "prove":
        if args.lean:
            statement = args.lean
        elif args.latex:
            statement = approve(args.latex, args.yes)
            if statement is None:
                print("Not searching.")
                return 1
        else:
            parser.error("give a theorem in LaTeX, or --lean STATEMENT")
        if args.interactive:
            result = interactive_search(statement, args.hint, args.rounds)
        else:
            result = search(statement, hints=args.hint, rounds=args.rounds, on_event=print_event)
        print(f"\nResult: {'PROVED' if result.proved else 'not proved'} ({result.message})")
        if not result.proved:
            return 1
        explanation = ""
        if not args.no_explain:
            print("\nWriting the explanation...")
            try:
                explanation = explain(args.latex or statement, result.plan, result.lean_code)
                print("\n" + explanation)
            except LLMError as e:  # the proof is still valid; only the write-up failed
                print(f"(could not write an explanation: {e})")
        print(f"\nSaved to {save_run(args.latex or statement, result.lean_code, explanation)}")
        return 0
    return 2

    What changed: new COMMANDS help text, handle_command, interactive_search, a --interactive flag, and printing for the new events (ask_user, user_answer, no_answer, stopped).

    handle_command uses str.partition(" ") to split "hint try x" into the command word and the rest. While a question is open, the whole line is your answer (except pause/resume/stop).

    interactive_search starts the search in a threading.Thread. daemon=True means the thread won't keep Python running after the main program ends. The main thread reads your typing, but input() would block forever even after the search finished. Instead, select.select([sys.stdin], [], [], 0.5) waits at most 0.5 seconds for a typed line, then loops round to check whether the search is still alive. An empty string from readline() means the input ended (Ctrl-D). The result comes back through box, a dictionary both threads can see.

  5. Update the command line tests. Replace the whole file:

    tests/test_cli.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/cli.py: approval, explanation, saving, typed commands. Lean and the model are faked."""

import pytest

from mathfinder import cli
from mathfinder.collab import Session
from mathfinder.formalize import Formalization
from mathfinder.llm import LLMError

@pytest.fixture

    def install(faithful=True):
        result = Formalization("latex", "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n", "For all integers n, 6 | n^3 - n.", faithful, "ok")
        monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "formalize", lambda latex: result)

    return install


def scripted_user(*answers):
    """Stands in for input(): gives the answers in order."""
    queue = list(answers)
    return lambda question: queue.pop(0)


def test_yes_returns_the_statement(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize()
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=scripted_user("y")) == "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"


def test_no_returns_none(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize()
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=scripted_user("n")) is None


def test_edit_is_checked_by_lean_before_it_is_used(fake_formalize, monkeypatch):
    fake_formalize()
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "statement_errors", lambda s: ["unknown identifier 'oops'"] if "oops" in s else [])
    user = scripted_user("e", "(n : ℤ) : oops", "e", "(n : ℕ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n", "y")
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=user) == "(n : ℕ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"

    fake_formalize(faithful=False)

def test_main_with_latex_and_yes_runs_the_search_explains_and_saves(fake_formalize, monkeypatch, tmp_path):
    fake_formalize()
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "RUNS_DIR", tmp_path)

    def fake_search(statement, hints, rounds, on_event):
        seen["statement"] = statement
        return SearchResult(True, "theorem main ... := by\n  decide\n", Plan("check all cases", [], "decide"))

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "search", fake_search)
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "explain", lambda theorem, plan, code: "## Theorem\nexplained")

Fast tests:

............................................................             [100%]
60 passed, 28 deselected in 0.71s

Common errors

  • Your typing seems ignored: commands are read only between the search's steps, and a single Lean run can take 30 s. The [hint added: ...] line confirms it arrived; the hint is used from the next round.
  • A test hangs: a thread waits forever. Check that stop() sets the event and puts None in the queue.
  • KeyError: 'result' after Ctrl-C: the search thread was interrupted before finishing. Just run again.

M8: Remember everything, then turn it into training data

What and why. Every search produces valuable information: proofs Lean accepted, proofs Lean rejected (with the exact error), lemmas that turned out easy, false or stuck, and hints people gave. Systems like AlphaProof improved by training on exactly this kind of record of their own search. This chapter keeps it. A trace logger writes every event of every run to runs/<id>/trace.jsonl, failed attempts included, and mathfinder export turns all traces into four datasets:

FileOne row isTrains
sft.jsonlgoal (+ lemmas it may use) → a proof Lean acceptedthe prover to imitate good proofs ("supervised fine-tuning")
preferences.jsonlsame goal: a proof that worked vs. one that failed (+ its error)the prover to prefer what works (e.g. DPO)
value.jsonllemma statement → proved / refuted / stucka value model that guesses how hard a goal is, so search can try promising plans first
hints.jsonlthe hints a person gave → whether it got provedlearning which advice actually helps

    What changed: the last line, data/. It's rebuilt any time by export.

  2. Make the prover report every attempt. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/prover.py
    """The prover: get ONE lemma proved, as cheaply as possible.

  1. cache   - proved this exact lemma before? reuse it, free
  2. hammer  - a dozen automatic tactics in one Lean run, no AI
               (also finds counterexamples: then the lemma is false, stop)
  3. LLM     - ask Claude for several proofs, check them all in ONE Lean run
  4. repair  - show Claude its failed proofs WITH Lean's error messages, ask for fixes

The lemma ends up "proved" (with a proof), "refuted" (with a counterexample) or
"stuck" (with the most useful Lean error, so the planner can rethink).

Every attempt, failed ones included, can be reported to an `on_attempt` callback.
Those records are the raw material for training data (M8).
"""

import json

from mathfinder import PROJECT_ROOT, llm
from mathfinder.hammer import hammer
from mathfinder.lean import Decl, check_decls
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma

CACHE_FILE = PROJECT_ROOT / "cache" / "lemmas.json"
ATTEMPTS = 4  # proofs asked for per LLM call
REPAIR_ROUNDS = 2  # extra rounds that see Lean's errors
MAX_ERROR = 600  # characters of each Lean error shown to the model

SYSTEM = """You are an expert in Lean 4 and Mathlib. You write tactic proofs that compile.
Rules:
- Lean 4 syntax, current Mathlib lemma names (e.g. Finset.sum_range_succ, sq_nonneg, Nat.succ_le_iff).
- Write ONLY the tactic script that goes after `:= by` (do not write `by` or the statement).
- Never use sorry, admit, or axiom.
- You may use the earlier lemmas listed, by name.
- Each proof should be a genuinely different approach.
Reply with ONLY a JSON object inside a ```json block: {"proofs": ["<tactic script>", ...]}"""


# ---------- cache ----------


def cache_key(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma]) -> str:
    """Same statement AND same available lemmas = same problem. Whitespace doesn't matter."""
    context = " ; ".join(f"{l.name} {l.statement}" for l in earlier)
    return " ".join(f"{context} ⊢ {lemma.statement}".split())


def load_cache() -> dict:
    if CACHE_FILE.exists():
        return json.loads(CACHE_FILE.read_text())
    return {}


def save_to_cache(key: str, proof: str) -> None:
    cache = load_cache()
    cache[key] = proof
    CACHE_FILE.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    CACHE_FILE.write_text(json.dumps(cache, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))


# ---------- talking to the model ----------


def preamble_for(earlier: list[Lemma]) -> str:
    """Earlier lemmas the proof may use: real proofs where we have them, sorry otherwise.

    They go inside `namespace MathFinder` (left open, which Lean allows at the end of
    a file), so a plan lemma called e.g. `sq_nonneg` can't clash with Mathlib's.
    """
    if not earlier:
        return ""
    parts = []
    for l in earlier:
        proof = l.proof if l.status == "proved" else "sorry"
        body = "\n".join("  " + line for line in proof.split("\n"))
        parts.append(f"theorem {l.name} {l.statement} := by\n{body}")
    return "namespace MathFinder\n\n" + "\n\n".join(parts)


def build_prompt(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma], failures: list[tuple[str, str]] = ()) -> str:
    parts = [f"Prove this lemma:\n  theorem {lemma.name} {lemma.statement}"]
    if lemma.informal:
        parts.append(f"In words: {lemma.informal}")
    if earlier:
        parts.append("Earlier lemmas you may use:\n" + "\n".join(f"  theorem {l.name} {l.statement}" for l in earlier))
    for proof, error in failures:
        parts.append(f"This attempt FAILED:\n{proof}\nLean said:\n{error[:MAX_ERROR]}")
    if failures:
        parts.append("Fix these mistakes. Use the error messages.")
    parts.append(f"Give {ATTEMPTS} different proofs.")
    return "\n\n".join(parts)


def parse_proofs(data) -> list[str]:
    """Keep only usable proof strings; strip a leading `by` the model may add."""
    proofs = []
    raw = data.get("proofs", []) if isinstance(data, dict) else []
    for p in raw:
        if not isinstance(p, str) or not p.strip():
            continue
        p = p.strip()
        if p.startswith("by"):
            p = p[2:].strip()
        if any(word in p for word in ("sorry", "admit", "axiom")):
            continue
        proofs.append(p)
    return proofs


def ask_for_proofs(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma], failures=()) -> list[str]:
    try:
        return parse_proofs(llm.complete_json(SYSTEM, build_prompt(lemma, earlier, failures)))
    except llm.LLMError:
        return []  # one bad model answer shouldn't end the search


def try_proofs(lemma: Lemma, proofs: list[str], preamble: str, report=None):
    """Check all proofs in one Lean run. Returns (first working proof or None, [(proof, error), ...]).
    `report(proof, ok, error)` is called for EVERY attempt: Lean checked them all anyway,
    and the failures next to a success are exactly what preference training needs."""
    decls = [Decl(f"attempt_{i}", lemma.statement, p) for i, p in enumerate(proofs)]
    results = check_decls(decls, preamble)
    found = None
    failures = []
    for i, p in enumerate(proofs):
        result = results[f"attempt_{i}"]
        error = "" if result.ok else ("\n".join(result.errors) or "proof uses sorry")
        if report:
            report(p, result.ok, error)
        if result.ok and found is None:
            found = p
        elif not result.ok:
            failures.append((p, error))
    return found, ([] if found else failures)


# ---------- the whole pipeline ----------


def prove_lemma(lemma: Lemma, earlier: list[Lemma] = (), use_cache: bool = True, on_attempt=None) -> Lemma:
    """Try to prove `lemma` (which may use the `earlier` lemmas). Updates and returns it.
    `on_attempt(record)` (optional) receives one dict per proof attempt, for the trace log."""
    earlier = list(earlier)
    key = cache_key(lemma, earlier)
    context = [f"theorem {l.name} {l.statement}" for l in earlier]

    def report(proof: str, ok: bool, error: str, source: str) -> None:
        if on_attempt:
            on_attempt({"statement": lemma.statement, "context": context, "proof": proof,
                        "ok": ok, "error": error[:MAX_ERROR], "source": source})

    if use_cache and key in load_cache():
        lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", load_cache()[key], "from cache"
        return lemma

    preamble = preamble_for(earlier)
    hit = hammer(lemma.statement, preamble)
    if hit.proved:
        report(hit.proof, True, "", "hammer")
        lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", hit.proof, f"hammer: {hit.tactic}"
        save_to_cache(key, hit.proof)
        return lemma
    if hit.counterexample:
        lemma.status, lemma.feedback = "refuted", f"counterexample: {hit.counterexample}"
        return lemma

    failures = []
    for round_number in range(1 + REPAIR_ROUNDS):
        proofs = ask_for_proofs(lemma, earlier, failures[:2])  # show the model at most 2 failures
        if not proofs:
            continue
        source = "llm" if round_number == 0 else "repair"
        found, failures = try_proofs(lemma, proofs, preamble, lambda p, ok, e: report(p, ok, e, source))
        if found:
            how = "LLM" if round_number == 0 else f"LLM after {round_number} repair round(s)"
            lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", found, how
            save_to_cache(key, found)
            return lemma

    lemma.status = "stuck"
    lemma.feedback = failures[0][1][:MAX_ERROR] if failures else "no usable proof attempts"
    return lemma

    What changed: prove_lemma takes on_attempt=None. Inside it, a small helper report(...) builds one record per attempt: the goal, the lemmas it could use (context), the proof, whether Lean accepted it, Lean's error, and where it came from (hammer, llm or repair). It's called for the hammer's winning tactic and, through try_proofs, for every LLM attempt.

    try_proofs now goes through all attempts instead of returning at the first success. Lean already checked them all in one run, so this costs nothing, and a failure sitting next to a success on the same goal is exactly a preference pair. We found this in a real run where preferences came out as 0.

    lambda p, ok, e: report(p, ok, e, source) adapts report (4 arguments) to what try_proofs calls (3 arguments), filling in source from the current round.

  3. Test the attempt records. Replace the whole file:

    tests/test_prover.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/prover.py.

The Lean tests use a FAKE model with scripted answers, so they're free and
repeatable while still checking every proof with real Lean.
"""

import json

import pytest

from mathfinder import llm, prover
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma

ODD_SUM = "(n : ℕ) : ∑ i ∈ Finset.range n, (2 * i + 1) = n ^ 2"  # needs induction: the hammer can't
GOOD = "induction n with\n| zero => simp\n| succ k ih =>\n  rw [Finset.sum_range_succ, ih]\n  ring"
WRONG = "simp"


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def temp_cache(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    """Every test gets its own empty cache file, so tests can't affect each other."""
    monkeypatch.setattr(prover, "CACHE_FILE", tmp_path / "lemmas.json")


@pytest.fixture
def scripted_model(monkeypatch):
    """A fake model that gives the scripted proof lists in order and records each prompt."""

    def install(*answers):
        prompts = []

        def fake(system, prompt):
            prompts.append(prompt)
            assert len(prompts) <= len(answers), "the model was called more times than expected"
            proofs = answers[len(prompts) - 1]
            return "```json\n" + json.dumps({"proofs": proofs}) + "\n```"

        monkeypatch.setitem(llm.BACKENDS, "fake", fake)
        monkeypatch.setenv("MATHFINDER_LLM", "fake")
        return prompts

    return install


# ---------- fast tests ----------


def test_parse_proofs_cleans_model_output():
    data = {"proofs": ["by simp", "  ring  ", "sorry", 42, "", "exact foo"]}
    assert prover.parse_proofs(data) == ["simp", "ring", "exact foo"]


def test_cache_key_ignores_whitespace_but_not_context():
    a = Lemma("x", "(n : ℕ) :  n = n", "")
    b = Lemma("y", "(n : ℕ) : n = n", "")
    helper = Lemma("h", ": True", "")
    assert prover.cache_key(a, []) == prover.cache_key(b, [])
    assert prover.cache_key(a, []) != prover.cache_key(a, [helper])


def test_repair_prompt_shows_lean_errors():
    lemma = Lemma("odd_sum", ODD_SUM, "sum of first n odd numbers")
    prompt = prover.build_prompt(lemma, [], [("simp", "simp made no progress")])
    assert "FAILED" in prompt and "simp made no progress" in prompt


def test_preamble_uses_real_proofs_when_known():
    done = Lemma("a", ": 1 = 1", "", status="proved", proof="rfl")
    open_ = Lemma("b", ": 2 = 2", "")
    assert prover.preamble_for([done, open_]) == (
        "namespace MathFinder\n\n"
        "theorem a : 1 = 1 := by\n  rfl\n\ntheorem b : 2 = 2 := by\n  sorry"
    )
    assert prover.preamble_for([]) == ""

@pytest.mark.lean
def test_false_lemma_is_refuted_without_asking_the_model(scripted_model):
    prompts = scripted_model()  # no answers: any model call fails the test
    lemma = prover.prove_lemma(Lemma("bad", "(n : ℕ) : n ^ 2 ≤ 2 * n + 3", ""))
    assert lemma.status == "refuted"
    assert "counterexample" in lemma.feedback
    assert prompts == []


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_repair_round_fixes_a_wrong_proof_then_cache_reuses_it(scripted_model):
    prompts = scripted_model([WRONG], [GOOD])
    attempts = []
    lemma = prover.prove_lemma(Lemma("odd_sum", ODD_SUM, "sum of the first n odd numbers is n^2"), on_attempt=attempts.append)
    assert lemma.status == "proved", lemma.feedback
    assert [(a["proof"], a["ok"], a["source"]) for a in attempts] == [(WRONG, False, "llm"), (GOOD, True, "repair")]
    assert attempts[0]["error"]  # the failure is logged WITH Lean's error
    assert lemma.proof == GOOD
    assert lemma.feedback == "LLM after 1 repair round(s)"
    assert "Lean said" in prompts[1]  # the second call saw the real Lean error

    again = prover.prove_lemma(Lemma("odd_sum_again", ODD_SUM, ""))
    assert again.status == "proved" and again.feedback == "from cache"
    assert len(prompts) == 2  # no new model call

  4. Forward attempts as events from the search. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/search.py
    """The search: plan, check, prove, verify, and replan with what we learned.

  0. maybe the hammer proves the whole theorem directly (or refutes it)
  1. the planner proposes a few plans
  2. the critic throws out plans that don't fit together (one Lean run)
  3. remaining plans are tried cheapest-first (fewest lemmas); each lemma goes to
     the prover, and a refuted lemma kills its plan immediately
  4. a plan with every lemma proved is assembled into one file and checked by
     the strict final verification (no sorry, only standard axioms)
  5. otherwise everything that went wrong becomes feedback for the next round

A lemma the prover gets stuck on becomes a smaller search of its own (a
"sub-search", at most MAX_DEPTH levels deep). If the sub-search proves it, its
helper lemmas are spliced into the parent plan.

With a collab.Session, you work together: your hints, lemmas and rejections are
read every round, pause/stop are honoured at checkpoints, and before giving up
the search ASKS you a specific question (your answer buys another round).

Everything that happens is reported through `on_event`, so the command line,
the web page and the trace logger can all watch the same search. That includes
an "attempt" event for every single proof attempt, failed ones too.
"""

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass

from mathfinder.critic import check_skeletons
from mathfinder.hammer import hammer
from mathfinder.lean import verify_final
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma, Plan, propose_plans
from mathfinder.prover import prove_lemma

NAMESPACE = "MathFinder"
MAX_DEPTH = 1  # a stuck lemma may get a sub-search; a stuck lemma inside that may not
MAX_QUESTIONS = 2  # how many times the search may ask the user before giving up


@dataclass
class SearchResult:
    proved: bool
    lean_code: str = ""  # the complete verified proof (everything after the file header)
    plan: Plan | None = None
    rounds: int = 0
    message: str = ""


def assemble(theorem: str, plan: Plan) -> str:
    """One Lean file: every lemma with its real proof, then the main theorem."""
    blocks = [f"namespace {NAMESPACE}"]
    for l in plan.lemmas:
        body = "\n".join("  " + line for line in l.proof.split("\n"))
        blocks.append(f"theorem {l.name} {l.statement} := by\n{body}")
    main_body = "\n".join("  " + line for line in plan.main_proof.split("\n"))
    blocks.append(f"theorem main {theorem} := by\n{main_body}")
    blocks.append(f"end {NAMESPACE}")
    return "\n\n".join(blocks) + "\n"


def score(plan: Plan) -> float:
    """Fraction of lemmas proved; a refuted lemma makes the whole plan worthless."""
    if any(l.status == "refuted" for l in plan.lemmas):
        return -1.0
    if not plan.lemmas:
        return 0.0
    return sum(l.status == "proved" for l in plan.lemmas) / len(plan.lemmas)


def summarize(plan: Plan) -> str:
    """What happened to one plan, written for the planner's next round."""
    lines = [f"Plan: {plan.sketch}  (main proof: {plan.main_proof!r})"]
    lines += [f"  - {note}" for note in plan.feedback]
    for l in plan.lemmas:
        lines.append(f"  - lemma {l.name} {l.statement}: {l.status.upper()}  {l.feedback[:300]}")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def splice(plan: Plan, lemma: Lemma, sub: Plan) -> int:
    """Put a sub-search's lemmas into `plan` just before `lemma`, and use the sub-search's
    main proof as `lemma`'s proof. Sub-lemmas are renamed `<lemma>_<name>` so names stay
    unique. Returns how many lemmas were inserted."""
    renames = {l.name: f"{lemma.name}_{l.name}" for l in sub.lemmas}

    def rename(text: str) -> str:
        for old, new in renames.items():
            text = re.sub(rf"\b{re.escape(old)}\b", new, text)  # \b = whole words only
        return text

    inserted = [
        Lemma(renames[l.name], rename(l.statement), l.informal, "proved", rename(l.proof), "from sub-search")
        for l in sub.lemmas
    ]
    i = plan.lemmas.index(lemma)
    plan.lemmas[i:i] = inserted  # slice assignment: insert the list at position i
    lemma.status, lemma.proof, lemma.feedback = "proved", rename(sub.main_proof), "proved by a sub-search"
    return len(inserted)


def prove_plan(plan: Plan, emit, on_event=None, hints=(), depth: int = 0, session=None) -> bool:
    """Prove the plan's lemmas in order. Stops at the first refuted lemma.
    A stuck lemma gets a sub-search while depth < MAX_DEPTH."""
    i = 0
    while i < len(plan.lemmas):  # a while loop, because splice() can insert lemmas
        if session and session.checkpoint():  # paused? wait here. stopped? give up this plan
            return False
        lemma = plan.lemmas[i]
        emit("lemma_start", name=lemma.name, statement=lemma.statement)
        prove_lemma(lemma, plan.lemmas[:i], on_attempt=lambda record: emit("attempt", **record))
        if lemma.status == "stuck" and depth < MAX_DEPTH:
            emit("subsearch_start", name=lemma.name, statement=lemma.statement)
            sub = search(lemma.statement, hints, rounds=1, on_event=on_event, depth=depth + 1, session=session)
            if sub.proved:
                i += splice(plan, lemma, sub.plan)  # skip past the inserted (already proved) lemmas
        emit("lemma_" + lemma.status, name=lemma.name, statement=lemma.statement, feedback=lemma.feedback)
        if lemma.status == "refuted":
            return False
        i += 1
    return all(l.status == "proved" for l in plan.lemmas)


def build_question(theorem: str, best: Plan | None) -> str:
    """A specific question for the user: the step we're stuck on, and what Lean said."""
    stuck = [l for l in best.lemmas if l.status == "stuck"] if best else []
    if stuck:
        l = stuck[0]
        return (
            f"I can't prove this step:\n  {l.name} : {l.statement}\n"
            f"Lean said: {l.feedback[:300]}\n"
            "Any idea how to prove it, or a different approach to the whole theorem?"
        )
    return f"I couldn't find a proof of\n  {theorem}\nWhich approach would you try?"


def try_direct(theorem: str, emit) -> SearchResult | None:
    """Step 0: maybe automation proves (or refutes) the whole theorem."""
    hit = hammer(theorem)
    if hit.counterexample:
        emit("refuted", counterexample=hit.counterexample)
        return SearchResult(False, message=f"the statement is false: {hit.counterexample}")
    if hit.proved:
        plan = Plan(f"Automation: {hit.tactic}", [], hit.proof)
        code = assemble(theorem, plan)
        if verify_final(code, f"{NAMESPACE}.main").ok:
            emit("done", lean_code=code, plan=plan.sketch)
            return SearchResult(True, code, plan, 0, "proved directly by automation")
    return None


def search(
    theorem: str,
    hints=(),
    rounds: int = 3,
    plans_per_round: int = 2,
    on_event=None,
    depth: int = 0,
    session=None,
) -> SearchResult:
    """Search for a verified proof of `theorem` (a Lean statement without a name).
    `depth` is 0 for the user's theorem, 1 for a sub-search on a stuck lemma, ...
    `session` (optional) is a collab.Session shared with the user."""

    def emit(kind, **data):
        if on_event:
            on_event({"kind": kind, "depth": depth, **data})

    def stop_reason():
        reason = session.checkpoint() if session else None
        if reason:
            emit("stopped", reason=reason)
        return reason

    emit("start", theorem=theorem)
    if depth == 0:  # in a sub-search, the prover has already run the hammer on this statement
        direct = try_direct(theorem, emit)
        if direct:
            return direct

    feedback = []  # what went wrong so far, fed to the planner
    best = None
    r = 0
    questions = 0
    while True:
        if r == rounds:  # out of rounds: ask the user before giving up
            if session is None or depth > 0 or questions == MAX_QUESTIONS:
                break
            question = build_question(theorem, best)
            emit("ask_user", question=question)
            questions += 1
            answer = session.ask(question)
            if not answer:
                emit("no_answer")
                break
            session.add_hint(answer)
            emit("user_answer", text=answer)
            rounds += 1  # the answer buys one more round
        r += 1
        if stop_reason():
            return SearchResult(False, plan=best, rounds=r, message=f"stopped: {session.checkpoint()}")

        emit("round", number=r)
        all_hints = list(hints) + (session.hints() if session else [])
        plans = propose_plans(theorem, plans_per_round, all_hints, "\n".join(feedback[-6:]))
        emit("plans", sketches=[p.sketch for p in plans])
        problems = check_skeletons(theorem, plans)

        fitting = []
        for plan, found in zip(plans, problems):
            rejected = session.uses_rejected(plan) if session else None
            if found:
                emit("plan_rejected", sketch=plan.sketch, reason=found[0][:300])
                feedback.append(summarize(plan))
            elif rejected:
                emit("plan_rejected", sketch=plan.sketch, reason=f"uses {rejected}, which you rejected")
            else:
                fitting.append(plan)

        for plan in sorted(fitting, key=lambda p: len(p.lemmas)):  # cheapest first
            if stop_reason():
                return SearchResult(False, plan=best, rounds=r, message=f"stopped: {session.checkpoint()}")
            emit("plan_start", sketch=plan.sketch, lemmas=[l.name for l in plan.lemmas])
            if prove_plan(plan, emit, on_event, all_hints, depth, session):
                code = assemble(theorem, plan)
                final = verify_final(code, f"{NAMESPACE}.main")
                if final.ok:
                    emit("done", lean_code=code, plan=plan.sketch)
                    return SearchResult(True, code, plan, r, "verified")
                plan.feedback.append("final check failed: " + " | ".join(final.errors)[:400])
            emit("plan_failed", sketch=plan.sketch, score=score(plan))
            feedback.append(summarize(plan))
            if best is None or score(plan) > score(best):
                best = plan

    if session and session.checkpoint():
        emit("stopped", reason=session.checkpoint())
        return SearchResult(False, plan=best, rounds=r, message=f"stopped: {session.checkpoint()}")
    emit("gave_up", best=best.sketch if best else None)
    message = f"no verified proof after {r} rounds"
    if best:
        message += f"; closest plan proved {score(best):.0%} of its lemmas"
    return SearchResult(False, plan=best, rounds=r, message=message)

    What changed: one line in prove_plan: prove_lemma(..., on_attempt=lambda record: emit("attempt", **record)). Every attempt becomes an attempt event, so every watcher (the terminal, the logger, later the website) can see it. **record unpacks the dictionary into keyword arguments.

    tests/test_collab.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/collab.py and how search() works with a Session.

Lean and the model are replaced by fakes, so these run instantly. Threads play
the user acting while the search runs.
"""

import threading
import time
from types import SimpleNamespace

import pytest

from mathfinder import search as search_module
from mathfinder.collab import Session
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma, Plan

# ---------- the Session on its own ----------


def test_hints_lemmas_and_pins_become_planner_hints():
    s = Session("(n : ℕ) : P n")
    s.add_hint("try induction on n")
    s.add_lemma("key", "(n : ℕ) : Q n", "Q always holds")
    s.pin("strong induction")
    hints = s.hints()
    assert hints[0] == "try induction on n"
    assert "theorem key (n : ℕ) : Q n  -- Q always holds" in hints[1]
    assert "strong induction" in hints[2]


def test_rejected_lemma_is_found_by_name_or_statement():
    s = Session("t")
    s.reject_lemma("bad")
    s.reject_lemma("(x : ℝ)   :  x = 0")  # spacing doesn't matter
    good = Plan("", [Lemma("fine", ": True", "")], "")
    by_name = Plan("", [Lemma("bad", ": True", "")], "")
    by_statement = Plan("", [Lemma("other", "(x : ℝ) : x = 0", "")], "")
    assert s.uses_rejected(good) is None
    assert s.uses_rejected(by_name) == "bad"
    assert s.uses_rejected(by_statement) == "other"


def test_pause_blocks_the_checkpoint_until_resume():
    s = Session("t")
    s.pause()
    passed = threading.Event()
    threading.Thread(target=lambda: (s.checkpoint(), passed.set()), daemon=True).start()
    time.sleep(0.2)
    assert not passed.is_set()  # still waiting
    s.resume()
    assert passed.wait(2)


def test_ask_waits_for_an_answer_from_another_thread():
    s = Session("t")
    threading.Timer(0.1, lambda: s.answer("use AM-GM")).start()
    assert s.ask("Any idea?") == "use AM-GM"
    assert s.question is None


def test_ask_gives_up_after_the_timeout():
    assert Session("t", answer_timeout=0.1).ask("Any idea?") is None


def test_stop_wakes_a_paused_search():
    s = Session("t")
    s.pause()
    s.stop()
    assert s.checkpoint() == "stopped by the user"


# ---------- search() with a session (fake Lean, fake model) ----------


@pytest.fixture
def fake_world(monkeypatch):
    """Lemmas named 'good*' get proved, 'hard*' get stuck. The planner is scripted per round."""
    seen_hints = []

    def install(plans_per_round):
        rounds = list(plans_per_round)

        def fake_propose(theorem, n, hints, feedback):
            seen_hints.append(list(hints))
            return rounds.pop(0) if rounds else []

        def fake_prove(lemma, earlier, on_attempt=None):
            lemma.status = "proved" if lemma.name.startswith("good") else "stuck"
            lemma.proof = "simp" if lemma.status == "proved" else ""
            lemma.feedback = "" if lemma.status == "proved" else "simp made no progress"
            return lemma

        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "try_direct", lambda theorem, emit: None)
        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "propose_plans", fake_propose)
        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "check_skeletons", lambda t, plans: [[] for _ in plans])
        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "prove_lemma", fake_prove)
        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "verify_final", lambda code, name: SimpleNamespace(ok=True, errors=[]))
        monkeypatch.setattr(search_module, "MAX_DEPTH", 0)  # no sub-searches in these tests
        return seen_hints

    return install


def plan(*names):
    return Plan(f"plan with {', '.join(names)}", [Lemma(n, f"(n : ℕ) : {n} n", "") for n in names], "simp")


def test_session_hints_reach_the_planner_and_rejected_plans_are_dropped(fake_world):
    seen_hints = fake_world([[plan("bad_idea"), plan("good_a")]])
    session = Session("t")
    session.add_hint("use induction")
    session.reject_lemma("bad_idea")
    events = []
    result = search_module.search("t", rounds=1, on_event=events.append, session=session)
    assert result.proved
    assert "use induction" in seen_hints[0]
    rejected = [e for e in events if e["kind"] == "plan_rejected"]
    assert rejected[0]["reason"] == "uses bad_idea, which you rejected"


def test_search_asks_the_user_and_the_answer_buys_another_round(fake_world):
    seen_hints = fake_world([[plan("hard_step")], [plan("good_b")]])
    session = Session("t")
    events = []

    def user_watches(event):
        events.append(event)
        if event["kind"] == "ask_user":  # answer from "another person", a moment later
            threading.Timer(0.1, lambda: session.answer("split it into two cases")).start()

    result = search_module.search("t", rounds=1, on_event=user_watches, session=session)
    assert result.proved and result.rounds == 2
    question = next(e["question"] for e in events if e["kind"] == "ask_user")
    assert "hard_step" in question and "simp made no progress" in question
    assert "split it into two cases" in seen_hints[1]  # the planner saw the answer in round 2


def test_no_answer_means_giving_up(fake_world):
    fake_world([[plan("hard_step")]])
    session = Session("t", answer_timeout=0.1)
    events = []
    result = search_module.search("t", rounds=1, on_event=events.append, session=session)
    assert not result.proved
    kinds = [e["kind"] for e in events]
    assert kinds[-3:] == ["ask_user", "no_answer", "gave_up"]


def test_editing_the_statement_stops_the_search(fake_world):
    fake_world([[plan("good_a")]])
    session = Session("t")
    session.edit_statement("(n : ℕ) : n = n")
    result = search_module.search("t", rounds=1, session=session)
    assert not result.proved
    assert result.message == "stopped: the statement was edited"
    assert session.theorem == "(n : ℕ) : n = n"
