  6. Create the trace module.

    mathfinder/traces.py
    """Traces: remember everything the search did, then turn it into training data.

While searching, a TraceLogger writes every event as one line of JSON to
runs/<id>/trace.jsonl ("JSON Lines": one JSON object per line, easy to append to
and easy to read back). Failed attempts are logged too: knowing what DOESN'T work
is just as useful for training.

`export` reads every trace and builds four datasets in data/:
  sft.jsonl          goal (+ lemmas it may use) → a proof Lean accepted      ("imitate this")
  preferences.jsonl  same goal: a proof that worked vs. one that failed      ("prefer this one")
  value.jsonl        lemma statement → proved / refuted / stuck              ("how hard is this?")
  hints.jsonl        the hints a person gave → whether the theorem got proved
"""

import json
import threading
import time
from pathlib import Path

DATASETS = ("sft", "preferences", "value", "hints")


class TraceLogger:
    """An on_event callback that appends every event to <run_dir>/trace.jsonl."""

    def __init__(self, run_dir: Path, theorem: str):
        run_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        self.path = run_dir / "trace.jsonl"
        self._lock = threading.Lock()  # events can arrive from the search thread and the main thread
        self({"kind": "meta", "theorem": theorem})

    def __call__(self, event: dict) -> None:
        record = {"time": round(time.time(), 3), **event}
        with self._lock, self.path.open("a") as f:  # "a" = append to the end of the file
            f.write(json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")

    def finish(self, proved: bool, hints: list[str]) -> None:
        self({"kind": "result", "proved": proved, "hints": list(hints)})


def chain(*callbacks):
    """One on_event callback that passes each event to several watchers (e.g. printer + logger)."""

    def on_event(event: dict) -> None:
        for callback in callbacks:
            callback(event)

    return on_event


def read_trace(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    return [json.loads(line) for line in path.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]


def records_from_trace(events: list[dict]) -> dict[str, list[dict]]:
    """Turn one run's events into rows for each dataset."""
    out = {name: [] for name in DATASETS}
    theorem = next((e["theorem"] for e in events if e["kind"] == "meta"), "")
    attempts = [e for e in events if e["kind"] == "attempt"]

    for a in attempts:
        if a["ok"]:
            out["sft"].append({"type": "lemma", "statement": a["statement"], "context": a["context"],
                               "proof": a["proof"], "source": a["source"]})
    for e in events:
        if e["kind"] == "done" and e.get("depth", 0) == 0:
            out["sft"].append({"type": "theorem", "statement": theorem, "context": [], "proof": e["lean_code"],
                               "source": "search"})

    by_goal = {}  # (statement, context) -> attempts on that exact goal
    for a in attempts:
        by_goal.setdefault((a["statement"], tuple(a["context"])), []).append(a)
    for (statement, context), group in by_goal.items():
        good = [a for a in group if a["ok"]]
        bad = [a for a in group if not a["ok"]]
        for g in good:
            for b in bad:
                out["preferences"].append({"statement": statement, "context": list(context), "chosen": g["proof"],
                                           "rejected": b["proof"], "rejected_error": b["error"]})

    for e in events:
        if e["kind"] in ("lemma_proved", "lemma_refuted", "lemma_stuck"):
            out["value"].append({"statement": e["statement"], "label": e["kind"].removeprefix("lemma_")})

    for e in events:
        if e["kind"] == "result" and e["hints"]:
            out["hints"].append({"theorem": theorem, "hints": e["hints"], "proved": e["proved"]})
    return out


def export(runs_dir: Path, out_dir: Path) -> dict[str, int]:
    """Build all datasets from every runs/*/trace.jsonl. Returns how many rows each got."""
    rows = {name: [] for name in DATASETS}
    for trace in sorted(runs_dir.glob("*/trace.jsonl")):
        for name, records in records_from_trace(read_trace(trace)).items():
            rows[name].extend(records)

    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    counts = {}
    for name, records in rows.items():
        unique = list({json.dumps(r, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False): r for r in records}.values())
        with (out_dir / f"{name}.jsonl").open("w") as f:
            for r in unique:
                f.write(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
        counts[name] = len(unique)
    return counts

    JSON Lines (.jsonl) is one JSON object per line. You can append to it without reading it first, and a crash mid-run loses at most one line. open("a") opens a file for appending. with self._lock, self.path.open("a") as f: uses two context managers in one with: hold the lock and keep the file open, then release both.

    TraceLogger has a __call__ method, so a logger object can be called like a function: logger(event). That means it can be used directly as an on_event callback. chain makes one callback out of several, so one search can be printed and logged.

    records_from_trace turns one run's events into dataset rows. For preferences it groups attempts by goal (dict.setdefault(key, []).append(...) creates the list the first time a goal appears) and pairs every success with every failure. export removes duplicate rows by using each row's JSON text (with sort_keys=True, so key order doesn't matter) as a dictionary key.

  7. Create its tests.

    tests/test_traces.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/traces.py."""

import json

from mathfinder.traces import TraceLogger, chain, export, read_trace, records_from_trace

GOAL = "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"

EVENTS = [
    {"kind": "meta", "theorem": "THM"},
    {"kind": "attempt", "statement": GOAL, "context": [], "proof": "ring", "ok": False, "error": "ring failed", "source": "llm"},
    {"kind": "attempt", "statement": GOAL, "context": [], "proof": "simp", "ok": True, "error": "", "source": "repair"},
    {"kind": "lemma_proved", "depth": 0, "name": "a", "statement": GOAL, "feedback": "LLM"},
    {"kind": "lemma_stuck", "depth": 0, "name": "b", "statement": ": False", "feedback": "no"},
    {"kind": "done", "depth": 0, "lean_code": "theorem main ...", "plan": "p"},
    {"kind": "result", "proved": True, "hints": ["try simp"]},
]


def test_logger_writes_one_json_line_per_event(tmp_path):
    log = TraceLogger(tmp_path / "run", "THM")
    log({"kind": "round", "number": 1})
    log.finish(True, ["a hint"])
    events = read_trace(tmp_path / "run" / "trace.jsonl")
    assert [e["kind"] for e in events] == ["meta", "round", "result"]
    assert events[0]["theorem"] == "THM" and "time" in events[1]
    assert events[2] == {**events[2], "proved": True, "hints": ["a hint"]}


def test_chain_calls_every_watcher():
    seen_a, seen_b = [], []
    chain(seen_a.append, seen_b.append)({"kind": "x"})
    assert seen_a == seen_b == [{"kind": "x"}]


def test_records_from_trace_builds_all_four_datasets():
    rows = records_from_trace(EVENTS)
    assert [r["proof"] for r in rows["sft"]] == ["simp", "theorem main ..."]
    assert rows["sft"][1]["statement"] == "THM"
    assert rows["preferences"] == [{"statement": GOAL, "context": [], "chosen": "simp",
                                    "rejected": "ring", "rejected_error": "ring failed"}]
    assert rows["value"] == [{"statement": GOAL, "label": "proved"}, {"statement": ": False", "label": "stuck"}]
    assert rows["hints"] == [{"theorem": "THM", "hints": ["try simp"], "proved": True}]


def test_export_reads_every_run_and_removes_duplicates(tmp_path):
    for name in ("run1", "run2"):  # the same trace twice: rows must not be doubled
        (tmp_path / "runs" / name).mkdir(parents=True)
        (tmp_path / "runs" / name / "trace.jsonl").write_text("\n".join(json.dumps(e) for e in EVENTS) + "\n")
    counts = export(tmp_path / "runs", tmp_path / "data")
    assert counts == {"sft": 2, "preferences": 1, "value": 2, "hints": 1}
    first = json.loads((tmp_path / "data" / "sft.jsonl").read_text().splitlines()[0])
    assert first["proof"] == "simp"

  8. Log every run and add the export command. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/cli.py
    """Command line.

  uv run python -m mathfinder prove "For every integer $n$, $6 \\mid n^3 - n$."
  uv run python -m mathfinder prove --lean "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"

With LaTeX, you first see the Lean statement, its back-translation and the
judge's opinion, and nothing is searched until you approve.
Every run gets a folder runs/<date-time>/ with trace.jsonl (everything that happened);
a proved theorem also gets proof.lean + explanation.md there.
With --interactive you can type hints and commands while the search runs.

  uv run python -m mathfinder export      builds training data in data/ from all traces
"""

import argparse
import select
import sys
import threading
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

from mathfinder import PROJECT_ROOT
from mathfinder.collab import Session
from mathfinder.explain import explain
from mathfinder.formalize import formalize
from mathfinder.lean import HEADER, statement_errors
from mathfinder.llm import LLMError
from mathfinder.search import search
from mathfinder.traces import TraceLogger, chain, export

RUNS_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / "runs"
DATA_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / "data"

SYMBOL = {"lemma_proved": "✓", "lemma_refuted": "✗", "lemma_stuck": "…"}


def print_event(event: dict) -> None:
    kind = event["kind"]
    if event.get("depth", 0) > 0:  # events from a sub-search are indented under their lemma
        indent = "        " * event["depth"]
        if kind in SYMBOL:
            print(f"{indent}{SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
        elif kind == "plan_start":
            print(f"{indent}▶ sub-plan: {event['sketch']}")
        elif kind == "done":
            print(f"{indent}✓ sub-search proved it")
        sys.stdout.flush()
        return
    if kind == "start":
        print(f"\nTheorem: {event['theorem']}")
    elif kind == "subsearch_start":
        print(f"      ↳ {event['name']} is stuck: starting a sub-search on it")
    elif kind == "refuted":
        print(f"✗ The statement is FALSE. Counterexample: {event['counterexample']}")
    elif kind == "round":
        print(f"\n— round {event['number']}: asking the planner —")
    elif kind == "plan_rejected":
        print(f"  ✗ plan doesn't fit together: {event['sketch']}\n      {event['reason'][:160]}")
    elif kind == "plan_start":
        print(f"  ▶ plan: {event['sketch']}  (lemmas: {', '.join(event['lemmas']) or 'none'})")
    elif kind in SYMBOL:
        print(f"      {SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
    elif kind == "plan_failed":
        print(f"    plan failed (score {event['score']:.2f})")
    elif kind == "done":
        print(f"\n✓ VERIFIED by Lean. Plan: {event['plan']}\n")
        print(event["lean_code"])
    elif kind == "gave_up":
        print("\nNo verified proof found.")
    elif kind == "ask_user":
        print(f"\n❓ {event['question']}\n   (type your answer and press Enter)")
    elif kind == "user_answer":
        print(f"  ← thanks, trying again with your idea: {event['text']}")
    elif kind == "no_answer":
        print("  (no answer)")
    elif kind == "stopped":
        print(f"\n■ Stopped: {event['reason']}")
    sys.stdout.flush()  # show progress immediately, even when output is piped to a file


def approve(latex: str, auto_yes: bool = False, ask=input) -> str | None:
    """Formalize `latex`, show it, and return the statement the user approved (None = no)."""
    print("Formalizing (LaTeX → Lean)...")
    f = formalize(latex)
    if not f.ok:
        print("Could not produce a statement Lean accepts:\n  " + f.errors[0][:400])
        return None
    print(f"\nFormal statement:  theorem main {f.statement}")
    print(f"Back-translation:  {f.back_translation}")
    print(f"Judge:             {'same meaning' if f.faithful else 'MEANING MAY DIFFER'}: {f.judgment}")

    statement = f.statement
    if auto_yes and f.faithful:
        return statement
    if auto_yes:
        print("(--yes ignored: the judge thinks the meaning may differ, so please look.)")
    while True:
        try:
            answer = ask("\nProve this statement? [y]es / [n]o / [e]dit: ").strip().lower()
        except EOFError:  # no keyboard attached (e.g. running in a script)
            return None
        if answer == "y":
            return statement
        if answer == "n":
            return None
        if answer == "e":
            new = ask("Lean statement (what comes after `theorem main`): ").strip()
            errors = statement_errors(new)
            if errors:
                print("Lean rejected it: " + errors[0][:300])
            else:
                statement = new
                print(f"Now:  theorem main {statement}")


COMMANDS = """While it runs you can type:
  hint <text>                     e.g.  hint try induction on n
  lemma <name> <lean statement>   e.g.  lemma key (a b : ℝ) : 0 ≤ (a - b) ^ 2
  reject <lemma name>             plans using that lemma are dropped
  pin <approach>                  "keep going in this direction"
  pause | resume | stop
When the search asks you a question, just type your answer."""


def handle_command(session: Session, line: str) -> str:
    """Apply one typed line to the session. Returns a short confirmation to print."""
    line = line.strip()
    word, _, rest = line.partition(" ")  # "hint try x" -> ("hint", " ", "try x")
    if session.question is not None and word not in ("pause", "resume", "stop"):
        session.answer(line)  # a question is open: the whole line is the answer
        return "answer sent"
    if word == "hint" and rest:
        session.add_hint(rest)
        return f"hint added: {rest}"
    if word == "lemma" and " " in rest:
        name, statement = rest.split(" ", 1)
        session.add_lemma(name, statement)
        return f"lemma suggested: {name}"
    if word == "reject" and rest:
        session.reject_lemma(rest)
        return f"rejected: {rest}"
    if word == "pin" and rest:
        session.pin(rest)
        return f"pinned: {rest}"
    if word == "pause":
        session.pause()
        return "paused (type resume to continue)"
    if word == "resume":
        session.resume()
        return "resumed"
    if word == "stop":
        session.stop()
        return "stopping..."
    return "unknown command. " + COMMANDS


def interactive_search(statement: str, hints: list[str], rounds: int, on_event=print_event):
    """Run the search in a background thread while this thread reads your commands.
    Returns (result, every hint used, including the ones you typed)."""
    session = Session(statement)
    for h in hints:
        session.add_hint(h)
    box = {}
    worker = threading.Thread(
        target=lambda: box.setdefault("result", search(statement, rounds=rounds, on_event=on_event, session=session)),
        daemon=True,  # don't keep Python alive just for this thread
    )
    worker.start()
    print(COMMANDS)
    keyboard_open = True
    while worker.is_alive():
        if not keyboard_open:
            worker.join(0.5)
            continue
        ready, _, _ = select.select([sys.stdin], [], [], 0.5)  # wait up to 0.5 s for a typed line
        if ready:
            line = sys.stdin.readline()
            if line == "":  # end of input (Ctrl-D, or a pipe that closed)
                keyboard_open = False
            elif line.strip():
                print(f"  [{handle_command(session, line)}]")
    return box["result"], session.hints()


def new_run_dir() -> Path:
    """A fresh folder for this run: runs/<date-time>/ (with -2, -3, ... if that name is taken)."""
    base = RUNS_DIR / datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d-%H%M%S")
    folder, n = base, 1
    while folder.exists():
        n += 1
        folder = base.with_name(f"{base.name}-{n}")
    folder.mkdir(parents=True)
    return folder


def save_run(folder: Path, theorem: str, lean_code: str, explanation: str) -> Path:
    """Save a proved theorem into its run folder: theorem.txt, proof.lean, explanation.md."""
    (folder / "theorem.txt").write_text(theorem + "\n")
    (folder / "proof.lean").write_text(HEADER + lean_code)  # a complete file Lean can check on its own
    (folder / "explanation.md").write_text(explanation + "\n")
    return folder


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="mathfinder")
    commands = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    prove = commands.add_parser("prove", help="search for a proof")
    prove.add_argument("latex", nargs="?", help="the theorem in LaTeX (in quotes)")
    prove.add_argument("--lean", help='skip formalization: a Lean statement, e.g. "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"')
    prove.add_argument("--yes", action="store_true", help="don't ask for approval if the judge is happy")
    prove.add_argument("--hint", action="append", default=[], help="a hint for the planner (repeatable)")
    prove.add_argument("--rounds", type=int, default=3)
    prove.add_argument("--no-explain", action="store_true", help="skip the plain-math explanation")
    prove.add_argument("--interactive", action="store_true", help="type hints and commands while it searches")
    export_cmd = commands.add_parser("export", help="build training data from all runs/*/trace.jsonl")
    export_cmd.add_argument("--out", default=str(DATA_DIR), help="output folder (default: data/)")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    if args.command == "export":
        counts = export(RUNS_DIR, Path(args.out))
        for name, count in counts.items():
            print(f"{name:12} {count:5} rows  -> {Path(args.out) / (name + '.jsonl')}")
        return 0

    if args.command == "prove":
        if args.lean:
            statement = args.lean
        elif args.latex:
            statement = approve(args.latex, args.yes)
            if statement is None:
                print("Not searching.")
                return 1
        else:
            parser.error("give a theorem in LaTeX, or --lean STATEMENT")
        folder = new_run_dir()
        logger = TraceLogger(folder, args.latex or statement)
        on_event = chain(print_event, logger)  # print AND log every event
        if args.interactive:
            result, hints_used = interactive_search(statement, args.hint, args.rounds, on_event)
        else:
            result = search(statement, hints=args.hint, rounds=args.rounds, on_event=on_event)
            hints_used = args.hint
        logger.finish(result.proved, hints_used)
        print(f"\nResult: {'PROVED' if result.proved else 'not proved'} ({result.message})")
        print(f"Trace: {logger.path}")
        if not result.proved:
            return 1
        explanation = ""
        if not args.no_explain:
            print("\nWriting the explanation...")
            try:
                explanation = explain(args.latex or statement, result.plan, result.lean_code)
                print("\n" + explanation)
            except LLMError as e:  # the proof is still valid; only the write-up failed
                print(f"(could not write an explanation: {e})")
        print(f"\nSaved to {save_run(folder, args.latex or statement, result.lean_code, explanation)}")
        return 0
    return 2

    What changed: new_run_dir makes the run folder before searching (adding -2, -3 if two runs start in the same second), and save_run now writes into that folder. main wraps the printer and the logger with chain, calls logger.finish(...) with the hints used (including ones typed in --interactive mode, which interactive_search now returns), and prints where the trace is. There's a new export sub-command. f"{name:12}" pads the name to 12 characters, so the counts line up in a column.

  9. Update the command line tests. Replace the whole file:

    tests/test_cli.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/cli.py: approval, explanation, saving, tracing, typed commands. Lean and the model are faked."""

import json

import pytest

from mathfinder import cli
from mathfinder.collab import Session
from mathfinder.formalize import Formalization
from mathfinder.llm import LLMError
from mathfinder.planner import Plan
from mathfinder.search import SearchResult


@pytest.fixture
def fake_formalize(monkeypatch):
    def install(faithful=True):
        result = Formalization("latex", "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n", "For all integers n, 6 | n^3 - n.", faithful, "ok")
        monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "formalize", lambda latex: result)

    return install


def scripted_user(*answers):
    """Stands in for input(): gives the answers in order."""
    queue = list(answers)
    return lambda question: queue.pop(0)


def test_yes_returns_the_statement(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize()
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=scripted_user("y")) == "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"


def test_no_returns_none(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize()
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=scripted_user("n")) is None


def test_edit_is_checked_by_lean_before_it_is_used(fake_formalize, monkeypatch):
    fake_formalize()
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "statement_errors", lambda s: ["unknown identifier 'oops'"] if "oops" in s else [])
    user = scripted_user("e", "(n : ℤ) : oops", "e", "(n : ℕ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n", "y")
    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=user) == "(n : ℕ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"


def test_auto_yes_only_when_the_judge_agrees(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize(faithful=True)
    assert cli.approve("latex", auto_yes=True, ask=scripted_user()) is not None  # never asks
    fake_formalize(faithful=False)
    assert cli.approve("latex", auto_yes=True, ask=scripted_user("n")) is None  # asks anyway


def test_no_keyboard_means_no(fake_formalize):
    fake_formalize()

    def no_keyboard(question):
        raise EOFError

    assert cli.approve("latex", ask=no_keyboard) is None


def test_main_with_latex_and_yes_runs_the_search_explains_and_saves(fake_formalize, monkeypatch, tmp_path):
    fake_formalize()
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "RUNS_DIR", tmp_path)
    seen = {}

    def fake_search(statement, hints, rounds, on_event):
        seen["statement"] = statement
        return SearchResult(True, "theorem main ... := by\n  decide\n", Plan("check all cases", [], "decide"))

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "search", fake_search)
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "explain", lambda theorem, plan, code: "## Theorem\nexplained")
    latex = r"For every integer $n$, $6 \mid n^3 - n$."
    assert cli.main(["prove", latex, "--yes"]) == 0
    assert seen["statement"] == "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"

    [run] = tmp_path.iterdir()  # exactly one run folder was created
    assert (run / "theorem.txt").read_text() == latex + "\n"
    assert (run / "proof.lean").read_text().startswith("import Mathlib")
    assert (run / "explanation.md").read_text() == "## Theorem\nexplained\n"
    trace = [json.loads(line) for line in (run / "trace.jsonl").read_text().splitlines()]
    assert trace[0]["kind"] == "meta" and trace[-1] == {**trace[-1], "kind": "result", "proved": True}


def test_a_failed_explanation_still_saves_the_proof(monkeypatch, tmp_path, capsys):
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "RUNS_DIR", tmp_path)
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "search", lambda *a, **k: SearchResult(True, "code\n", Plan("p", [], "simp")))

    def broken_explain(theorem, plan, code):
        raise LLMError("model unavailable")

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "explain", broken_explain)
    assert cli.main(["prove", "--lean", "(n : ℕ) : n = n"]) == 0
    assert "could not write an explanation" in capsys.readouterr().out
    assert len(list(tmp_path.iterdir())) == 1


def test_typed_commands_change_the_session():
    s = Session("t")
    assert cli.handle_command(s, "hint try induction on n\n") == "hint added: try induction on n"
    assert cli.handle_command(s, "lemma key (a : ℝ) : 0 ≤ a ^ 2").startswith("lemma suggested: key")
    assert cli.handle_command(s, "reject bad_step") == "rejected: bad_step"
    assert cli.handle_command(s, "pause").startswith("paused")
    assert cli.handle_command(s, "resume") == "resumed"
    assert "theorem key (a : ℝ) : 0 ≤ a ^ 2" in s.hints()[1]
    assert cli.handle_command(s, "dance").startswith("unknown command")


def test_while_a_question_is_open_any_line_is_the_answer():
    s = Session("t")
    s.question = "Any idea?"
    assert cli.handle_command(s, "hint this whole line is an answer") == "answer sent"
    assert s.ask("Any idea?") == "hint this whole line is an answer"


def test_export_command_prints_row_counts(monkeypatch, tmp_path, capsys):
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "RUNS_DIR", tmp_path / "runs")
    (tmp_path / "runs").mkdir()
    assert cli.main(["export", "--out", str(tmp_path / "data")]) == 0
    out = capsys.readouterr().out
    assert "sft" in out and "preferences" in out and (tmp_path / "data" / "value.jsonl").exists()

    What changed: the main test also checks trace.jsonl (first line meta, last line result), and a new test runs export on an empty runs folder.

Checkpoint

What we got (about 1½ minutes for the proof):

  ▶ plan: Write x + 1/x as the single fraction (x*x+1)/x. Clear the denominator with le_div_iff₀, ...  (lemmas: combine_frac, quad_ineq)
      ✓ combine_frac : (x : ℝ) (hx : 0 < x) : x + 1 / x = (x * x + 1) / x   [hammer: field_simp]
      ✓ quad_ineq : (x : ℝ) : 2 * x ≤ x * x + 1   [LLM]
✓ VERIFIED by Lean. ...
Result: PROVED (verified)
Trace: /Users/you/Desktop/math_proofs/runs/20261114-152642/trace.jsonl

sft              3 rows  -> .../data/sft.jsonl
preferences      0 rows  -> .../data/preferences.jsonl
value            2 rows  -> .../data/value.jsonl
hints            0 rows  -> .../data/hints.jsonl

Your counts depend on all your runs so far. Preferences appear only when some attempt on a goal failed and another succeeded. Easy theorems often have none; harder ones (M12) produce plenty. Look inside: head -c 600 data/sft.jsonl.

Fast tests:

.................................................................        [100%]
65 passed, 29 deselected in 0.77s

Common errors

  • export shows all zeros: no runs/*/trace.jsonl files yet. Runs from before this chapter have no trace; prove something new.
  • json.decoder.JSONDecodeError reading a trace: a run was killed mid-write. Delete that run's last (broken) line, or the folder.
  • TypeError: fake_prove() got an unexpected keyword argument 'on_attempt': a test fake needs on_attempt=None (step 5).

M9: The web server

What and why. The website (M10) runs in your browser and can't call Python functions directly. It talks to a server over the network. This chapter puts the whole engine behind one, in two kinds of connection:

  • REST endpoints (plain HTTP requests, like loading a web page): "formalize this LaTeX", "start a search", "add this hint", "pause". Each request gets one reply.
  • A WebSocket: a connection that stays open, so the server can push every search event to the page the moment it happens (plan proposed, lemma proved, question for you) without the page asking over and over.

New ideas in this chapter

  • FastAPI is a Python web framework. You write a normal function and put @app.post("/api/check") above it, and FastAPI calls it for every POST request to that address, turning the returned dictionary into JSON.
  • pydantic models (class CheckRequest(BaseModel)) describe what a request body must look like. FastAPI checks every request against them and replies 422 by itself if something's missing or the wrong type.
  • async / await: an async def function can pause at each await (say, while waiting for the next event) and let the server work on other connections in the meantime. One thread (the event loop) juggles many WebSockets this way. uvicorn is the program that runs the event loop and our app.
  • Two worlds meeting: our search is slow, blocking code (Lean, the model), so it runs in ordinary threads, while the WebSockets live in the event loop. An asyncio.Queue may only be touched from its own event loop, so the search thread uses loop.call_soon_threadsafe(q.put_nowait, event), which means "event loop, please put this in the queue yourself".

  1. Add the web libraries. This edits pyproject.toml and installs them:

    uv add "fastapi>=0.115" "uvicorn[standard]>=0.32"
uv add --dev "httpx>=0.27"

    uvicorn[standard] includes the extras for WebSockets (the websockets package), and httpx is what FastAPI's test client uses.

  2. Let tests import from the project root. The whole file should now look like this:

    pyproject.toml

    What changed: the two new dependency lines from uv add, httpx in the dev group, and pythonpath = ["."]. That last one lets tests import server.app, since server/ sits next to mathfinder/ rather than inside it.

  3. Make server/ a package.

    server/__init__.py
    """The Math Finder web server (FastAPI). Run it with:  uv run uvicorn server.app:app --port 8000"""

  4. Create the server.

    server/app.py
    """The web server: the search engine, reachable from a browser.

  POST /api/formalize            {latex}             → Lean statement + back-translation + judge
  POST /api/check                {statement}         → Lean errors for a statement you edited ([] = fine)
  POST /api/sessions             {statement, ...}    → starts a search in the background, returns its id
  GET  /api/sessions/{id}                            → status, open question, result
  POST /api/sessions/{id}/{action}  {text|name|statement}
        action = hint | lemma | reject | pin | pause | resume | stop | answer | edit
  WS   /ws/{id}                                      → every event of that search, live

Each search runs in its own thread (it's slow, blocking work: Lean and the model).
The web server itself is asynchronous: one thread juggles many connections.
The Run class is the bridge between the two worlds.
"""

import asyncio
import threading
import uuid
from dataclasses import asdict

from fastapi import FastAPI, HTTPException, WebSocket, WebSocketDisconnect
from pydantic import BaseModel

import mathfinder.explain as explainer
import mathfinder.formalize as formalizer
import mathfinder.search as engine
from mathfinder import cli
from mathfinder.collab import Session
from mathfinder.lean import statement_errors
from mathfinder.llm import LLMError
from mathfinder.traces import TraceLogger

app = FastAPI(title="Math Finder")


# ---------- request bodies (pydantic checks their shape for us) ----------


class FormalizeRequest(BaseModel):
    latex: str


class CheckRequest(BaseModel):
    statement: str


class StartRequest(BaseModel):
    statement: str  # the approved Lean statement
    latex: str = ""  # the original LaTeX, used for the explanation
    hints: list[str] = []
    rounds: int = 3


class ActionRequest(BaseModel):
    text: str = ""  # hint / pin / answer text
    name: str = ""  # lemma name (lemma, reject)
    statement: str = ""  # lemma statement (lemma) or new theorem statement (edit)


# ---------- one running search ----------


class Run:
    """A search running in a background thread, plus everyone watching it."""

    def __init__(self, statement: str, latex: str, hints: list[str], rounds: int):
        self.id = uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]
        self.statement, self.latex, self.hints, self.rounds = statement, latex, hints, rounds
        self.session = Session(statement)
        for h in hints:
            self.session.add_hint(h)
        self.status = "running"  # running | proved | not_proved | stopped
        self.result = None
        self.events = []  # full history, so a page that connects late can catch up
        self.listeners = []  # (event loop, asyncio.Queue) for each open WebSocket
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.folder = cli.new_run_dir()
        self.logger = TraceLogger(self.folder, latex or statement)

    def on_event(self, event: dict) -> None:
        """Called from the SEARCH thread for every event."""
        self.logger(event)
        with self.lock:
            self.events.append(event)
            listeners = list(self.listeners)
        for loop, q in listeners:
            # asyncio queues aren't thread-safe: ask each WebSocket's event loop to do the put itself
            loop.call_soon_threadsafe(q.put_nowait, event)

    def subscribe(self, loop) -> tuple[list[dict], asyncio.Queue]:
        q = asyncio.Queue()
        with self.lock:  # history + registration together, so no event is missed or sent twice
            self.listeners.append((loop, q))
            return list(self.events), q

    def unsubscribe(self, q: asyncio.Queue) -> None:
        with self.lock:
            self.listeners = [(l, x) for l, x in self.listeners if x is not q]

    def start(self) -> None:
        threading.Thread(target=self._work, daemon=True).start()

    def _work(self) -> None:
        try:
            result = engine.search(self.statement, rounds=self.rounds, on_event=self.on_event, session=self.session)
        except Exception as e:  # never let a crash leave the page waiting forever
            self.status, self.result = "not_proved", {"proved": False, "message": f"internal error: {e}"}
            self.on_event({"kind": "finished", "status": self.status, "result": self.result})
            return
        self.logger.finish(result.proved, self.session.hints())
        explanation = ""
        if result.proved:
            self.on_event({"kind": "explaining"})
            try:
                explanation = explainer.explain(self.latex or self.statement, result.plan, result.lean_code)
            except LLMError as e:
                explanation = f"(could not write an explanation: {e})"
            cli.save_run(self.folder, self.latex or self.statement, result.lean_code, explanation)
        stop = self.session.checkpoint()
        self.status = "proved" if result.proved else ("stopped" if stop else "not_proved")
        self.result = {
            "proved": result.proved,
            "message": result.message,
            "lean_code": result.lean_code,
            "explanation": explanation,
            "plan": result.plan.sketch if result.plan else "",
            "rounds": result.rounds,
            "folder": str(self.folder),
        }
        self.on_event({"kind": "finished", "status": self.status, "result": self.result})


RUNS: dict[str, Run] = {}


def get_run(run_id: str) -> Run:
    if run_id not in RUNS:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="no such session")
    return RUNS[run_id]


# ---------- endpoints ----------


@app.post("/api/formalize")
def formalize(req: FormalizeRequest):
    # A plain `def` (not `async def`): FastAPI runs it in a worker thread, so this slow call
    # (model + Lean) doesn't freeze the server for everyone else.
    f = formalizer.formalize(req.latex)
    return {**asdict(f), "ok": f.ok}


@app.post("/api/check")
def check(req: CheckRequest):
    return {"errors": statement_errors(req.statement)}


@app.post("/api/sessions")
def start_session(req: StartRequest):
    run = Run(req.statement, req.latex, req.hints, req.rounds)
    RUNS[run.id] = run
    run.start()
    return {"id": run.id}


@app.get("/api/sessions/{run_id}")
def session_status(run_id: str):
    run = get_run(run_id)
    return {
        "id": run.id,
        "statement": run.statement,
        "status": run.status,
        "question": run.session.question,
        "hints": run.session.hints(),
        "events": len(run.events),
        "result": run.result,
    }


@app.post("/api/sessions/{run_id}/{action}")
def session_action(run_id: str, action: str, req: ActionRequest):
    run = get_run(run_id)
    s = run.session
    if action == "hint":
        s.add_hint(req.text)
    elif action == "lemma":
        s.add_lemma(req.name, req.statement, req.text)
    elif action == "reject":
        s.reject_lemma(req.name or req.statement)
    elif action == "pin":
        s.pin(req.text)
    elif action == "pause":
        s.pause()
    elif action == "resume":
        s.resume()
    elif action == "stop":
        s.stop()
    elif action == "answer":
        s.answer(req.text)
    elif action == "edit":  # stop this search and start a fresh one on the new statement
        errors = statement_errors(req.statement)
        if errors:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=f"Lean rejected the statement: {errors[0][:300]}")
        s.edit_statement(req.statement)
        new = Run(req.statement, "", s.hints(), run.rounds)
        RUNS[new.id] = new
        new.start()
        return {"ok": True, "new_id": new.id}
    else:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=f"unknown action {action!r}")
    return {"ok": True}


@app.websocket("/ws/{run_id}")
async def watch(websocket: WebSocket, run_id: str):
    await websocket.accept()
    if run_id not in RUNS:
        await websocket.close(code=4404)
        return
    run = RUNS[run_id]
    history, q = run.subscribe(asyncio.get_running_loop())
    try:
        for event in history:
            await websocket.send_json(event)
            if event["kind"] == "finished":
                await websocket.close()  # nothing more will happen
                return
        while True:
            event = await q.get()  # `await` = pause here until an event arrives, without blocking the server
            await websocket.send_json(event)
            if event["kind"] == "finished":
                await websocket.close()
                return
    except WebSocketDisconnect:
        pass  # the browser tab closed; that's fine
    finally:
        run.unsubscribe(q)

    import mathfinder.search as engine: we import the module and call engine.search(...) rather than importing the function. Tests can then swap in a fake with monkeypatch.setattr(srv.engine, "search", fake).

    Run is one search in progress. on_event is called from the search thread. It logs the event, appends it to events (the history, so a page that connects late can catch up), and hands it to every open WebSocket's queue. subscribe takes the history and registers the new listener under one lock, so an event arriving at that exact moment is neither missed nor sent twice. _work runs the search, writes the explanation, saves the run, and always ends with a finished event, even after a crash, so the page never waits forever.

    Plain def endpoints like formalize do slow blocking work. FastAPI runs those in a worker thread, so they don't freeze the event loop. Only watch is async, because it spends its life waiting.

    HTTPException(status_code=404) sends an HTTP error code instead of a normal reply: 404 means "no such session", 400 means "bad request". edit checks the new statement with Lean first, stops the old search, and starts a new one that keeps your hints.

    watch is the WebSocket endpoint. It accepts the connection, replays the history, then loops on await q.get(), sending each event with send_json until finished, and then closes. WebSocketDisconnect means the browser went away, and finally: run.unsubscribe(q) stops sending to it.

  5. Create the server tests.

    tests/test_server.py
    """Tests for server/app.py. The search, explainer and formalizer are faked, so no Lean or model is needed."""

import pytest
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

import server.app as srv
from mathfinder import cli
from mathfinder.formalize import Formalization
from mathfinder.planner import Plan
from mathfinder.search import SearchResult


@pytest.fixture
def client(monkeypatch, tmp_path):
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "RUNS_DIR", tmp_path)  # run folders go to a temporary place
    monkeypatch.setattr(srv.explainer, "explain", lambda theorem, plan, code: "## Proof\nby simp")
    srv.RUNS.clear()
    return TestClient(srv.app)


def fake_search_that_proves(statement, rounds, on_event, session):
    on_event({"kind": "start", "depth": 0, "theorem": statement})
    on_event({"kind": "lemma_proved", "depth": 0, "name": "key", "statement": ": True", "feedback": "hammer"})
    on_event({"kind": "done", "depth": 0, "lean_code": "theorem main ...", "plan": "simp it"})
    return SearchResult(True, "theorem main ...", Plan("simp it", [], "simp"), 1, "verified")


def watch_until_finished(client, run_id):
    with client.websocket_connect(f"/ws/{run_id}") as ws:
        events = []
        while not events or events[-1]["kind"] != "finished":
            events.append(ws.receive_json())
    return events


def test_formalize_endpoint(client, monkeypatch):
    f = Formalization("x", "(n : ℕ) : n = n", "For all n, n = n.", True, "same")
    monkeypatch.setattr(srv.formalizer, "formalize", lambda latex: f)
    data = client.post("/api/formalize", json={"latex": "$n = n$"}).json()
    assert data["statement"] == "(n : ℕ) : n = n" and data["ok"] and data["faithful"]


def test_a_search_streams_its_events_and_ends_with_the_result(client, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(srv.engine, "search", fake_search_that_proves)
    run_id = client.post("/api/sessions", json={"statement": "(n : ℕ) : n = n"}).json()["id"]

    events = watch_until_finished(client, run_id)
    kinds = [e["kind"] for e in events]
    assert kinds == ["start", "lemma_proved", "done", "explaining", "finished"]
    result = events[-1]["result"]
    assert result["proved"] and result["explanation"] == "## Proof\nby simp"

    status = client.get(f"/api/sessions/{run_id}").json()
    assert status["status"] == "proved"
    assert (cli.RUNS_DIR / status["result"]["folder"].split("/")[-1] / "proof.lean").exists()


def test_commands_reach_the_session_while_it_waits_for_an_answer(client, monkeypatch):
    def fake_search_that_asks(statement, rounds, on_event, session):
        on_event({"kind": "ask_user", "depth": 0, "question": "Any idea?"})
        answer = session.ask("Any idea?")
        on_event({"kind": "user_answer", "depth": 0, "text": answer})
        return SearchResult(False, message=f"hints were {session.hints()}")

    monkeypatch.setattr(srv.engine, "search", fake_search_that_asks)
    run_id = client.post("/api/sessions", json={"statement": "t", "hints": ["start hint"]}).json()["id"]
    assert client.post(f"/api/sessions/{run_id}/hint", json={"text": "try cases"}).json() == {"ok": True}
    with client.websocket_connect(f"/ws/{run_id}") as ws:
        assert ws.receive_json()["kind"] == "ask_user"
        assert client.get(f"/api/sessions/{run_id}").json()["question"] == "Any idea?"
        client.post(f"/api/sessions/{run_id}/answer", json={"text": "use induction"})
        assert ws.receive_json() == {"kind": "user_answer", "depth": 0, "text": "use induction"}
        final = ws.receive_json()
    assert final["kind"] == "finished" and final["status"] == "not_proved"
    assert final["result"]["message"] == "hints were ['start hint', 'try cases']"


def test_edit_checks_the_statement_then_starts_a_new_search(client, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(srv.engine, "search", fake_search_that_proves)
    monkeypatch.setattr(srv, "statement_errors", lambda s: ["bad"] if "oops" in s else [])
    run_id = client.post("/api/sessions", json={"statement": "t"}).json()["id"]
    assert client.post(f"/api/sessions/{run_id}/edit", json={"statement": "oops"}).status_code == 400
    new_id = client.post(f"/api/sessions/{run_id}/edit", json={"statement": "(n : ℕ) : n = n"}).json()["new_id"]
    assert new_id != run_id
    assert watch_until_finished(client, new_id)[-1]["status"] == "proved"


def test_unknown_session_and_unknown_action(client, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(srv.engine, "search", fake_search_that_proves)
    assert client.get("/api/sessions/nope").status_code == 404
    run_id = client.post("/api/sessions", json={"statement": "t"}).json()["id"]
    assert client.post(f"/api/sessions/{run_id}/dance", json={}).status_code == 400

    TestClient runs the app in-process: client.post(...) and client.websocket_connect(...) behave like real requests, with no network and no uvicorn. The fake searches emit events and use the session exactly like the real one. One of them asks a question and waits, and the test answers through the REST endpoint while watching the WebSocket, the same way the web page will.

  6. Create a small terminal watcher, handy for debugging the server without a browser.

    tools/watch.py
    """Watch a running search from the terminal, one line per event.

  uv run python tools/watch.py <session id> [port]

It connects to the server's WebSocket and prints every event until the search finishes.
"""

import json
import sys

from websockets.sync.client import connect


def main() -> None:
    run_id = sys.argv[1]
    port = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "8765"
    with connect(f"ws://localhost:{port}/ws/{run_id}") as ws:
        for message in ws:  # the loop ends when the server closes the connection
            event = json.loads(message)
            details = {k: v for k, v in event.items() if k not in ("kind", "depth")}
            print(f"{event['kind']:15} {json.dumps(details, ensure_ascii=False)[:140]}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

    websockets.sync.client.connect is the simple, non-async client. Iterating over the connection yields each message, and the loop ends when the server closes it.

  7. Run the tests:

    uv run pytest -q -m "not lean and not llm"

  8. Start the server in its own terminal window and leave it running:

    uv run uvicorn server.app:app --port 8765

    server.app:app means "the variable app in the module server.app". We use port 8765 because 8000, the usual default, is often taken by another program.

  9. In a second terminal, formalize a theorem over HTTP with curl:

    curl -s -X POST localhost:8765/api/formalize -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"latex": "For every natural number $n$, $n^2 + n$ is even."}' | python3 -m json.tool

  10. Start a search, send it a hint, and watch it live:

    ID=$(curl -s -X POST localhost:8765/api/sessions -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"statement": "(n : ℕ) : Even (n ^ 2 + n)", "latex": "For every natural number $n$, $n^2 + n$ is even."}' \
  | python3 -c "import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['id'])")
curl -s -X POST localhost:8765/api/sessions/$ID/hint -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"text": "n^2 + n = n * (n + 1), a product of consecutive numbers"}'
uv run python tools/watch.py $ID

    ID=$(...) stores a command's output in a shell variable, and $ID uses it.

Checkpoint

The formalize call (about 40 s) returned:

{
    "latex": "For every natural number $n$, $n^2 + n$ is even.",
    "statement": "(n : ℕ) : Even (n ^ 2 + n)",
    "back_translation": "For every $n \\in \\mathbb{N}$, the number $n^2 + n$ is even.",
    "faithful": true,
    ...
    "ok": true
}

ℕ is just how JSON spells ℕ.

The watcher printed (shortened):

start           {"theorem": "(n : ℕ) : Even (n ^ 2 + n)"}
round           {"number": 1}
plans           {"sketches": ["Rewrite n^2 + n as n * (n + 1) by ring, then apply Mathlib's Nat.even_mul_succ_self, ...
plan_start      {"sketch": "Rewrite n^2 + n as n * (n + 1) ...", "lemmas": ["sq_add_self_eq_mul_succ"]}
lemma_start     {"name": "sq_add_self_eq_mul_succ", "statement": "(n : ℕ) : n ^ 2 + n = n * (n + 1)"}
attempt         {"statement": "(n : ℕ) : n ^ 2 + n = n * (n + 1)", "context": [], "proof": "linarith", "ok": true, ...
lemma_proved    {"name": "sq_add_self_eq_mul_succ", ..., "feedback": "hammer: linarith"}
done            {"lean_code": "namespace MathFinder\n\ntheorem sq_add_self_eq_mul_succ ...
explaining      {}
finished        {"status": "proved", "result": {"proved": true, "message": "verified", ...

And in the server's terminal you'll see one line per request, like "POST /api/sessions HTTP/1.1" 200 OK.

Fast tests:

......................................................................   [100%]
70 passed, 29 deselected in 0.97s

Common errors

  • [Errno 48] ... address already in use: another program has that port. Pick another (--port 8766) and use it everywhere.
  • {"detail":"Method Not Allowed"} from curl: you reached a different program on that port, or forgot -X POST.
  • {"detail":[{"type":"missing",...}]} (status 422): the JSON body is missing a field. Compare it with the pydantic model.
  • ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'server' in tests: pythonpath = ["."] is missing from pyproject.toml.
  • The watcher exits right away: the session id is wrong (the server closes unknown ids with code 4404).

M10: The website

What and why. Everything so far happens in a terminal. This chapter gives Math Finder a face: one web page that walks you through the whole story. (1) Type a theorem in LaTeX and see it typeset as you type. (2) Check and approve its Lean form (or edit it). (3) Watch the search live as a tree of plans and lemmas, colour-coded by status, with a chat panel next to it for hints and answers; click any lemma to hint about it, reject it, or pin its plan. (4) Read the result: the explanation with real maths typesetting, the verified Lean proof, and a few numbers about the search.

Three languages, one page

  • HTML (index.html) is the structure: which boxes and buttons exist. Each important element has an id, so the code can find it.
  • CSS (style.css) is the look. Colours are defined once as variables (--ok: #6fd39a) and used everywhere (var(--ok)). A class like .lemma.proved styles every lemma that is proved.
  • JavaScript (app.js) is the behaviour: it reacts to clicks, talks to the server, and redraws the tree when events arrive.

Two libraries are loaded from a CDN (a public file server), so the page needs an internet connection: KaTeX typesets $...$ maths, and marked turns the Markdown explanation into HTML.

  1. Create the page's structure.

    web/index.html
    <!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8">
<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
<title>Math Finder</title>
<link rel="stylesheet" href="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/katex@0.16.11/dist/katex.min.css">
<script defer src="https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/katex@0.16.11/dist/katex.min.js"></script>

<link rel="stylesheet" href="style.css">
<script defer src="app.js"></script>
</head>
<body>
<header>
  <h1>Math Finder</h1>
  <p>Write a theorem. Approve its Lean form. Work with the search until Lean verifies a proof.</p>
</header>

<main>
  <!-- 1. the theorem -->
  <section id="step-input" class="card">
    <h2><span class="num">1</span> Your theorem, in LaTeX</h2>
    <textarea id="latex" rows="3" spellcheck="false">For all positive reals $a, b$: $\frac{a}{b} + \frac{b}{a} \ge 2$.</textarea>
    <div class="label">Preview</div>
    <div id="preview" class="preview"></div>
    <div class="label">Hints for the search (optional, one per line)</div>

    <div class="row">
      <button id="formalize-btn" class="primary">Formalize</button>
      <span id="formalize-status" class="muted"></span>
    </div>
  </section>

  <!-- 2. approve the formal statement -->
  <section id="step-approve" class="card hidden">
    <h2><span class="num">2</span> Is this what you mean?</h2>
    <div class="label">Lean statement (you can edit it)</div>
    <div class="lean-line"><code>theorem main</code><input id="statement" spellcheck="false"></div>
    <div id="check-errors" class="error hidden"></div>

    <div id="back" class="preview"></div>
    <div id="verdict" class="verdict"></div>
    <div class="row">
      <button id="check-btn">Check my edit</button>
      <button id="approve-btn" class="primary">Approve &amp; search</button>
    </div>
  </section>

  <!-- 3. the search, live -->
  <section id="step-search" class="card hidden">
    <h2><span class="num">3</span> Searching <span id="search-status" class="badge">running</span></h2>
    <div class="search-layout">
      <div>
        <div class="label">Plans and lemmas (click a lemma for options)</div>
        <div id="tree" class="tree"></div>
      </div>
      <div class="chat">
        <div class="label">Work together</div>
        <div id="question" class="question hidden"></div>
        <div id="log" class="log"></div>
        <div class="row">
          <input id="chat-input" placeholder="Type a hint, e.g. try induction on n" spellcheck="false">
          <button id="send-btn" class="primary">Send</button>
        </div>
        <details>
          <summary>Suggest a lemma</summary>
          <div class="row">
            <input id="lemma-name" placeholder="name, e.g. key" spellcheck="false">
            <input id="lemma-statement" placeholder="(a b : ℝ) : 0 ≤ (a - b) ^ 2" spellcheck="false">
            <button id="lemma-btn">Suggest</button>
          </div>
        </details>
        <div class="row">
          <button id="pause-btn">Pause</button>
          <button id="stop-btn" class="danger">Stop</button>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>

  <!-- 4. the result -->
  <section id="step-result" class="card hidden">
    <h2><span class="num">4</span> <span id="result-title">Result</span></h2>
    <div id="metrics" class="metrics"></div>
    <div id="explanation" class="explanation"></div>
    <div class="label">The Lean proof, verified by Lean</div>
    <pre id="lean-code"></pre>
    <p id="saved" class="muted"></p>
  </section>
</main>

<div id="lemma-menu" class="menu hidden"></div>
</body>
</html>

    The four <section>s are the four steps. All but the first start with class="hidden", and the code removes that class when it's time to show them. defer on the scripts means "download now, run after the page is built", so app.js can safely look up elements by id.

  2. Create the look.

    web/style.css

    display: grid; grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 3fr) minmax(0, 2fr) puts the tree and the chat side by side (60% / 40%), and the @media (max-width: 900px) rule stacks them on narrow screens. The lemma status symbols (○ ◐ ✓ ✗ …) come from CSS alone: .lemma.proved::before { content: "✓ " } inserts the symbol before the text, so the code only has to set the class.

  3. Create the behaviour.

    web/app.js
    // Math Finder web page. Talks to server/app.py: REST for actions, a WebSocket for live events.

const $ = (id) => document.getElementById(id);

const state = {
  latex: "",
  sessionId: null,
  plans: [],          // [{sketch, status, reason, lemmas: [{name, statement, status, feedback}]}]
  current: null,      // the plan being worked on right now
  question: null,     // the open question from the search, if any
  paused: false,
  startedAt: 0,
  attempts: 0,
  attemptsOk: 0,
};

// ---------- talking to the server ----------

async function post(path, body = {}) {
  const response = await fetch(path, {
    method: "POST",
    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify(body),
  });
  const data = await response.json();
  if (!response.ok) throw new Error(data.detail || response.statusText);
  return data;
}

function action(name, body = {}) {
  return post(`/api/sessions/${state.sessionId}/${name}`, body);
}

// ---------- math and markdown ----------

function renderMath(el) {
  if (window.renderMathInElement) {
    renderMathInElement(el, {
      delimiters: [
        { left: "$$", right: "$$", display: true },
        { left: "$", right: "$", display: false },
      ],
      throwOnError: false,
    });
  }
}

function escapeHtml(text) {
  return text.replace(/&/g, "&amp;").replace(/</g, "&lt;").replace(/>/g, "&gt;");
}

// Markdown would mangle LaTeX (it treats _ and \ specially), so we hide the math first,
// let marked do the Markdown, put the math back, and let KaTeX draw it.
function renderMarkdown(el, text) {
  const math = [];
  const hidden = text.replace(/\$\$[\s\S]+?\$\$|\$[^$\n]+?\$/g, (m) => {
    math.push(m);
    return `@@MATH${math.length - 1}@@`;
  });
  let html = window.marked ? marked.parse(escapeHtml(hidden)) : `<pre>${escapeHtml(hidden)}</pre>`;
  html = html.replace(/@@MATH(\d+)@@/g, (_, i) => escapeHtml(math[Number(i)]));
  el.innerHTML = html;
  renderMath(el);
}

function showText(el, text) {
  el.textContent = text;
  renderMath(el);
}

// ---------- step 1: LaTeX in, live preview ----------

function updatePreview() {
  showText($("preview"), $("latex").value);
}

async function formalize() {
  state.latex = $("latex").value.trim();
  $("formalize-btn").disabled = true;
  $("formalize-status").textContent = "Translating to Lean and checking it (about 30 s)…";
  try {
    const f = await post("/api/formalize", { latex: state.latex });
    if (!f.ok) {
      $("formalize-status").textContent = "Lean couldn't accept any translation: " + (f.errors[0] || "");
      return;
    }
    $("formalize-status").textContent = "";
    $("statement").value = f.statement;
    showText($("back"), f.back_translation);
    const v = $("verdict");
    v.className = "verdict " + (f.faithful ? "ok" : "warn");
    v.textContent = (f.faithful ? "✓ Same meaning: " : "⚠ The meaning may differ: ") + f.judgment;
    $("check-errors").classList.add("hidden");
    $("step-approve").classList.remove("hidden");
    $("step-approve").scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth" });
  } catch (e) {
    $("formalize-status").textContent = "Error: " + e.message;
  } finally {
    $("formalize-btn").disabled = false;
  }
}

// ---------- step 2: approve ----------

async function checkEdit() {
  const box = $("check-errors");
  const { errors } = await post("/api/check", { statement: $("statement").value });
  box.classList.toggle("hidden", errors.length === 0);
  box.textContent = errors.length ? "Lean rejected it:\n" + errors[0] : "";
  if (!errors.length) {
    box.classList.remove("hidden");
    box.textContent = "✓ Lean accepts this statement.";
  }
  return errors.length === 0;
}

async function approve() {
  $("approve-btn").disabled = true;
  const hints = $("start-hints").value.split("\n").map((h) => h.trim()).filter(Boolean);
  const { id } = await post("/api/sessions", { statement: $("statement").value, latex: state.latex, hints });
  startWatching(id);
  hints.forEach((h) => addMessage(`hint: ${h}`, "you"));
}

// ---------- step 3: the live search ----------

function startWatching(id) {
  Object.assign(state, { sessionId: id, plans: [], current: null, question: null, startedAt: Date.now(),
                         attempts: 0, attemptsOk: 0, paused: false });
  $("log").innerHTML = "";
  $("step-result").classList.add("hidden");
  $("step-search").classList.remove("hidden");
  setStatus("running");
  renderTree();
  $("step-search").scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth" });

  const protocol = location.protocol === "https:" ? "wss" : "ws";
  const ws = new WebSocket(`${protocol}://${location.host}/ws/${id}`);
  ws.onmessage = (message) => handleEvent(JSON.parse(message.data));
  ws.onclose = () => addMessage("(connection closed)", "sys");
}

function setStatus(status) {
  const badge = $("search-status");
  badge.textContent = status.replace("_", " ");
  badge.className = "badge " + status;
}

function addMessage(text, who = "sys") {
  const div = document.createElement("div");
  div.className = "msg " + who;
  div.textContent = text;
  $("log").appendChild(div);
  $("log").scrollTop = $("log").scrollHeight;
}

function findLemma(name) {
  return state.current && state.current.lemmas.find((l) => l.name === name);
}

function handleEvent(e) {
  const deep = (e.depth || 0) > 0; // events from a sub-search on a stuck lemma
  switch (e.kind) {
    case "start":
      if (!deep) addMessage("Search started.");
      break;
    case "refuted":
      addMessage(`The statement is FALSE. Counterexample: ${e.counterexample}`);
      break;
    case "round":
      if (!deep) addMessage(`Round ${e.number}: asking the planner…`);
      break;
    case "plan_rejected":
      if (!deep) state.plans.push({ sketch: e.sketch, status: "rejected", reason: e.reason, lemmas: [] });
      break;
    case "plan_start":
      if (deep) { addMessage(`  sub-plan: ${e.sketch}`); break; }
      state.current = { sketch: e.sketch, status: "active", reason: "",
                        lemmas: e.lemmas.map((name) => ({ name, statement: "", status: "open", feedback: "" })) };
      state.plans.push(state.current);
      break;
    case "lemma_start": {
      if (deep) break;
      const l = findLemma(e.name);
      if (l) Object.assign(l, { statement: e.statement, status: "working" });
      break;
    }
    case "subsearch_start":
      addMessage(`${e.name} is stuck: starting a smaller search on it.`);
      break;
    case "lemma_proved":
    case "lemma_refuted":
    case "lemma_stuck": {
      const status = e.kind.replace("lemma_", "");
      if (deep) { addMessage(`  sub-lemma ${e.name}: ${status}`); break; }
      let l = findLemma(e.name);
      if (!l && state.current) { // a lemma spliced in from a sub-search
        l = { name: e.name, statement: e.statement, status, feedback: "" };
        state.current.lemmas.push(l);
      }
      if (l) Object.assign(l, { statement: e.statement, status, feedback: e.feedback });
      break;
    }
    case "attempt":
      state.attempts += 1;
      if (e.ok) state.attemptsOk += 1;
      break;
    case "plan_failed":
      if (!deep && state.current) state.current.status = "failed";
      break;
    case "done":
      if (deep) break;
      if (state.current) state.current.status = "proved";
      else { // proved directly by automation: there was no plan, so show one node for it
        state.current = { sketch: e.plan, status: "proved", reason: "", lemmas: [] };
        state.plans.push(state.current);
      }
      addMessage("✓ Lean verified a proof.");
      break;
    case "ask_user":
      state.question = e.question;
      $("question").textContent = "❓ " + e.question;
      $("question").classList.remove("hidden");
      $("chat-input").placeholder = "Type your answer…";
      $("chat-input").focus();
      break;
    case "user_answer":
      addMessage("Trying again with your idea.");
      break;
    case "no_answer":
      addMessage("(no answer)");
      break;
    case "stopped":
      addMessage(`Stopped: ${e.reason}`);
      break;
    case "gave_up":
      addMessage("No verified proof found.");
      break;
    case "explaining":
      addMessage("Writing the explanation…");
      break;
    case "finished":
      showResult(e);
      break;
  }
  renderTree();
}

function renderTree() {
  const tree = $("tree");
  tree.innerHTML = "";
  if (!state.plans.length) {
    tree.innerHTML = '<div class="muted">Waiting for the first plans…</div>';
    return;
  }
  state.plans.forEach((plan) => {
    const div = document.createElement("div");
    div.className = "plan " + plan.status;
    const sketch = document.createElement("div");
    sketch.className = "sketch";
    sketch.textContent = plan.sketch;
    div.appendChild(sketch);
    if (plan.reason) {
      const reason = document.createElement("div");
      reason.className = "reason";
      reason.textContent = plan.reason;
      div.appendChild(reason);
    }
    const ul = document.createElement("ul");
    ul.className = "lemmas";
    plan.lemmas.forEach((l) => {
      const li = document.createElement("li");
      li.className = "lemma " + l.status;
      li.innerHTML = '<span class="name"></span> <span class="stmt"></span><div class="fb"></div>';
      li.querySelector(".name").textContent = l.name;
      li.querySelector(".stmt").textContent = l.statement;
      li.querySelector(".fb").textContent = l.feedback;
      li.onclick = (event) => openLemmaMenu(event, l, plan);
      ul.appendChild(li);
    });
    div.appendChild(ul);
    tree.appendChild(div);
  });
}

function openLemmaMenu(event, lemma, plan) {
  event.stopPropagation();
  const menu = $("lemma-menu");
  menu.innerHTML = "";
  const choices = [
    ["Give a hint about this lemma", () => {
      $("chat-input").value = `For ${lemma.name} (${lemma.statement}): `;
      $("chat-input").focus();
    }],
    ["Reject: false or useless", () => send("reject", { name: lemma.name }, `reject ${lemma.name}`)],
    ["Pin this plan's approach", () => send("pin", { text: plan.sketch }, `pin: ${plan.sketch}`)],
  ];
  choices.forEach(([label, onClick]) => {
    const b = document.createElement("button");
    b.textContent = label;
    b.onclick = () => { menu.classList.add("hidden"); onClick(); };
    menu.appendChild(b);
  });
  menu.style.left = `${event.pageX}px`;
  menu.style.top = `${event.pageY}px`;
  menu.classList.remove("hidden");
}

async function send(name, body, shown) {
  try {
    await action(name, body);
    addMessage(shown, "you");
  } catch (e) {
    addMessage("Error: " + e.message);
  }
}

async function sendChat() {
  const text = $("chat-input").value.trim();
  if (!text) return;
  $("chat-input").value = "";
  if (state.question) { // an open question: this is the answer
    state.question = null;
    $("question").classList.add("hidden");
    $("chat-input").placeholder = "Type a hint, e.g. try induction on n";
    await send("answer", { text }, `answer: ${text}`);
  } else {
    await send("hint", { text }, `hint: ${text}`);
  }
}

async function suggestLemma() {
  const name = $("lemma-name").value.trim();
  const statement = $("lemma-statement").value.trim();
  if (!name || !statement) return;
  await send("lemma", { name, statement }, `lemma ${name} : ${statement}`);
  $("lemma-name").value = "";
  $("lemma-statement").value = "";
}

async function togglePause() {
  state.paused = !state.paused;
  await send(state.paused ? "pause" : "resume", {}, state.paused ? "pause" : "resume");
  $("pause-btn").textContent = state.paused ? "Resume" : "Pause";
}

// ---------- step 4: the result ----------

function showResult(e) {
  setStatus(e.status);
  const r = e.result || {};
  $("result-title").textContent = r.proved ? "Proved, and verified by Lean" : "Not proved";
  const seconds = Math.round((Date.now() - state.startedAt) / 1000);
  const lemmas = state.current && r.proved ? state.current.lemmas.length : 0;
  const metrics = [
    [`${seconds} s`, "time"],
    [String(r.rounds ?? 0), "planning rounds"],
    [String(state.plans.length), "plans considered"],
    [String(lemmas), "lemmas in the proof"],
    [`${state.attemptsOk}/${state.attempts}`, "proof attempts that worked"],
  ];
  $("metrics").innerHTML = "";
  metrics.forEach(([v, k]) => {
    const div = document.createElement("div");
    div.className = "metric";
    div.innerHTML = '<div class="v"></div><div class="k"></div>';
    div.querySelector(".v").textContent = v;
    div.querySelector(".k").textContent = k;
    $("metrics").appendChild(div);
  });
  if (r.explanation) renderMarkdown($("explanation"), r.explanation);
  else $("explanation").textContent = r.message || "";
  $("lean-code").textContent = r.lean_code || "(no proof)";
  $("saved").textContent = r.folder ? `Saved in ${r.folder}` : "";
  $("step-result").classList.remove("hidden");
  $("step-result").scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth" });
}

// ---------- wire up the buttons ----------

window.addEventListener("DOMContentLoaded", () => {
  $("latex").addEventListener("input", updatePreview);
  $("formalize-btn").onclick = formalize;
  $("check-btn").onclick = checkEdit;
  $("approve-btn").onclick = approve;
  $("send-btn").onclick = sendChat;
  $("chat-input").addEventListener("keydown", (e) => { if (e.key === "Enter") sendChat(); });
  $("lemma-btn").onclick = suggestLemma;
  $("pause-btn").onclick = togglePause;
  $("stop-btn").onclick = () => send("stop", {}, "stop");
  document.addEventListener("click", () => $("lemma-menu").classList.add("hidden"));
  updatePreview();
  // Open http://localhost:8765/#demo to see the page with recorded events, no search needed.
  if (location.hash === "#demo") runDemo();
});

// ---------- demo: replay a recorded search (used to check the page without Lean or the model) ----------

async function runDemo() {
  const events = await (await fetch("demo_events.json")).json();
  Object.assign(state, { sessionId: "demo", startedAt: Date.now() - 95000 });
  $("step-search").classList.remove("hidden");
  setStatus("running");
  events.forEach(handleEvent);
}

    post() sends JSON to the server with fetch. In JavaScript, async/await work just like in Python: await fetch(...) waits for the reply without freezing the page. If the server answers with an error code, throw raises an exception, the JavaScript version of Python's raise.

    renderMarkdown solves a real clash: Markdown treats _ and \ specially, which would corrupt LaTeX like $a_1$. So it swaps each maths piece for a placeholder (@@MATH0@@), lets marked convert the Markdown, puts the maths back, and only then lets KaTeX draw it.

    Safety: text from the model or from you is inserted with textContent (always shown as plain text) or escaped with escapeHtml before it goes into innerHTML. That way a stray <b> in a lemma, or in a<b inside some maths, can't turn into real HTML.

    startWatching opens the WebSocket: new WebSocket("ws://.../ws/" + id), with ws.onmessage called for every event. handleEvent is one big switch on the event kind that updates state (the plans, the current plan, the open question, counters), and then renderTree redraws the tree from state. Keeping all data in one state object and redrawing from it is simpler than editing the page piece by piece. Events with depth > 0 come from a sub-search and go into the chat log, not the main tree.

    The chat box sends whatever you type as a hint, unless the search has asked a question, in which case it's sent as the answer. The search can't wait for both at once, so the page keeps track of which one is wanted.

    runDemo replays recorded events from demo_events.json when you open the page with #demo at the end of the address. It's how you can work on the page's look without waiting for Lean or the model.

  4. Serve the page from the server. Replace the whole file:

    server/app.py
    """The web server: the search engine, reachable from a browser.

  POST /api/formalize            {latex}             → Lean statement + back-translation + judge
  POST /api/check                {statement}         → Lean errors for a statement you edited ([] = fine)
  POST /api/sessions             {statement, ...}    → starts a search in the background, returns its id
  GET  /api/sessions/{id}                            → status, open question, result
  POST /api/sessions/{id}/{action}  {text|name|statement}
        action = hint | lemma | reject | pin | pause | resume | stop | answer | edit
  WS   /ws/{id}                                      → every event of that search, live
  GET  /                                             → the web page (the files in web/)

Each search runs in its own thread (it's slow, blocking work: Lean and the model).
The web server itself is asynchronous: one thread juggles many connections.
The Run class is the bridge between the two worlds.
"""

import asyncio
import threading
import uuid
from dataclasses import asdict
from pathlib import Path

from fastapi import FastAPI, HTTPException, WebSocket, WebSocketDisconnect
from fastapi.staticfiles import StaticFiles
from pydantic import BaseModel

import mathfinder.explain as explainer
import mathfinder.formalize as formalizer
import mathfinder.search as engine
from mathfinder import cli
from mathfinder.collab import Session
from mathfinder.lean import statement_errors
from mathfinder.llm import LLMError
from mathfinder.traces import TraceLogger

app = FastAPI(title="Math Finder")
WEB_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "web"


# ---------- request bodies (pydantic checks their shape for us) ----------


class FormalizeRequest(BaseModel):
    latex: str


class CheckRequest(BaseModel):
    statement: str


class StartRequest(BaseModel):
    statement: str  # the approved Lean statement
    latex: str = ""  # the original LaTeX, used for the explanation
    hints: list[str] = []
    rounds: int = 3


class ActionRequest(BaseModel):
    text: str = ""  # hint / pin / answer text
    name: str = ""  # lemma name (lemma, reject)
    statement: str = ""  # lemma statement (lemma) or new theorem statement (edit)


# ---------- one running search ----------


class Run:
    """A search running in a background thread, plus everyone watching it."""

    def __init__(self, statement: str, latex: str, hints: list[str], rounds: int):
        self.id = uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]
        self.statement, self.latex, self.hints, self.rounds = statement, latex, hints, rounds
        self.session = Session(statement)
        for h in hints:
            self.session.add_hint(h)
        self.status = "running"  # running | proved | not_proved | stopped
        self.result = None
        self.events = []  # full history, so a page that connects late can catch up
        self.listeners = []  # (event loop, asyncio.Queue) for each open WebSocket
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.folder = cli.new_run_dir()
        self.logger = TraceLogger(self.folder, latex or statement)

    def on_event(self, event: dict) -> None:
        """Called from the SEARCH thread for every event."""
        self.logger(event)
        with self.lock:
            self.events.append(event)
            listeners = list(self.listeners)
        for loop, q in listeners:
            # asyncio queues aren't thread-safe: ask each WebSocket's event loop to do the put itself
            loop.call_soon_threadsafe(q.put_nowait, event)

    def subscribe(self, loop) -> tuple[list[dict], asyncio.Queue]:
        q = asyncio.Queue()
        with self.lock:  # history + registration together, so no event is missed or sent twice
            self.listeners.append((loop, q))
            return list(self.events), q

    def unsubscribe(self, q: asyncio.Queue) -> None:
        with self.lock:
            self.listeners = [(l, x) for l, x in self.listeners if x is not q]

    def start(self) -> None:
        threading.Thread(target=self._work, daemon=True).start()

    def _work(self) -> None:
        try:
            result = engine.search(self.statement, rounds=self.rounds, on_event=self.on_event, session=self.session)
        except Exception as e:  # never let a crash leave the page waiting forever
            self.status, self.result = "not_proved", {"proved": False, "message": f"internal error: {e}"}
            self.on_event({"kind": "finished", "status": self.status, "result": self.result})
            return
        self.logger.finish(result.proved, self.session.hints())
        explanation = ""
        if result.proved:
            self.on_event({"kind": "explaining"})
            try:
                explanation = explainer.explain(self.latex or self.statement, result.plan, result.lean_code)
            except LLMError as e:
                explanation = f"(could not write an explanation: {e})"
            cli.save_run(self.folder, self.latex or self.statement, result.lean_code, explanation)
        stop = self.session.checkpoint()
        self.status = "proved" if result.proved else ("stopped" if stop else "not_proved")
        self.result = {
            "proved": result.proved,
            "message": result.message,
            "lean_code": result.lean_code,
            "explanation": explanation,
            "plan": result.plan.sketch if result.plan else "",
            "rounds": result.rounds,
            "folder": str(self.folder),
        }
        self.on_event({"kind": "finished", "status": self.status, "result": self.result})


RUNS: dict[str, Run] = {}


def get_run(run_id: str) -> Run:
    if run_id not in RUNS:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail="no such session")
    return RUNS[run_id]


# ---------- endpoints ----------


@app.post("/api/formalize")
def formalize(req: FormalizeRequest):
    # A plain `def` (not `async def`): FastAPI runs it in a worker thread, so this slow call
    # (model + Lean) doesn't freeze the server for everyone else.
    f = formalizer.formalize(req.latex)
    return {**asdict(f), "ok": f.ok}


@app.post("/api/check")
def check(req: CheckRequest):
    return {"errors": statement_errors(req.statement)}


@app.post("/api/sessions")
def start_session(req: StartRequest):
    run = Run(req.statement, req.latex, req.hints, req.rounds)
    RUNS[run.id] = run
    run.start()
    return {"id": run.id}


@app.get("/api/sessions/{run_id}")
def session_status(run_id: str):
    run = get_run(run_id)
    return {
        "id": run.id,
        "statement": run.statement,
        "status": run.status,
        "question": run.session.question,
        "hints": run.session.hints(),
        "events": len(run.events),
        "result": run.result,
    }


@app.post("/api/sessions/{run_id}/{action}")
def session_action(run_id: str, action: str, req: ActionRequest):
    run = get_run(run_id)
    s = run.session
    if action == "hint":
        s.add_hint(req.text)
    elif action == "lemma":
        s.add_lemma(req.name, req.statement, req.text)
    elif action == "reject":
        s.reject_lemma(req.name or req.statement)
    elif action == "pin":
        s.pin(req.text)
    elif action == "pause":
        s.pause()
    elif action == "resume":
        s.resume()
    elif action == "stop":
        s.stop()
    elif action == "answer":
        s.answer(req.text)
    elif action == "edit":  # stop this search and start a fresh one on the new statement
        errors = statement_errors(req.statement)
        if errors:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=f"Lean rejected the statement: {errors[0][:300]}")
        s.edit_statement(req.statement)
        new = Run(req.statement, "", s.hints(), run.rounds)
        RUNS[new.id] = new
        new.start()
        return {"ok": True, "new_id": new.id}
    else:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=f"unknown action {action!r}")
    return {"ok": True}


@app.websocket("/ws/{run_id}")
async def watch(websocket: WebSocket, run_id: str):
    await websocket.accept()
    if run_id not in RUNS:
        await websocket.close(code=4404)
        return
    run = RUNS[run_id]
    history, q = run.subscribe(asyncio.get_running_loop())
    try:
        for event in history:
            await websocket.send_json(event)
            if event["kind"] == "finished":
                await websocket.close()  # nothing more will happen
                return
        while True:
            event = await q.get()  # `await` = pause here until an event arrives, without blocking the server
            await websocket.send_json(event)
            if event["kind"] == "finished":
                await websocket.close()
                return
    except WebSocketDisconnect:
        pass  # the browser tab closed; that's fine
    finally:
        run.unsubscribe(q)


# Everything that isn't an /api or /ws address is a file from web/ ("/" gives index.html).
# This must come LAST: a mount at "/" would otherwise catch the API addresses too.
app.mount("/", StaticFiles(directory=WEB_DIR, html=True), name="web")

    What changed: two imports (Path, StaticFiles), WEB_DIR, and the last line, app.mount("/", StaticFiles(directory=WEB_DIR, html=True)). That serves every file in web/, and html=True makes / give index.html. It must come after all the routes, because FastAPI tries routes in order and a mount at / matches everything.
