  10. Send the metric and show the optimizer's work. Replace the whole file:

    web/app.js
    // Math Finder web page. Talks to server/app.py: REST for actions, a WebSocket for live events.

const $ = (id) => document.getElementById(id);

const state = {
  latex: "",
  sessionId: null,
  plans: [],          // [{sketch, status, reason, lemmas: [{name, statement, status, feedback}]}]
  current: null,      // the plan being worked on right now
  question: null,     // the open question from the search, if any
  paused: false,
  startedAt: 0,
  attempts: 0,
  attemptsOk: 0,
};

// ---------- talking to the server ----------

async function post(path, body = {}) {
  const response = await fetch(path, {
    method: "POST",
    headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },
    body: JSON.stringify(body),
  });
  const data = await response.json();
  if (!response.ok) throw new Error(data.detail || response.statusText);
  return data;
}

function action(name, body = {}) {
  return post(`/api/sessions/${state.sessionId}/${name}`, body);
}

// ---------- math and markdown ----------

function renderMath(el) {
  if (window.renderMathInElement) {
    renderMathInElement(el, {
      delimiters: [
        { left: "$$", right: "$$", display: true },
        { left: "$", right: "$", display: false },
      ],
      throwOnError: false,
    });
  }
}

function escapeHtml(text) {
  return text.replace(/&/g, "&amp;").replace(/</g, "&lt;").replace(/>/g, "&gt;");
}

// Markdown would mangle LaTeX (it treats _ and \ specially), so we hide the math first,
// let marked do the Markdown, put the math back, and let KaTeX draw it.
function renderMarkdown(el, text) {
  const math = [];
  const hidden = text.replace(/\$\$[\s\S]+?\$\$|\$[^$\n]+?\$/g, (m) => {
    math.push(m);
    return `@@MATH${math.length - 1}@@`;
  });
  let html = window.marked ? marked.parse(escapeHtml(hidden)) : `<pre>${escapeHtml(hidden)}</pre>`;
  html = html.replace(/@@MATH(\d+)@@/g, (_, i) => escapeHtml(math[Number(i)]));
  el.innerHTML = html;
  renderMath(el);
}

function showText(el, text) {
  el.textContent = text;
  renderMath(el);
}

// ---------- step 1: LaTeX in, live preview ----------

function updatePreview() {
  showText($("preview"), $("latex").value);
}

async function formalize() {
  state.latex = $("latex").value.trim();
  $("formalize-btn").disabled = true;
  $("formalize-status").textContent = "Translating to Lean and checking it (about 30 s)…";
  try {
    const f = await post("/api/formalize", { latex: state.latex });
    if (!f.ok) {
      $("formalize-status").textContent = "Lean couldn't accept any translation: " + (f.errors[0] || "");
      return;
    }
    $("formalize-status").textContent = "";
    $("statement").value = f.statement;
    showText($("back"), f.back_translation);
    const v = $("verdict");
    v.className = "verdict " + (f.faithful ? "ok" : "warn");
    v.textContent = (f.faithful ? "✓ Same meaning: " : "⚠ The meaning may differ: ") + f.judgment;
    $("check-errors").classList.add("hidden");
    $("step-approve").classList.remove("hidden");
    $("step-approve").scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth" });
  } catch (e) {
    $("formalize-status").textContent = "Error: " + e.message;
  } finally {
    $("formalize-btn").disabled = false;
  }
}

// ---------- step 2: approve ----------

async function checkEdit() {
  const box = $("check-errors");
  const { errors } = await post("/api/check", { statement: $("statement").value });
  box.classList.toggle("hidden", errors.length === 0);
  box.textContent = errors.length ? "Lean rejected it:\n" + errors[0] : "";
  if (!errors.length) {
    box.classList.remove("hidden");
    box.textContent = "✓ Lean accepts this statement.";
  }
  return errors.length === 0;
}

async function approve() {
  $("approve-btn").disabled = true;
  const hints = $("start-hints").value.split("\n").map((h) => h.trim()).filter(Boolean);
  const metric = $("metric").value;
  const { id } = await post("/api/sessions", { statement: $("statement").value, latex: state.latex, hints, metric });
  startWatching(id);
  hints.forEach((h) => addMessage(`hint: ${h}`, "you"));
}

// ---------- step 3: the live search ----------

function startWatching(id) {
  Object.assign(state, { sessionId: id, plans: [], current: null, question: null, startedAt: Date.now(),
                         attempts: 0, attemptsOk: 0, paused: false });
  $("log").innerHTML = "";
  $("step-result").classList.add("hidden");
  $("step-search").classList.remove("hidden");
  setStatus("running");
  renderTree();
  $("step-search").scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth" });

  const protocol = location.protocol === "https:" ? "wss" : "ws";
  const ws = new WebSocket(`${protocol}://${location.host}/ws/${id}`);
  ws.onmessage = (message) => handleEvent(JSON.parse(message.data));
  ws.onclose = () => addMessage("(connection closed)", "sys");
}

function setStatus(status) {
  const badge = $("search-status");
  badge.textContent = status.replace("_", " ");
  badge.className = "badge " + status;
}

function addMessage(text, who = "sys") {
  const div = document.createElement("div");
  div.className = "msg " + who;
  div.textContent = text;
  $("log").appendChild(div);
  $("log").scrollTop = $("log").scrollHeight;
}

function findLemma(name) {
  return state.current && state.current.lemmas.find((l) => l.name === name);
}

function handleEvent(e) {
  const deep = (e.depth || 0) > 0; // events from a sub-search on a stuck lemma
  switch (e.kind) {
    case "start":
      if (!deep) addMessage("Search started.");
      break;
    case "refuted":
      addMessage(`The statement is FALSE. Counterexample: ${e.counterexample}`);
      break;
    case "round":
      if (!deep) addMessage(`Round ${e.number}: asking the planner…`);
      break;
    case "plan_rejected":
      if (!deep) state.plans.push({ sketch: e.sketch, status: "rejected", reason: e.reason, lemmas: [] });
      break;
    case "plan_start":
      if (deep) { addMessage(`  sub-plan: ${e.sketch}`); break; }
      state.current = { sketch: e.sketch, status: "active", reason: "",
                        lemmas: e.lemmas.map((name) => ({ name, statement: "", status: "open", feedback: "" })) };
      state.plans.push(state.current);
      break;
    case "lemma_start": {
      if (deep) break;
      const l = findLemma(e.name);
      if (l) Object.assign(l, { statement: e.statement, status: "working" });
      break;
    }
    case "subsearch_start":
      addMessage(`${e.name} is stuck: starting a smaller search on it.`);
      break;
    case "lemma_proved":
    case "lemma_refuted":
    case "lemma_stuck": {
      const status = e.kind.replace("lemma_", "");
      if (deep) { addMessage(`  sub-lemma ${e.name}: ${status}`); break; }
      let l = findLemma(e.name);
      if (!l && state.current) { // a lemma spliced in from a sub-search
        l = { name: e.name, statement: e.statement, status, feedback: "" };
        state.current.lemmas.push(l);
      }
      if (l) Object.assign(l, { statement: e.statement, status, feedback: e.feedback });
      break;
    }
    case "attempt":
      state.attempts += 1;
      if (e.ok) state.attemptsOk += 1;
      break;
    case "plan_failed":
      if (!deep && state.current) state.current.status = "failed";
      break;
    case "done":
      if (deep) break;
      if (state.current) state.current.status = "proved";
      else { // proved directly by automation: there was no plan, so show one node for it
        state.current = { sketch: e.plan, status: "proved", reason: "", lemmas: [] };
        state.plans.push(state.current);
      }
      addMessage("✓ Lean verified a proof.");
      break;
    case "ask_user":
      state.question = e.question;
      $("question").textContent = "❓ " + e.question;
      $("question").classList.remove("hidden");
      $("chat-input").placeholder = "Type your answer…";
      $("chat-input").focus();
      break;
    case "user_answer":
      addMessage("Trying again with your idea.");
      break;
    case "no_answer":
      addMessage("(no answer)");
      break;
    case "stopped":
      addMessage(`Stopped: ${e.reason}`);
      break;
    case "gave_up":
      addMessage("No verified proof found.");
      break;
    case "optimize_start":
      addMessage(`Looking for a better proof (${e.metric}, current cost ${e.cost})…`);
      break;
    case "optimize_candidate":
      state.attempts += 1;
      if (e.ok) state.attemptsOk += 1;
      break;
    case "optimized":
      addMessage(e.improved ? `Found a better proof: cost ${e.before.cost} → ${e.after.cost}.`
                            : "Kept the first proof (no better one found).");
      break;
    case "explaining":
      addMessage("Writing the explanation…");
      break;
    case "finished":
      showResult(e);
      break;
  }
  renderTree();
}

function renderTree() {
  const tree = $("tree");
  tree.innerHTML = "";
  if (!state.plans.length) {
    tree.innerHTML = '<div class="muted">Waiting for the first plans…</div>';
    return;
  }
  state.plans.forEach((plan) => {
    const div = document.createElement("div");
    div.className = "plan " + plan.status;
    const sketch = document.createElement("div");
    sketch.className = "sketch";
    sketch.textContent = plan.sketch;
    div.appendChild(sketch);
    if (plan.reason) {
      const reason = document.createElement("div");
      reason.className = "reason";
      reason.textContent = plan.reason;
      div.appendChild(reason);
    }
    const ul = document.createElement("ul");
    ul.className = "lemmas";
    plan.lemmas.forEach((l) => {
      const li = document.createElement("li");
      li.className = "lemma " + l.status;
      li.innerHTML = '<span class="name"></span> <span class="stmt"></span><div class="fb"></div>';
      li.querySelector(".name").textContent = l.name;
      li.querySelector(".stmt").textContent = l.statement;
      li.querySelector(".fb").textContent = l.feedback;
      li.onclick = (event) => openLemmaMenu(event, l, plan);
      ul.appendChild(li);
    });
    div.appendChild(ul);
    tree.appendChild(div);
  });
}

function openLemmaMenu(event, lemma, plan) {
  event.stopPropagation();
  const menu = $("lemma-menu");
  menu.innerHTML = "";
  const choices = [
    ["Give a hint about this lemma", () => {
      $("chat-input").value = `For ${lemma.name} (${lemma.statement}): `;
      $("chat-input").focus();
    }],
    ["Reject: false or useless", () => send("reject", { name: lemma.name }, `reject ${lemma.name}`)],
    ["Pin this plan's approach", () => send("pin", { text: plan.sketch }, `pin: ${plan.sketch}`)],
  ];
  choices.forEach(([label, onClick]) => {
    const b = document.createElement("button");
    b.textContent = label;
    b.onclick = () => { menu.classList.add("hidden"); onClick(); };
    menu.appendChild(b);
  });
  menu.style.left = `${event.pageX}px`;
  menu.style.top = `${event.pageY}px`;
  menu.classList.remove("hidden");
}

async function send(name, body, shown) {
  try {
    await action(name, body);
    addMessage(shown, "you");
  } catch (e) {
    addMessage("Error: " + e.message);
  }
}

async function sendChat() {
  const text = $("chat-input").value.trim();
  if (!text) return;
  $("chat-input").value = "";
  if (state.question) { // an open question: this is the answer
    state.question = null;
    $("question").classList.add("hidden");
    $("chat-input").placeholder = "Type a hint, e.g. try induction on n";
    await send("answer", { text }, `answer: ${text}`);
  } else {
    await send("hint", { text }, `hint: ${text}`);
  }
}

async function suggestLemma() {
  const name = $("lemma-name").value.trim();
  const statement = $("lemma-statement").value.trim();
  if (!name || !statement) return;
  await send("lemma", { name, statement }, `lemma ${name} : ${statement}`);
  $("lemma-name").value = "";
  $("lemma-statement").value = "";
}

async function togglePause() {
  state.paused = !state.paused;
  await send(state.paused ? "pause" : "resume", {}, state.paused ? "pause" : "resume");
  $("pause-btn").textContent = state.paused ? "Resume" : "Pause";
}

// ---------- step 4: the result ----------

function showResult(e) {
  setStatus(e.status);
  const r = e.result || {};
  $("result-title").textContent = r.proved ? "Proved, and verified by Lean" : "Not proved";
  const seconds = Math.round((Date.now() - state.startedAt) / 1000);
  const lemmas = state.current && r.proved ? state.current.lemmas.length : 0;
  const metrics = [
    [`${seconds} s`, "time"],
    [String(r.rounds ?? 0), "planning rounds"],
    [String(state.plans.length), "plans considered"],
    [String(lemmas), "lemmas in the proof"],
    [`${state.attemptsOk}/${state.attempts}`, "proof attempts that worked"],
  ];
  if (r.optimization) {
    const o = r.optimization;
    metrics.push([`${o.before.cost} → ${o.after.cost}`, `proof cost (${o.metric})`]);
  }
  $("metrics").innerHTML = "";
  metrics.forEach(([v, k]) => {
    const div = document.createElement("div");
    div.className = "metric";
    div.innerHTML = '<div class="v"></div><div class="k"></div>';
    div.querySelector(".v").textContent = v;
    div.querySelector(".k").textContent = k;
    $("metrics").appendChild(div);
  });
  if (r.explanation) renderMarkdown($("explanation"), r.explanation);
  else $("explanation").textContent = r.message || "";
  $("lean-code").textContent = r.lean_code || "(no proof)";
  $("saved").textContent = r.folder ? `Saved in ${r.folder}` : "";
  $("step-result").classList.remove("hidden");
  $("step-result").scrollIntoView({ behavior: "smooth" });
}

// ---------- wire up the buttons ----------

window.addEventListener("DOMContentLoaded", () => {
  $("latex").addEventListener("input", updatePreview);
  $("formalize-btn").onclick = formalize;
  $("check-btn").onclick = checkEdit;
  $("approve-btn").onclick = approve;
  $("send-btn").onclick = sendChat;
  $("chat-input").addEventListener("keydown", (e) => { if (e.key === "Enter") sendChat(); });
  $("lemma-btn").onclick = suggestLemma;
  $("pause-btn").onclick = togglePause;
  $("stop-btn").onclick = () => send("stop", {}, "stop");
  document.addEventListener("click", () => $("lemma-menu").classList.add("hidden"));
  updatePreview();
  // Open http://localhost:8765/#demo to see the page with recorded events, no search needed.
  if (location.hash === "#demo") runDemo();
});

// ---------- demo: replay a recorded search (used to check the page without Lean or the model) ----------

async function runDemo() {
  const events = await (await fetch("demo_events.json")).json();
  Object.assign(state, { sessionId: "demo", startedAt: Date.now() - 95000 });
  $("step-search").classList.remove("hidden");
  setStatus("running");
  events.forEach(handleEvent);
}

    What changed: approve sends metric, handleEvent handles optimize_start, optimize_candidate (counted as proof attempts) and optimized, and showResult adds a "proof cost before → after" tile.

  11. Run the tests:

    uv run pytest -q -m "not lean and not llm"
uv run pytest -q tests/test_optimize.py

  12. Optimize a proof you already have. This uses the one saved in M8 (use your own folder name):

    uv run python - <<'EOF'
from pathlib import Path
from mathfinder.lean import HEADER
from mathfinder.optimize import optimize
from mathfinder.planner import Plan
code = Path("runs/20261114-152642/proof.lean").read_text().removeprefix(HEADER)
T = "(x : ℝ) (hx : 0 < x) : x + 1 / x ≥ 2"
for metric in ("short", "readable"):
    r = optimize(T, code, Plan("original", [], ""), metric)
    print(f"\n== metric={metric} improved={r.improved} cost {r.original['cost']} -> {r.best['cost']}")
    print(r.lean_code)
EOF

Checkpoint

We started from the two-lemma proof of x + 1/x ≥ 2 (combine_frac + quad_ineq, 4 steps, 1 nlinarith). About 1½ minutes later:

== metric=short improved=True cost 6 -> 2
theorem main (x : ℝ) (hx : 0 < x) : x + 1 / x ≥ 2 := by
  have h : x * (1 / x) = 1 := by field_simp
  nlinarith [sq_nonneg (x - 1), h]

== metric=readable improved=True cost 11 -> 4
theorem main (x : ℝ) (hx : 0 < x) : x + 1 / x ≥ 2 := by
  have key : x + 1 / x - 2 = (x - 1) ^ 2 / x := by
    field_simp <;> ring
  rw [ge_iff_le, ← sub_nonneg, key]
  exact div_nonneg (sq_nonneg _) hx.le

Different metrics, different winners. short picked a compact nlinarith proof. readable picked the one that shows the real reason: x + 1/x − 2 = (x−1)²/x, and a square over a positive number is ≥ 0. It has no black-box automation.

On the website, with "readable" chosen, the chat says Looking for a better proof (readable, current cost 6)… and then either Found a better proof: cost a → b or Kept the first proof, and the result shows a proof cost tile.

Fast tests:

.....                                                                    [100%]
77 passed, 30 deselected in 1.15s

Common errors

  • improved=False every time: that's allowed. The first proof may already be the best the model can find. Try another metric.
  • Fast tests take many seconds: some CLI test reaches the real optimizer. Add --no-optimize to it.
  • argument --metric: invalid choice: the names are short, readable, few_lemmas.

M12: The benchmark

What and why. A handful of hand-picked examples can make any system look good. A benchmark is a fixed set of problems you run the same way every time, so you can measure how good the system really is, and whether a change made it better or worse. Research provers are compared on miniF2F, a set of competition problems (AMC, AIME, IMO and similar) already written as Lean statements. Ours is a small set in that style: 20 problems across algebra, number theory, induction and inequalities, from "solve 3x + 2 = 11" to "if two primes sum to 9, their product is 14". Every benchmark run is also traced, so it produces training data for M13 too.

  1. Create the problem set, one JSON object per line.

    bench/problems.jsonl
    {"name": "linear_eq", "topic": "algebra", "statement": "(x : ℝ) (h : 3 * x + 2 = 11) : x = 3"}
{"name": "sum_diff_product", "topic": "algebra", "statement": "(a b : ℝ) (h₀ : a + b = 7) (h₁ : a - b = 1) : a * b = 12"}
{"name": "rational_eq", "topic": "algebra", "statement": "(x : ℝ) (h₀ : x + 1 ≠ 0) (h₁ : (x - 1) / (x + 1) = 3) : x = -2"}
{"name": "function_compose", "topic": "algebra", "statement": "(f : ℝ → ℝ) (h : ∀ x, f x = 2 * x + 3) : f (f 1) = 13"}
{"name": "log_square", "topic": "algebra", "statement": "(x : ℝ) (hx : 0 < x) : Real.log (x ^ 2) = 2 * Real.log x"}
{"name": "mod_double", "topic": "number theory", "statement": "(n : ℕ) (h : n % 5 = 3) : (2 * n) % 5 = 1"}
{"name": "gcd_180_168", "topic": "number theory", "statement": ": Nat.gcd 180 168 = 12"}
{"name": "crt_small", "topic": "number theory", "statement": "(n : ℕ) (h₀ : n % 3 = 2) (h₁ : n % 4 = 3) (h₂ : n < 20) : n = 11"}
{"name": "squares_mod_4", "topic": "number theory", "statement": "(a : ℤ) : a ^ 2 % 4 = 0 ∨ a ^ 2 % 4 = 1"}
{"name": "three_divides", "topic": "number theory", "statement": "(n : ℕ) : 3 ∣ n ^ 3 + 2 * n"}
{"name": "six_divides_consecutive", "topic": "number theory", "statement": "(n : ℕ) : 6 ∣ n * (n + 1) * (n + 2)"}
{"name": "seven_divides_power", "topic": "number theory", "statement": "(n : ℕ) : 7 ∣ 2 ^ (3 * n) - 1"}
{"name": "primes_sum_nine", "topic": "number theory", "statement": "(a b : ℕ) (h₀ : Nat.Prime a) (h₁ : Nat.Prime b) (h₂ : a + b = 9) : a * b = 14"}
{"name": "sqrt3_irrational", "topic": "number theory", "statement": ": Irrational (Real.sqrt 3)"}
{"name": "gauss_sum_101", "topic": "induction", "statement": ": ∑ k ∈ Finset.range 101, k = 5050"}
{"name": "gauss_sum_formula", "topic": "induction", "statement": "(n : ℕ) : ∑ i ∈ Finset.range (n + 1), (i : ℚ) = n * (n + 1) / 2"}
{"name": "two_pow_ge", "topic": "induction", "statement": "(n : ℕ) : 2 ^ n ≥ n + 1"}
{"name": "amgm_three_pairs", "topic": "inequality", "statement": "(a b c : ℝ) (ha : 0 < a) (hb : 0 < b) (hc : 0 < c) : (a + b) * (b + c) * (c + a) ≥ 8 * a * b * c"}
{"name": "product_max", "topic": "inequality", "statement": "(x y : ℝ) (h : x + y = 10) : x * y ≤ 25"}
{"name": "quartic_nonneg", "topic": "inequality", "statement": "(x : ℝ) : x ^ 4 - 4 * x + 3 ≥ 0"}

    The statements are already formal, the way miniF2F gives them, so the benchmark measures the search, not the formalizer. Names like h₀ are just hypothesis labels (type h\0 or copy them). A few things to notice: (i : ℚ) in gauss_sum_formula turns each i into a rational number so / 2 is real division, and in seven_divides_power the ℕ subtraction 2 ^ (3 * n) - 1 is safe because 2³ⁿ ≥ 1.

  2. Create the benchmark runner.

    mathfinder/bench.py
    """The benchmark: how good is Math Finder, really?

bench/problems.jsonl holds competition-style problems (in the spirit of miniF2F),
already written as Lean statements, one JSON object per line:
  {"name": "...", "topic": "...", "statement": "..."}

`run_bench` runs the full search on each one and records what happened. Every run
is also traced (runs/bench-<time>/<name>/trace.jsonl), so the benchmark doubles as
a source of training data. Results go to bench/results/<time>.json and .md.
"""

import json
import time
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

from mathfinder import PROJECT_ROOT
from mathfinder.optimize import proof_stats
from mathfinder.search import search
from mathfinder.traces import TraceLogger, chain

PROBLEMS_FILE = PROJECT_ROOT / "bench" / "problems.jsonl"
RESULTS_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / "bench" / "results"
RUNS_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / "runs"


def load_problems(path: Path = PROBLEMS_FILE) -> list[dict]:
    return [json.loads(line) for line in path.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]


def how_it_was_proved(result) -> str:
    if not result.proved:
        return result.message
    if result.rounds == 0:
        return result.plan.sketch  # e.g. "Automation: nlinarith"
    return f"plan with {len(result.plan.lemmas)} lemma(s), round {result.rounds}"


def run_one(problem: dict, rounds: int, run_dir: Path) -> dict:
    """Search for a proof of one problem; return a results row."""
    events = []
    logger = TraceLogger(run_dir / problem["name"], problem["statement"])
    start = time.time()
    try:
        result = search(problem["statement"], rounds=rounds, on_event=chain(events.append, logger))
    except Exception as e:  # one broken problem must not stop the whole benchmark
        logger.finish(False, [])
        return {**problem, "proved": False, "seconds": round(time.time() - start), "how": f"crash: {e}",
                "attempts": 0, "steps": None}
    logger.finish(result.proved, [])
    attempts = [e for e in events if e["kind"] == "attempt"]
    return {
        **problem,
        "proved": result.proved,
        "seconds": round(time.time() - start),
        "how": how_it_was_proved(result),
        "attempts": len(attempts),
        "steps": proof_stats(result.lean_code)["steps"] if result.proved else None,
    }


def results_table(rows: list[dict]) -> str:
    """A Markdown table, plus a one-line summary and a per-topic count."""
    proved = sum(r["proved"] for r in rows)
    lines = [
        f"**{proved}/{len(rows)} proved** ({proved / len(rows):.0%}), "
        f"total time {sum(r['seconds'] for r in rows) // 60} min",
        "",
        "| # | Problem | Topic | Result | Time | How |",
        "|---|---------|-------|--------|------|-----|",
    ]
    for i, r in enumerate(rows, 1):
        mark = "✓ proved" if r["proved"] else "✗ not proved"
        lines.append(f"| {i} | `{r['name']}` | {r['topic']} | {mark} | {r['seconds']} s | {r['how'][:90]} |")
    topics = sorted({r["topic"] for r in rows})
    by_topic = ", ".join(f"{t} {sum(r['proved'] for r in rows if r['topic'] == t)}/"
                         f"{sum(r['topic'] == t for r in rows)}" for t in topics)
    lines += ["", f"By topic: {by_topic}"]
    return "\n".join(lines)


def run_bench(problems: list[dict], rounds: int = 2, on_progress=print) -> tuple[list[dict], Path]:
    """Run every problem, save the results, return (rows, path of the .md file)."""
    stamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d-%H%M%S")
    run_dir = RUNS_DIR / f"bench-{stamp}"
    rows = []
    for i, problem in enumerate(problems, 1):
        on_progress(f"[{i}/{len(problems)}] {problem['name']}: {problem['statement']}")
        row = run_one(problem, rounds, run_dir)
        rows.append(row)
        on_progress(f"    {'✓ proved' if row['proved'] else '✗ not proved'} in {row['seconds']} s  ({row['how'][:80]})")

    RESULTS_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    (RESULTS_DIR / f"{stamp}.json").write_text(json.dumps(rows, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))
    md_path = RESULTS_DIR / f"{stamp}.md"
    md_path.write_text(results_table(rows) + "\n")
    return rows, md_path

    run_one runs the ordinary search (no optimizer, no explanation: we measure finding proofs) and records whether it was proved, how long it took, how (direct automation or a plan with k lemmas), how many proof attempts were made, and the proof's length. try/except Exception makes sure one crashing problem can't stop the other nineteen.

    results_table builds a Markdown table: a summary line, one row per problem, and a per-topic count. {proved / len(rows):.0%} formats a fraction as a whole percentage. sorted({...}) makes a set of topics (no duplicates) and sorts it.

  3. Add the bench command. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/cli.py
    """Command line.

  uv run python -m mathfinder prove "For every integer $n$, $6 \\mid n^3 - n$."
  uv run python -m mathfinder prove --lean "(n : ℤ) : 6 ∣ n ^ 3 - n"

With LaTeX, you first see the Lean statement, its back-translation and the
judge's opinion, and nothing is searched until you approve.
Every run gets a folder runs/<date-time>/ with trace.jsonl (everything that happened);
a proved theorem also gets proof.lean + explanation.md there. After the first proof,
the optimizer looks for a better one (--metric short | readable | few_lemmas).
With --interactive you can type hints and commands while the search runs.

  uv run python -m mathfinder export      builds training data in data/ from all traces
  uv run python -m mathfinder bench       runs the benchmark in bench/problems.jsonl
"""

import argparse
import select
import sys
import threading
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

from mathfinder import PROJECT_ROOT
from mathfinder.bench import load_problems, results_table, run_bench
from mathfinder.collab import Session
from mathfinder.explain import explain
from mathfinder.formalize import formalize
from mathfinder.lean import HEADER, statement_errors
from mathfinder.llm import LLMError
from mathfinder.optimize import METRICS, optimize
from mathfinder.search import search
from mathfinder.traces import TraceLogger, chain, export

RUNS_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / "runs"
DATA_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / "data"

SYMBOL = {"lemma_proved": "✓", "lemma_refuted": "✗", "lemma_stuck": "…"}


def print_event(event: dict) -> None:
    kind = event["kind"]
    if event.get("depth", 0) > 0:  # events from a sub-search are indented under their lemma
        indent = "        " * event["depth"]
        if kind in SYMBOL:
            print(f"{indent}{SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
        elif kind == "plan_start":
            print(f"{indent}▶ sub-plan: {event['sketch']}")
        elif kind == "done":
            print(f"{indent}✓ sub-search proved it")
        sys.stdout.flush()
        return
    if kind == "start":
        print(f"\nTheorem: {event['theorem']}")
    elif kind == "subsearch_start":
        print(f"      ↳ {event['name']} is stuck: starting a sub-search on it")
    elif kind == "refuted":
        print(f"✗ The statement is FALSE. Counterexample: {event['counterexample']}")
    elif kind == "round":
        print(f"\n— round {event['number']}: asking the planner —")
    elif kind == "plan_rejected":
        print(f"  ✗ plan doesn't fit together: {event['sketch']}\n      {event['reason'][:160]}")
    elif kind == "plan_start":
        print(f"  ▶ plan: {event['sketch']}  (lemmas: {', '.join(event['lemmas']) or 'none'})")
    elif kind in SYMBOL:
        print(f"      {SYMBOL[kind]} {event['name']} : {event['statement']}   [{event['feedback'][:80]}]")
    elif kind == "plan_failed":
        print(f"    plan failed (score {event['score']:.2f})")
    elif kind == "done":
        print(f"\n✓ VERIFIED by Lean. Plan: {event['plan']}\n")
        print(event["lean_code"])
    elif kind == "gave_up":
        print("\nNo verified proof found.")
    elif kind == "ask_user":
        print(f"\n❓ {event['question']}\n   (type your answer and press Enter)")
    elif kind == "user_answer":
        print(f"  ← thanks, trying again with your idea: {event['text']}")
    elif kind == "no_answer":
        print("  (no answer)")
    elif kind == "stopped":
        print(f"\n■ Stopped: {event['reason']}")
    sys.stdout.flush()  # show progress immediately, even when output is piped to a file


def approve(latex: str, auto_yes: bool = False, ask=input) -> str | None:
    """Formalize `latex`, show it, and return the statement the user approved (None = no)."""
    print("Formalizing (LaTeX → Lean)...")
    f = formalize(latex)
    if not f.ok:
        print("Could not produce a statement Lean accepts:\n  " + f.errors[0][:400])
        return None
    print(f"\nFormal statement:  theorem main {f.statement}")
    print(f"Back-translation:  {f.back_translation}")
    print(f"Judge:             {'same meaning' if f.faithful else 'MEANING MAY DIFFER'}: {f.judgment}")

    statement = f.statement
    if auto_yes and f.faithful:
        return statement
    if auto_yes:
        print("(--yes ignored: the judge thinks the meaning may differ, so please look.)")
    while True:
        try:
            answer = ask("\nProve this statement? [y]es / [n]o / [e]dit: ").strip().lower()
        except EOFError:  # no keyboard attached (e.g. running in a script)
            return None
        if answer == "y":
            return statement
        if answer == "n":
            return None
        if answer == "e":
            new = ask("Lean statement (what comes after `theorem main`): ").strip()
            errors = statement_errors(new)
            if errors:
                print("Lean rejected it: " + errors[0][:300])
            else:
                statement = new
                print(f"Now:  theorem main {statement}")


COMMANDS = """While it runs you can type:
  hint <text>                     e.g.  hint try induction on n
  lemma <name> <lean statement>   e.g.  lemma key (a b : ℝ) : 0 ≤ (a - b) ^ 2
  reject <lemma name>             plans using that lemma are dropped
  pin <approach>                  "keep going in this direction"
  pause | resume | stop
When the search asks you a question, just type your answer."""


def handle_command(session: Session, line: str) -> str:
    """Apply one typed line to the session. Returns a short confirmation to print."""
    line = line.strip()
    word, _, rest = line.partition(" ")  # "hint try x" -> ("hint", " ", "try x")
    if session.question is not None and word not in ("pause", "resume", "stop"):
        session.answer(line)  # a question is open: the whole line is the answer
        return "answer sent"
    if word == "hint" and rest:
        session.add_hint(rest)
        return f"hint added: {rest}"
    if word == "lemma" and " " in rest:
        name, statement = rest.split(" ", 1)
        session.add_lemma(name, statement)
        return f"lemma suggested: {name}"
    if word == "reject" and rest:
        session.reject_lemma(rest)
        return f"rejected: {rest}"
    if word == "pin" and rest:
        session.pin(rest)
        return f"pinned: {rest}"
    if word == "pause":
        session.pause()
        return "paused (type resume to continue)"
    if word == "resume":
        session.resume()
        return "resumed"
    if word == "stop":
        session.stop()
        return "stopping..."
    return "unknown command. " + COMMANDS


def interactive_search(statement: str, hints: list[str], rounds: int, on_event=print_event):
    """Run the search in a background thread while this thread reads your commands.
    Returns (result, every hint used, including the ones you typed)."""
    session = Session(statement)
    for h in hints:
        session.add_hint(h)
    box = {}
    worker = threading.Thread(
        target=lambda: box.setdefault("result", search(statement, rounds=rounds, on_event=on_event, session=session)),
        daemon=True,  # don't keep Python alive just for this thread
    )
    worker.start()
    print(COMMANDS)
    keyboard_open = True
    while worker.is_alive():
        if not keyboard_open:
            worker.join(0.5)
            continue
        ready, _, _ = select.select([sys.stdin], [], [], 0.5)  # wait up to 0.5 s for a typed line
        if ready:
            line = sys.stdin.readline()
            if line == "":  # end of input (Ctrl-D, or a pipe that closed)
                keyboard_open = False
            elif line.strip():
                print(f"  [{handle_command(session, line)}]")
    return box["result"], session.hints()


def new_run_dir() -> Path:
    """A fresh folder for this run: runs/<date-time>/ (with -2, -3, ... if that name is taken)."""
    base = RUNS_DIR / datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d-%H%M%S")
    folder, n = base, 1
    while folder.exists():
        n += 1
        folder = base.with_name(f"{base.name}-{n}")
    folder.mkdir(parents=True)
    return folder


def save_run(folder: Path, theorem: str, lean_code: str, explanation: str) -> Path:
    """Save a proved theorem into its run folder: theorem.txt, proof.lean, explanation.md."""
    (folder / "theorem.txt").write_text(theorem + "\n")
    (folder / "proof.lean").write_text(HEADER + lean_code)  # a complete file Lean can check on its own
    (folder / "explanation.md").write_text(explanation + "\n")
    return folder


def main(argv=None) -> int:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(prog="mathfinder")
    commands = parser.add_subparsers(dest="command", required=True)
    prove = commands.add_parser("prove", help="search for a proof")
    prove.add_argument("latex", nargs="?", help="the theorem in LaTeX (in quotes)")
    prove.add_argument("--lean", help='skip formalization: a Lean statement, e.g. "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"')
    prove.add_argument("--yes", action="store_true", help="don't ask for approval if the judge is happy")
    prove.add_argument("--hint", action="append", default=[], help="a hint for the planner (repeatable)")
    prove.add_argument("--rounds", type=int, default=3)
    prove.add_argument("--no-explain", action="store_true", help="skip the plain-math explanation")
    prove.add_argument("--metric", choices=sorted(METRICS), default="short", help="what 'better proof' means")
    prove.add_argument("--no-optimize", action="store_true", help="keep the first proof found")
    prove.add_argument("--interactive", action="store_true", help="type hints and commands while it searches")
    export_cmd = commands.add_parser("export", help="build training data from all runs/*/trace.jsonl")
    export_cmd.add_argument("--out", default=str(DATA_DIR), help="output folder (default: data/)")
    bench_cmd = commands.add_parser("bench", help="run the benchmark in bench/problems.jsonl")
    bench_cmd.add_argument("--rounds", type=int, default=2, help="planning rounds per problem")
    bench_cmd.add_argument("--only", nargs="*", default=[], help="names of problems to run (default: all)")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    if args.command == "bench":
        problems = load_problems()
        if args.only:
            problems = [p for p in problems if p["name"] in args.only]
        rows, md_path = run_bench(problems, args.rounds)
        print("\n" + results_table(rows))
        print(f"\nSaved {md_path}")
        return 0

    if args.command == "export":
        counts = export(RUNS_DIR, Path(args.out))
        for name, count in counts.items():
            print(f"{name:12} {count:5} rows  -> {Path(args.out) / (name + '.jsonl')}")
        return 0

    if args.command == "prove":
        if args.lean:
            statement = args.lean
        elif args.latex:
            statement = approve(args.latex, args.yes)
            if statement is None:
                print("Not searching.")
                return 1
        else:
            parser.error("give a theorem in LaTeX, or --lean STATEMENT")
        folder = new_run_dir()
        logger = TraceLogger(folder, args.latex or statement)
        on_event = chain(print_event, logger)  # print AND log every event
        if args.interactive:
            result, hints_used = interactive_search(statement, args.hint, args.rounds, on_event)
        else:
            result = search(statement, hints=args.hint, rounds=args.rounds, on_event=on_event)
            hints_used = args.hint
        logger.finish(result.proved, hints_used)
        print(f"\nResult: {'PROVED' if result.proved else 'not proved'} ({result.message})")
        print(f"Trace: {logger.path}")
        if not result.proved:
            return 1
        lean_code, plan = result.lean_code, result.plan
        if not args.no_optimize:
            print(f"\nLooking for a better proof (metric: {args.metric})...")
            better = optimize(statement, lean_code, plan, args.metric, on_event)
            print(f"  cost {better.original['cost']} → {better.best['cost']}"
                  + ("  (better proof found)" if better.improved else "  (kept the first proof)"))
            if better.improved:
                lean_code, plan = better.lean_code, better.plan
                print("\n" + lean_code)
        explanation = ""
        if not args.no_explain:
            print("\nWriting the explanation...")
            try:
                explanation = explain(args.latex or statement, plan, lean_code)
                print("\n" + explanation)
            except LLMError as e:  # the proof is still valid; only the write-up failed
                print(f"(could not write an explanation: {e})")
        print(f"\nSaved to {save_run(folder, args.latex or statement, lean_code, explanation)}")
        return 0
    return 2

    What changed: the import of load_problems, results_table, run_bench, and a new sub-command bench with --rounds (default 2, so a hard problem can't run forever) and --only (nargs="*" takes any number of names).

  4. Create the tests.

    tests/test_bench.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/bench.py and the `bench` command."""

import json

import pytest

from mathfinder import bench, cli
from mathfinder.lean import Decl, check_decls
from mathfinder.planner import Lemma, Plan
from mathfinder.search import SearchResult


def test_the_benchmark_has_20_problems_with_unique_names():
    problems = bench.load_problems()
    assert len(problems) == 20
    assert len({p["name"] for p in problems}) == 20
    assert all(p["topic"] and p["statement"] for p in problems)


def test_results_table_summarizes_by_topic():
    rows = [
        {"name": "a", "topic": "algebra", "proved": True, "seconds": 30, "how": "Automation: linarith"},
        {"name": "b", "topic": "algebra", "proved": False, "seconds": 90, "how": "no verified proof"},
        {"name": "c", "topic": "induction", "proved": True, "seconds": 60, "how": "plan with 1 lemma(s), round 1"},
    ]
    table = bench.results_table(rows)
    assert table.startswith("**2/3 proved** (67%), total time 3 min")
    assert "| 2 | `b` | algebra | ✗ not proved | 90 s | no verified proof |" in table
    assert table.endswith("By topic: algebra 1/2, induction 1/1")


def test_run_bench_records_and_saves_every_problem(monkeypatch, tmp_path):
    monkeypatch.setattr(bench, "RESULTS_DIR", tmp_path / "results")
    monkeypatch.setattr(bench, "RUNS_DIR", tmp_path / "runs")

    def fake_search(statement, rounds, on_event):
        on_event({"kind": "attempt", "ok": True})
        if "fail" in statement:
            return SearchResult(False, message="no verified proof after 2 rounds")
        plan = Plan("p", [Lemma("k", ": True", "", status="proved", proof="trivial")], "exact k")
        return SearchResult(True, "theorem main : True := by\n  exact k\n", plan, 1, "verified")

    monkeypatch.setattr(bench, "search", fake_search)
    problems = [{"name": "good", "topic": "t", "statement": ": True"},
                {"name": "bad", "topic": "t", "statement": ": fail"}]
    rows, md_path = bench.run_bench(problems, rounds=2, on_progress=lambda line: None)

    assert [(r["name"], r["proved"], r["attempts"]) for r in rows] == [("good", True, 1), ("bad", False, 1)]
    assert rows[0]["how"] == "plan with 1 lemma(s), round 1" and rows[0]["steps"] == 1
    assert json.loads(md_path.with_suffix(".json").read_text())[1]["name"] == "bad"
    assert len(list((tmp_path / "runs").glob("bench-*/*/trace.jsonl"))) == 2  # each run was traced


def test_bench_command_can_run_only_some_problems(monkeypatch, tmp_path, capsys):
    seen = {}

    def fake_run_bench(problems, rounds):
        seen["names"] = [p["name"] for p in problems]
        return [], tmp_path / "x.md"

    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "run_bench", fake_run_bench)
    monkeypatch.setattr(cli, "results_table", lambda rows: "TABLE")
    assert cli.main(["bench", "--only", "gcd_180_168", "two_pow_ge"]) == 0
    assert seen["names"] == ["gcd_180_168", "two_pow_ge"]  # file order, filtered
    assert "TABLE" in capsys.readouterr().out


@pytest.mark.lean
def test_every_benchmark_statement_typechecks():
    problems = bench.load_problems()
    results = check_decls([Decl(p["name"], p["statement"], "sorry") for p in problems])
    assert {p["name"]: results[p["name"]].errors for p in problems} == {p["name"]: [] for p in problems}

    The Lean test checks that all 20 statements type-check, in a single Lean run with sorry proofs. A typo in a benchmark statement would silently make a problem unprovable, so this matters.

  5. Let export find the benchmark's traces. Replace the whole file:

    mathfinder/traces.py
    """Traces: remember everything the search did, then turn it into training data.

While searching, a TraceLogger writes every event as one line of JSON to
runs/<id>/trace.jsonl ("JSON Lines": one JSON object per line, easy to append to
and easy to read back). Failed attempts are logged too: knowing what DOESN'T work
is just as useful for training.

`export` reads every trace and builds four datasets in data/:
  sft.jsonl          goal (+ lemmas it may use) → a proof Lean accepted      ("imitate this")
  preferences.jsonl  same goal: a proof that worked vs. one that failed      ("prefer this one")
  value.jsonl        lemma statement → proved / refuted / stuck              ("how hard is this?")
  hints.jsonl        the hints a person gave → whether the theorem got proved
"""

import json
import threading
import time
from pathlib import Path

DATASETS = ("sft", "preferences", "value", "hints")


class TraceLogger:
    """An on_event callback that appends every event to <run_dir>/trace.jsonl."""

    def __init__(self, run_dir: Path, theorem: str):
        run_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        self.path = run_dir / "trace.jsonl"
        self._lock = threading.Lock()  # events can arrive from the search thread and the main thread
        self({"kind": "meta", "theorem": theorem})

    def __call__(self, event: dict) -> None:
        record = {"time": round(time.time(), 3), **event}
        with self._lock, self.path.open("a") as f:  # "a" = append to the end of the file
            f.write(json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")

    def finish(self, proved: bool, hints: list[str]) -> None:
        self({"kind": "result", "proved": proved, "hints": list(hints)})


def chain(*callbacks):
    """One on_event callback that passes each event to several watchers (e.g. printer + logger)."""

    def on_event(event: dict) -> None:
        for callback in callbacks:
            callback(event)

    return on_event


def read_trace(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    return [json.loads(line) for line in path.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]


def records_from_trace(events: list[dict]) -> dict[str, list[dict]]:
    """Turn one run's events into rows for each dataset."""
    out = {name: [] for name in DATASETS}
    theorem = next((e["theorem"] for e in events if e["kind"] == "meta"), "")
    attempts = [e for e in events if e["kind"] == "attempt"]

    for a in attempts:
        if a["ok"]:
            out["sft"].append({"type": "lemma", "statement": a["statement"], "context": a["context"],
                               "proof": a["proof"], "source": a["source"]})
    for e in events:
        if e["kind"] == "done" and e.get("depth", 0) == 0:
            out["sft"].append({"type": "theorem", "statement": theorem, "context": [], "proof": e["lean_code"],
                               "source": "search"})

    by_goal = {}  # (statement, context) -> attempts on that exact goal
    for a in attempts:
        by_goal.setdefault((a["statement"], tuple(a["context"])), []).append(a)
    for (statement, context), group in by_goal.items():
        good = [a for a in group if a["ok"]]
        bad = [a for a in group if not a["ok"]]
        for g in good:
            for b in bad:
                out["preferences"].append({"statement": statement, "context": list(context), "chosen": g["proof"],
                                           "rejected": b["proof"], "rejected_error": b["error"]})

    for e in events:
        if e["kind"] in ("lemma_proved", "lemma_refuted", "lemma_stuck"):
            out["value"].append({"statement": e["statement"], "label": e["kind"].removeprefix("lemma_")})

    for e in events:
        if e["kind"] == "result" and e["hints"]:
            out["hints"].append({"theorem": theorem, "hints": e["hints"], "proved": e["proved"]})
    return out


def export(runs_dir: Path, out_dir: Path) -> dict[str, int]:
    """Build all datasets from every trace.jsonl under runs_dir, at any depth
    (benchmark traces live one level deeper, in runs/bench-<time>/<problem>/). Returns row counts."""
    rows = {name: [] for name in DATASETS}
    for trace in sorted(runs_dir.rglob("trace.jsonl")):  # rglob = search all sub-folders too
        for name, records in records_from_trace(read_trace(trace)).items():
            rows[name].extend(records)

    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    counts = {}
    for name, records in rows.items():
        unique = list({json.dumps(r, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False): r for r in records}.values())
        with (out_dir / f"{name}.jsonl").open("w") as f:
            for r in unique:
                f.write(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
        counts[name] = len(unique)
    return counts

    What changed: export used runs_dir.glob("*/trace.jsonl"), which only looks one folder deep. Benchmark traces live two deep (runs/bench-<time>/<problem>/trace.jsonl), so after our first benchmark run export silently ignored all of them. rglob ("recursive glob") searches every sub-folder.

  6. Add a test for that. Replace the whole file:

    tests/test_traces.py
    """Tests for mathfinder/traces.py."""

import json

from mathfinder.traces import TraceLogger, chain, export, read_trace, records_from_trace

GOAL = "(n : ℕ) : n + 0 = n"

EVENTS = [
    {"kind": "meta", "theorem": "THM"},
    {"kind": "attempt", "statement": GOAL, "context": [], "proof": "ring", "ok": False, "error": "ring failed", "source": "llm"},
    {"kind": "attempt", "statement": GOAL, "context": [], "proof": "simp", "ok": True, "error": "", "source": "repair"},
    {"kind": "lemma_proved", "depth": 0, "name": "a", "statement": GOAL, "feedback": "LLM"},
    {"kind": "lemma_stuck", "depth": 0, "name": "b", "statement": ": False", "feedback": "no"},
    {"kind": "done", "depth": 0, "lean_code": "theorem main ...", "plan": "p"},
    {"kind": "result", "proved": True, "hints": ["try simp"]},
]


def test_logger_writes_one_json_line_per_event(tmp_path):
    log = TraceLogger(tmp_path / "run", "THM")
    log({"kind": "round", "number": 1})
    log.finish(True, ["a hint"])
    events = read_trace(tmp_path / "run" / "trace.jsonl")
    assert [e["kind"] for e in events] == ["meta", "round", "result"]
    assert events[0]["theorem"] == "THM" and "time" in events[1]
    assert events[2] == {**events[2], "proved": True, "hints": ["a hint"]}


def test_chain_calls_every_watcher():
    seen_a, seen_b = [], []
    chain(seen_a.append, seen_b.append)({"kind": "x"})
    assert seen_a == seen_b == [{"kind": "x"}]


def test_records_from_trace_builds_all_four_datasets():
    rows = records_from_trace(EVENTS)
    assert [r["proof"] for r in rows["sft"]] == ["simp", "theorem main ..."]
    assert rows["sft"][1]["statement"] == "THM"
    assert rows["preferences"] == [{"statement": GOAL, "context": [], "chosen": "simp",
                                    "rejected": "ring", "rejected_error": "ring failed"}]
    assert rows["value"] == [{"statement": GOAL, "label": "proved"}, {"statement": ": False", "label": "stuck"}]
    assert rows["hints"] == [{"theorem": "THM", "hints": ["try simp"], "proved": True}]


def test_export_reads_every_run_and_removes_duplicates(tmp_path):
    for name in ("run1", "run2"):  # the same trace twice: rows must not be doubled
        (tmp_path / "runs" / name).mkdir(parents=True)
        (tmp_path / "runs" / name / "trace.jsonl").write_text("\n".join(json.dumps(e) for e in EVENTS) + "\n")
    counts = export(tmp_path / "runs", tmp_path / "data")
    assert counts == {"sft": 2, "preferences": 1, "value": 2, "hints": 1}
    first = json.loads((tmp_path / "data" / "sft.jsonl").read_text().splitlines()[0])
    assert first["proof"] == "simp"


def test_export_also_finds_benchmark_traces_one_level_deeper(tmp_path):
    deep = tmp_path / "runs" / "bench-20261114" / "some_problem"
    deep.mkdir(parents=True)
    (deep / "trace.jsonl").write_text("\n".join(json.dumps(e) for e in EVENTS) + "\n")
    assert export(tmp_path / "runs", tmp_path / "data")["sft"] == 2

    What changed: one new test at the end, with a trace two folders deep.

Checkpoint

Our full run (64 minutes) ended with this table, also saved as bench/results/<time>.md:

| 18 | amgm_three_pairs        | inequality    | ✓ proved | 88 s  | plan with 0 lemma(s), round 1    |
| 19 | product_max             | inequality    | ✓ proved | 69 s  | plan with 0 lemma(s), round 1    |
| 20 | quartic_nonneg          | inequality    | ✓ proved | 76 s  | plan with 0 lemma(s), round 1    |

By topic: algebra 5/5, induction 3/3, inequality 3/3, number theory 9/9

Read it honestly. 8 of 20 fell to the hammer alone, and every other one was solved in the first planning round. That means this set is easy for the system: it never needed sub-searches, many repair rounds, or your help. It's a good regression test (a change that breaks it is clearly bad), but not a measure of hard-problem skill. The real next step is the actual miniF2F set, or your own harder problems added to problems.jsonl. Your times and "how" column will vary, since the model is creative.

Common errors

  • test_every_benchmark_statement_typechecks fails: a statement in problems.jsonl has a typo. The error names it.
  • json.decoder.JSONDecodeError: a line in problems.jsonl isn't valid JSON (watch the quotes and commas).
  • The benchmark is much slower than an hour: another Lean-heavy job is running at the same time (e.g. the full test suite). Run them one after the other.

M13: Training scaffolding

What and why. Since M8 every run has been recorded, and mathfinder export turns the records into datasets. This last chapter prepares the ground for training your own prover: a small open model that learns from the proofs Math Finder found (and from the mistakes it made). That is how systems like AlphaProof improve: search produces proofs, the proofs train the model, and the better model searches better. We build the two missing pieces and test them for real on a laptop. We don't run serious training: that needs a GPU, hours, and far more data than a few dozen runs produce.

Three kinds of training data

FileTeachesMethod
sft_chat.jsonl"here's a goal, write this proof"SFT, supervised fine-tuning: imitate good examples
dpo.jsonl"for this goal, this proof is better than that one"DPO, direct preference optimization: learn from pairs
value.jsonl"will this lemma get proved?"a classifier, a value model the search could use to try promising plans first

  1. Make training/ a package (so tests can import it).

    training/__init__.py
    """Training scaffolding: turn Math Finder's traces into datasets, and fine-tune a small prover on them."""

  2. Create the data preparation script.

    training/prepare.py
    """Turn the exported datasets (data/*.jsonl, from `mathfinder export`) into training files.

  uv run python training/prepare.py            reads data/, writes data/train/ and data/test/

Output, in the formats the Hugging Face TRL library expects:
  sft_chat.jsonl  {"messages": [system, user, assistant]}       teach the prover to write proofs
  dpo.jsonl       {"prompt": [...], "chosen": [...], "rejected": [...]}   prefer proofs that work
  value.jsonl     {"text": statement, "label": 1 or 0, "status": ...}      predict "will this get proved?"

The train/test split is by GOAL: every row about the same statement lands on the same side.
Otherwise a model could be tested on a goal it has already seen the answer to (a "leak").
"""

import hashlib
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
TEST_PERCENT = 10

SYSTEM = (
    "You are an expert in Lean 4 and Mathlib. Write ONLY the tactic proof that goes after `:= by`. "
    "Never use sorry, admit or axiom."
)


def split_of(statement: str) -> str:
    """'test' for about TEST_PERCENT% of statements, else 'train'. Always the same for the same statement."""
    digest = hashlib.sha256(" ".join(statement.split()).encode()).hexdigest()
    return "test" if int(digest, 16) % 100 < TEST_PERCENT else "train"


def user_prompt(statement: str, context: list[str]) -> str:
    text = f"Prove this lemma:\n  theorem goal {statement}"
    if context:
        text += "\n\nEarlier lemmas you may use:\n" + "\n".join(f"  {c}" for c in context)
    return text


def sft_rows(sft: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
    rows = []
    for r in sft:
        if r["type"] != "lemma":  # theorem rows hold whole files; the prover writes tactic proofs
            continue
        rows.append({
            "messages": [
                {"role": "system", "content": SYSTEM},
                {"role": "user", "content": user_prompt(r["statement"], r["context"])},
                {"role": "assistant", "content": r["proof"]},
            ],
            "statement": r["statement"],
        })
    return rows


def dpo_rows(preferences: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
    return [
        {
            "prompt": [{"role": "system", "content": SYSTEM},
                       {"role": "user", "content": user_prompt(p["statement"], p["context"])}],
            "chosen": [{"role": "assistant", "content": p["chosen"]}],
            "rejected": [{"role": "assistant", "content": p["rejected"]}],
            "statement": p["statement"],
        }
        for p in preferences
    ]


def value_rows(value: list[dict]) -> list[dict]:
    return [{"text": v["statement"], "label": int(v["label"] == "proved"), "status": v["label"],
             "statement": v["statement"]} for v in value]


def read_jsonl(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    if not path.exists():
        return []
    return [json.loads(line) for line in path.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]


def prepare(data_dir: Path) -> dict[str, dict[str, int]]:
    """Write data_dir/train/*.jsonl and data_dir/test/*.jsonl. Returns row counts."""
    datasets = {
        "sft_chat": sft_rows(read_jsonl(data_dir / "sft.jsonl")),
        "dpo": dpo_rows(read_jsonl(data_dir / "preferences.jsonl")),
        "value": value_rows(read_jsonl(data_dir / "value.jsonl")),
    }
    counts = {"train": {}, "test": {}}
    for split in ("train", "test"):
        (data_dir / split).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        for name, rows in datasets.items():
            chosen = [r for r in rows if split_of(r["statement"]) == split]
            with (data_dir / split / f"{name}.jsonl").open("w") as f:
                for r in chosen:
                    f.write(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
            counts[split][name] = len(chosen)
    return counts


if __name__ == "__main__":
    data_dir = Path(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else ROOT / "data"
    for split, numbers in prepare(data_dir).items():
        print(f"{split:5}  " + "  ".join(f"{name} {n}" for name, n in numbers.items()))

    Chat format. Modern models are trained on conversations: a list of {"role", "content"} messages (system, user, assistant). sft_rows builds one conversation per proved lemma, and the assistant's reply is the proof. The prompt looks like the one the prover uses in M4b, so a trained model would feel at home there. dpo_rows builds TRL's preference format: one prompt, a chosen reply and a rejected reply.

    The split by goal (split_of) is the important detail. If two proofs of the same lemma landed in train and test, the test would measure memory, not skill: that's a leak. So we hash the statement (SHA-256, a fingerprint that's always the same for the same text) and send about 10% of statements to test. int(digest, 16) % 100 turns the hexadecimal fingerprint into a number from 0 to 99.

  3. Create the fine-tuning script.

    training/finetune.py
    """Fine-tune a small open model on Math Finder's proofs (supervised fine-tuning with LoRA).

This is SCAFFOLDING: it shows exactly how training would work, and runs end to end on a
tiny model, but real training needs a GPU and hours. Nothing here runs unless you ask.

  # 1. check the data and settings, without any machine-learning libraries installed:
  uv run python training/finetune.py --dry-run

  # 2. a 2-step smoke test on a tiny model, on a laptop CPU (downloads ~300 MB once):
  uv run --with torch --with transformers --with datasets --with peft --with trl \
      python training/finetune.py --base-model HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-135M-Instruct --max-steps 2

  # 3. real training (on a GPU machine): pick a prover model from huggingface.co, e.g. a
  #    DeepSeek-Prover or Kimina-Prover model, check its card for the licence and chat template.
  ... python training/finetune.py --base-model <model id> --epochs 2

LoRA ("low-rank adaptation") trains a few small extra matrices instead of the whole model,
so it fits on one GPU, and the result is a small "adapter" you load on top of the base model.
"""

import argparse
import json
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent


def load_rows(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    return [json.loads(line) for line in path.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()]


def describe(rows: list[dict]) -> str:
    """A short report about the dataset, so you know what you're about to train on."""
    chars = sum(len(m["content"]) for r in rows for m in r["messages"])
    example = rows[0]["messages"] if rows else []
    lines = [f"{len(rows)} examples, about {chars // 4} tokens in total (1 token ≈ 4 characters)"]
    if example:
        lines.append("first example:")
        lines += [f"  [{m['role']}] {m['content'][:120]!r}" for m in example]
    return "\n".join(lines)


def train(args) -> None:
    # Imported here, not at the top: these libraries are big and only needed for real training.
    from datasets import load_dataset
    from peft import LoraConfig
    from trl import SFTConfig, SFTTrainer

    dataset = load_dataset("json", data_files=str(args.data), split="train")
    dataset = dataset.remove_columns([c for c in dataset.column_names if c != "messages"])
    config = SFTConfig(
        output_dir=str(args.out),
        num_train_epochs=args.epochs,
        max_steps=args.max_steps,  # -1 means "use the epochs"
        per_device_train_batch_size=1,
        gradient_accumulation_steps=8,  # behaves like a batch of 8, using the memory of 1
        learning_rate=2e-4,
        logging_steps=1,
        save_strategy="no" if args.max_steps > 0 else "epoch",
        report_to="none",
    )
    lora = LoraConfig(r=args.lora_rank, lora_alpha=2 * args.lora_rank, target_modules="all-linear", task_type="CAUSAL_LM")
    trainer = SFTTrainer(model=args.base_model, train_dataset=dataset, args=config, peft_config=lora)
    trainer.train()
    trainer.save_model(str(args.out))
    print(f"saved the LoRA adapter to {args.out}")


def main(argv=None) -> None:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    parser.add_argument("--data", type=Path, default=ROOT / "data" / "train" / "sft_chat.jsonl")
    parser.add_argument("--base-model", default="HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-135M-Instruct")
    parser.add_argument("--out", type=Path, default=ROOT / "models" / "prover-lora")
    parser.add_argument("--epochs", type=float, default=2)
    parser.add_argument("--max-steps", type=int, default=-1, help="stop after this many steps (for smoke tests)")
    parser.add_argument("--lora-rank", type=int, default=16)
    parser.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true", help="only check the data and print the plan")
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)

    rows = load_rows(args.data)
    print(describe(rows))
    print(f"base model: {args.base_model}\noutput:     {args.out}\nepochs: {args.epochs}, max steps: {args.max_steps}, "
          f"LoRA rank: {args.lora_rank}")
    if not rows:
        raise SystemExit("no training examples: run `mathfinder export` and `training/prepare.py` first")
    if args.dry_run:
        print("dry run: not training.")
        return
    train(args)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

    LoRA freezes the model and trains small extra matrices next to it. Instead of updating billions of numbers, you update a few million, which fits on one GPU, and the result is a small adapter file (19 MB for the tiny model). target_modules="all-linear" puts adapters next to every linear layer.

    SFTConfig holds the training settings. gradient_accumulation_steps=8 adds up 8 small steps before updating, which behaves like a batch of 8 while only using the memory of 1. max_steps overrides the epochs, which is handy for smoke tests. The heavy libraries (torch, transformers, datasets, peft, trl) are imported inside train(), so --dry-run and the tests work without installing them.

  5. Create the tests.

    tests/test_training.py
    """Tests for training/prepare.py and training/finetune.py (no machine-learning libraries needed)."""

import json

import pytest

from training import finetune, prepare


def test_split_is_stable_and_about_ten_percent():
    statements = [f"(n : ℕ) : n + {i} = {i} + n" for i in range(2000)]
    splits = [prepare.split_of(s) for s in statements]
    assert splits == [prepare.split_of(s) for s in statements]  # same answer every time
    assert 0.07 < splits.count("test") / len(splits) < 0.13
    assert prepare.split_of("(n : ℕ) :  n = n") == prepare.split_of("(n : ℕ) : n = n")  # spacing ignored


def test_rows_have_the_shapes_trl_expects():
    sft = prepare.sft_rows([
        {"type": "lemma", "statement": "S", "context": ["theorem a : True"], "proof": "simp", "source": "llm"},
        {"type": "theorem", "statement": "T", "context": [], "proof": "whole file", "source": "search"},
    ])
    assert len(sft) == 1  # theorem rows are skipped
    roles = [m["role"] for m in sft[0]["messages"]]
    assert roles == ["system", "user", "assistant"] and sft[0]["messages"][2]["content"] == "simp"
    assert "theorem a : True" in sft[0]["messages"][1]["content"]

    [dpo] = prepare.dpo_rows([{"statement": "S", "context": [], "chosen": "good", "rejected": "bad"}])
    assert dpo["chosen"][0]["content"] == "good" and dpo["rejected"][0]["content"] == "bad"

    labels = prepare.value_rows([{"statement": "S", "label": "proved"}, {"statement": "T", "label": "stuck"}])
    assert [v["label"] for v in labels] == [1, 0]


def test_prepare_keeps_each_goal_on_one_side(tmp_path):
    rows = [{"type": "lemma", "statement": f"(n : ℕ) : n * {i} = {i} * n", "context": [], "proof": p, "source": "llm"}
            for i in range(60) for p in ("ring", "simp")]  # two proofs of every goal
    (tmp_path / "sft.jsonl").write_text("\n".join(json.dumps(r) for r in rows))
    counts = prepare.prepare(tmp_path)
    assert counts["train"]["sft_chat"] + counts["test"]["sft_chat"] == 120
    train = {json.loads(l)["statement"] for l in (tmp_path / "train" / "sft_chat.jsonl").read_text().splitlines()}
    test = {json.loads(l)["statement"] for l in (tmp_path / "test" / "sft_chat.jsonl").read_text().splitlines()}
    assert train and test and not (train & test)  # no goal in both


def test_finetune_dry_run_reports_without_training(tmp_path, capsys):
    data = tmp_path / "sft_chat.jsonl"
    data.write_text(json.dumps({"messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "x" * 40},
                                             {"role": "assistant", "content": "simp"}]}) + "\n")
    finetune.main(["--data", str(data), "--dry-run"])
    out = capsys.readouterr().out
    assert "1 examples, about 11 tokens" in out and "dry run: not training." in out


def test_finetune_refuses_an_empty_dataset(tmp_path):
    empty = tmp_path / "empty.jsonl"
    empty.write_text("")
    with pytest.raises(SystemExit):
        finetune.main(["--data", str(empty), "--dry-run"])

    test_prepare_keeps_each_goal_on_one_side writes two proofs for each of 60 goals and checks no goal appears in both train and test, which is exactly the leak the split exists to prevent.

  6. Build the datasets from all your runs, and check them:

    uv run python -m mathfinder export
uv run python training/prepare.py
uv run python training/finetune.py --dry-run

  7. Optional: a real 2-step training run on a tiny model, on your CPU (about a minute; downloads ~300 MB of libraries and model the first time, into a temporary environment, so your project stays light):

    uv run --with torch --with transformers --with datasets --with peft --with trl \
    python training/finetune.py --base-model HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-135M-Instruct --max-steps 2 --out models/smoke-test

    uv run --with X runs the command with extra packages available just for that run.

With so few examples, test can easily be empty. The split is by goal, and with only a handful of goals none may fall in the 10%. Run the benchmark (M12) and a few more proofs to grow the data.

uv run pytest -q tests/test_training.py
.....                                                                    [100%]
5 passed in 0.03s

Common errors

  • no training examples: run mathfinder export and training/prepare.py first. Only lemma-level proofs become SFT examples, so runs proved by direct automation contribute none.
  • Cannot use chat template functions ... no template: the base model has no chat template. Use an "-Instruct" or "-Chat" model.
  • Out of memory on a real model: lower --lora-rank, use a smaller model, or train on a GPU machine.

What's next

All fourteen milestones are built and verified. PROGRESS.md has the full log, including every verification command and its output. Ideas for going further, each a project of its own:

  • Harder benchmark: add real miniF2F problems to bench/problems.jsonl. The current set is solved 20/20, so it can no longer show improvement.
  • Faster Lean: keep Mathlib loaded in a persistent Lean process (the Lean REPL) instead of starting Lean for every batch. Most of the time spent is loading Mathlib.
  • Use your trained model: serve the LoRA adapter from M13 and add it as a third backend in llm.BACKENDS.
  • A value model in the search: train a classifier on value.jsonl and use it to order plans, instead of "fewest lemmas first".
